- **accept** — **the state plane is prefetched with the KV plane, so a hit reads nothing on the calling thread; the warm ratio is unchanged at 1.99x against 2.04x and the win is the cold/evicted case this bench cannot reach.** An entry is two files and only the `.kv` was prefetched: `load_state` read the `.st` on the caller at lookup time, **157 MiB against the .kv's 178**, 151 ms of a 1.260 s faulted arm, in neither `fetch_ms` nor `fetch_bytes`. `_fetch_loop` now reads both planes and parks them together, `take` returns the pair, `_fault_in` hands the snapshot to `load_state`; `/health` keeps `ssd_state_load_ms` and `ssd_state_loads` published at 0, additive-only. **B covers everything a hit reads** — 321.6 MiB at **~1.24 GB/s** warm, where before it priced the `.kv` alone, which made `n* = (S/B) / (1/R − k/B)` divide the snapshot S by a rate measured **without S in it**; `break_even_tokens` stays 8–9 because S and B grew together. **The measurement refutes the obvious claim and is reported as such**: four runs on card 1 give 2.063 / 1.992 / 2.003 / 1.913 against five before at 1.939–2.061, and the faulted arm is 1.262 s against 1.260 — a restart empties HBM but not the host page cache, so the read that moved was a memcpy, and the two timers sum to the one that replaced them (122 + 151 = 273 before, **259 mean after**). The claim the change is for is the reboot/evicted arm, where the bench's own measured 182.6 MiB/s makes that 157 MiB a **0.86 s device read**. **The gate counts `torch.load` by thread, not wall clock** — with the ratio flat a timing assertion would have called this a no-op; three mutants each kill it on its own assertion, and it is red on the parent commit. 467 passed, 14 skipped — [wins/2026-09-07-half-the-bytes-were-read-on-the-tick.md](docs/experience/wins/2026-09-07-half-the-bytes-were-read-on-the-tick.md)

- **accept** — **the fp8 frozen dX gets a TileLang kernel: `backward_secs` 23.194 -> 22.264 s, 1.042x, from a measured 1.411x on the op.** The fp4 dX kernel is not a template past one design point: fp4's scale is one f32 per 16 elements of a weight *row*, so the dequant macro applies it to the **operand** in shared memory; fp8's is one f32 per 128x128 *tile*, constant across both axes, so it applies to the **accumulator** after the gemm and the N reduction must step by the scale block (128), not `_RED_TILE` (32), or one gemm spans two scales. `linear_fp8_bwd` is sm90-only and `reference.linear_frozen_bwd` stays the parity oracle — **there cannot be a CPU twin**: tilelang's C backend has no sub-f32 type, measured with three controls (`float8_e4m3fn` and `bfloat16` both give `Cannot convert type X to C type`, the same kernel at f32 compiles). **Three ratios were on the table and only one is a measurement**: 2.68x predicted from a bf16 GEMM floor mistaken for the fp4 kernel's rate (the fp4 dX measures **67.0-69.6 TFLOP/s**, not the 133-139 the prediction used, so it asked for twice what any frozen-dX kernel on this card has reached); 1.29x inferred by dividing the 0.930 s step delta by 1864 calls, which assumes its own conclusion — restating it as "the step gained what the op gained" recovers the input, not evidence; and **1.411x measured** by `bench_frozen_bwd` running kernel and eager in one process, 12 reps, 8 shapes, per-shape 1.218x (N=1024) to 1.492x (the lm_head). The step kept **74.9%** of the op's 1.241 s and the missing 0.311 s is stated as unattributed rather than explained. Two cross-checks that the arms are the same work: the shape recorder counts 3728 calls over two steps, exactly 2x the instrumented 1864, and the bare kernel's 3.018 s/step is 4.6% under the instrumented row's 3.162 (the per-handler timer). Gates: parity against the oracle at M in 64/128/192 with and without `oscale`, n=k=256 so both axes span more than one scale block — 6 passed on card 0; mutant `WScale[n, bx * block_N // block_N]`, the scale misalignment that still returns plausible finite numbers, gives 1 failed / 2 passed; gradcheck on the oracle itself, since a wrong oracle would certify a wrong kernel with both green. **`bench_frozen_bwd` was unrun code in the first draft of this change** — it sits behind `--frozen-shapes`, which neither profiling run passed, so it was exercised before the PR opened rather than after. Six readers the change falsified, all corrected here: `reference.py`'s two stale ponytail markers, `prof_backward_ops.py`'s `KIND` label printing "eager" for a row now running a kernel plus the docstring whose reason for skipping fp8 no longer existed, `support-matrix.md`'s five wrong `make_*` counts and its "no cell needs a packed-weight backward kernel", `design-kernels.md`'s Files table (the registry moved to `registry.py`; three kernel files were absent), and the `AGENTS.md` contract line. tests/ 467 passed, 15 skipped, 1 xfailed — [wins/2026-09-07-fp8-frozen-backward-kernel.md](docs/experience/wins/2026-09-07-fp8-frozen-backward-kernel.md)
- **reject** — **the sm70 prefill cell is REJECTED and #232 closes unmerged: TTFT at 16,384 tokens is at least 1180 s, >= 5.97x the baseline's 197.59 s.** The bound is arithmetic over measured values, not an extrapolation: two `py-spy dump --locals` reads **118 s apart** both put the profiler's own loop at `ticks=22, prefix=11264`, so that one 512-token chunk cost **>= 118 s** against the same arm's first chunk at 8.607 s, and a 16,384-token arm contains **10** chunks at prefix >= 11264, each at least as expensive because cost rises monotonically with prefix — #224's accept measured that rise as 75.98x first-to-last on this same arm. Compilation is excluded three ways: **0** `begins to compile` lines, **0** cache files under `find -newermt`, no child compiler process, and a warm 766 MB / 1578-cubin cache already holding the cell's own 21 MB cubin — so the 33+ minutes the run spent inside one *untimed* warm-up arm, which the same profiler finished in 224.7-329.1 s on the baseline, are execution. **The tile was priced on bytes and never on latency**, and that is what reverses #224: `T.Pipelined(..., num_stages=1)` is serial lowering (`pipeline_planning.cc:1270` gates the copy hoist on `num_stages >= 2`), and raising it cannot help on Volta, which has no `cp.async` (`TargetCudaHasAsyncCopy` is `arch >= 80`) and no room to double-buffer a tile chosen to sit exactly at Volta's 96 KiB shared-memory limit. At `block_N=16` the loop runs **1358** sequential K/V iterations at 21,727 tokens inside each of **192** blocks, where the replaced `paged_attention_split` launches **196,608** blocks over disjoint slices of the history and hides the same loads by occupancy; 64x fewer K/V bytes do not pay for that. **#232's own routing proof was also not a proof** and is withdrawn: its 191/191 py-spy samples on `backend.py:969` came from the serve process, while the profiler process the timed arms actually measure is **40/40** in `torch.cuda.synchronize` and **0/40** on any `backend.py` line; the guard `"paged_attention_prefill" in _resolve(...)` tests a name `_CPU_KERNELS` registers too, and "the split `elif` took none" is entailed by the branch order rather than measured — the two are mutually exclusive at `s=512`, and on main the same call does dispatch split. `scripts/prove_kernel_routing.py` replaces it by counting dispatches per *resolved maker*, with four controls run, one of them exactly the case a name test cannot distinguish. [errors/2026-09-07-the-sm70-prefill-cell-is-6x-slower-than-the-split-path.md](docs/experience/errors/2026-09-07-the-sm70-prefill-cell-is-6x-slower-than-the-split-path.md)
- **accept** — **a conversation's decode publishes now REPLACE rather than accumulate, bounding one conversation to `prefill_chunks + 1` live prefix entries instead of one per boundary.** The row was blocked on a sharing *rate*; the binding operand is an *interval* — `kv_cache.py:1120` refreshes a matched entry to MRU, so a shared head survives a gap of `budget − 1` publishes, exactly at six budgets swept (11 → **10** on the V100). Against that, one conversation emitted more publishes than the window. **And it emitted half what the entry claimed**: `engine.py:1313` guards on `i == last`, so only a chain's last accepted token is tested and a chain whose length does not divide `BLOCK_TOKENS` steps over boundaries silently. Live V100 child (pid 2977356, `--depth 1`), no card taken: 5410 tokens / 3047 forwards = 1.776 tok/fwd, accept 0.776, **338 boundaries exist against 188 published — 44% never happen**. The chain is Bernoulli at depth 1 and `1 + 0.776 = 1.776` reproduces the measured tok/fwd, so simulating it gives 163–214 landings (mean 190.6) against 188, **1.4% off**, with 338 outside that range as the negative control. **Two earlier models matched 188 to ~1% and were both wrong** — `gen/16/tok_per_fwd` = 190.4 (coincidence: no *fixed* chain length gives 1.776) and uniform 1..W bracketing at W=3 (impossible: the argv says `--depth 1`). The fix is neither end-of-turn nor a stride, both refused on the state invariant: `insert` rejects a partial block (`kv_cache.py:1045`), the lookup ladder walks 16-multiples, a sequence end is aligned 1 time in 16, and `_finish` → `_release` frees the blocks first — so a publish there would write an unfindable length or slice an entry below its snapshot. Instead a row retires its own previous decode entry when the next lands: turn 2 still matches the longest boundary crossed (**no loss**), and a mid-generation sharer still finds the latest entry, which every stride policy would have cost while *also* publishing more. Two details read rather than guessed: retire **after** the insert, since the entries share blocks and `retire` frees by refcount; and a separate `superseded` counter (`prefix_superseded` on `/health`) because routing a replace through `_drop`'s `evictions` would inflate the very counter that shows pressure. Gate counts entries **per tick**, since the old behaviour converges to the same final contents once LRU churns; mutant both ways — removing `retire` fails the non-vacuous `superseded > 0`, and with that relaxed the same mutant climbs 1→2→3→4→5→**6 against a bound of 5**. The `#252` store-level xfail stays xfail **by construction**: it calls `store.insert` directly and cannot see an engine-site fix. 110 passed, 1 skipped, 1 xfailed. Card numbers pending-remote — [errors/2026-09-07-a-prompts-own-publishes-evict-its-shared-prefix.md](docs/experience/errors/2026-09-07-a-prompts-own-publishes-evict-its-shared-prefix.md)

- **accept** — **one draft forward streams 1.065 GB, so the 32-42% speculative-ceiling bracket collapses to 38.2% and 34.3%, and the wide end was wrong because it charged a served tensor at its stored size.** The bracket's missing operand, closed on the real checkpoint: `scripts/draft_streamed_bytes.py` buckets the head the way `check_scale_f16.py` buckets the trunk — per tensor, at the dtype the engine actually serves, keeping only what a forward multiplies by. Shard `model-00018-of-00018.safetensors`, 18 tensors, 1.645 GB stored, 15 kept. **Head as stored (bf16) 0.849 GB → as SERVED (fp4 on sm70) 0.265**, because `_quantize_draft` re-packs every [N,K] ≥128×128 to 4-bit plus one f32 per 32; plus the trunk's **0.800** `lm_head`, since `read_head_params` skips the head's own readout and warns "the trunk's are shared" (the probe printed the three skipped tensors: `lm_head.wq/scale/oscale`, so the readout is charged once, not twice). **0.849 → 0.265 is the whole correction** — the same class of error as counting `embed_tokens` in the trunk roofline, one level down, and 1.065 against the 1.65 charged is 1.55x. Depth 3 = 16.04 + 3 × 1.065 = **19.24 GB**, **46.79 forward-sets/s** at 900 GB/s, so by the entry's own identity **52.7 tok/s at 2.95 tok/fwd is 38.2% of 138.0**, and **46.5 at 2.90 is 34.3% of 135.7** — both inside the bracket, with the probe reproducing both of its ends from the same code path (0 bytes → 165.5 → 31.8%; 1.65 GB → 126.5 → 41.7%), which is what makes 38.2% a reading of the system and not of a new script. The fp4 byte model is asserted against `reference.pack_fp4`'s own output before any total prints: 81,920 modelled vs 82,944 real on a 256×512, 1.2% apart (the oscale plane). Perfect acceptance would give 187.1 tok/s; printed separately because that is not the quantity 32-42% was computed against. Excludes the KV plane, which grows with context and is not a weight roofline term. All four carrying sites updated — `LOG-v100-sm70.md:211`, `wins/2026-09-01-sm70-gemv-packed-x-f16.md:83`, `wins/2026-09-01-sm70-attention-thread-redundancy.md:93`, and the entry's own results table. **A served size is not a stored size, and a bracket's wide end is where that difference hides** — the wide end reads as the conservative choice, which is exactly why nobody re-derived it. - **default flip** — **the chat page renders GFM: tables, nested lists, blockquotes, task lists, strikethrough, images.** ckl on the V100 page: "md 组件不全, 图表也没有". The hand-rolled grammar in `web/src/render.ts` covered headings, flat lists, fences, links and bold, so a table arrived as prose full of pipes, a nested list flattened to one level, and a blockquote kept its `>` as text — reproduced as the negative control before any code changed: `<p>| op | ms |...</p><ul><li>outer</li><li>inner</li></ul><p>&gt; quoted</p>`. Now `marked`'s **lexer** does the grammar and we walk its token tree with `createElement`/`createTextNode`. **Not `marked.parse()` + DOMPurify + `innerHTML`**, the usual shape: that costs **23.5 KB gz against 12.8 for the lexer alone**, and it would put an `innerHTML` sink back in the page where the old string renderer's attribute breakout lived (`test_the_bundle_has_no_html_string_sink` asserts zero sinks in the artifact *and* the sources; DOMPurify contains 10 `innerHTML` uses by design, since sanitising is parsing a string into markup). Absent beats sanitised, and it is the cheaper of the two — measured, not assumed. Bundle **2.55 → 15.55 KB gz** (6.6 → 50.4 KB raw); the estimate in the old comment, "marked/markdown-it are 30-40 KB gzipped", was high. `safeHref`'s policy (http/https/mailto/relative) now covers `img src` as well as `href`, on the same reasoning: a `src` fetches. The streaming state machine (`lastBlockStart`/`paint`/`settle`) is untouched — it only ever needed string→nodes for the last block. **Mermaid is not in scope** (ckl ruled): a ` ```mermaid ` fence renders as an ordinary code block with its Copy button, verified through the bundle. KaTeX out — 78 KB gz standalone. Gate: `test_gfm_tables_nested_lists_and_blockquotes_render` streams one reply carrying all six constructs through the **real committed bundle** and asserts the structure (`<table>`, `<thead>`, `<th>`, `<tbody>`, `<td>`, `<blockquote>`, an inner `<ul>` inside an `<li>`, `<del>`) rather than the text — the text was always present, the markup around it was what was missing. 26 passed. UI, off the hot path: no bench entry

- **accept** — **the SSD tier's restart win is 2.041x, and the 1.784x it replaces was measured one commit before the change that broke it.** Two bugs, both on the write side of a tier whose read side had passed every gate. `_finish_prefills` published the prompt-only entry only at `prompt_len % BLOCK_TOKENS == 0`, so **15 of 16 prompt lengths put nothing servable on disk** — the one entry that landed was the decode publish, prompt plus generated reply, which `blocks_to_text` strips from replayed history by design. And a row was admitted on the tick after `submit`, while its own prefetch was still reading, so `lookup` declined and the row prefilled what the fetch was fetching: **321 MiB written, zero bytes read**, on every arm. `_pick` now cuts the last prefill chunk to a block boundary and `_finish_prefills` spills exactly that boundary — one function answers both, since they disagreed in the first draft and `len % 16 == 1` published an entry then withheld it. `_build_plan` holds a row whose prefetch is in flight, set aside rather than `break` so it does not stall the queue. Measured card 1, 2729-token prompt: the tier now holds 2720 (a clean prefix of turn 2's 2755 ids) beside the 2736 that diverges at 2727. **2.041x warm over five runs (1.939–2.061)**, cold 2.601 / faulted 1.260 / control 2.515, `saved_over_ceiling` 0.309; a sixth run at 1.434x has **no established cause** — the eviction probe looked like the explanation until a seventh at a higher probe landed in the cluster, so the hypothesis is dropped rather than reported. **The bench was wrong three ways and each made a bad number readable**: `_matched_tokens` inferred coverage from file-size ratios and returned 512 for a hit covering 2720, so the ceiling claimed 2.8x more saved than possible; the guard for exactly that sat below a branch matching every warm restart and never ran; and reading every entry made a mid-save `.kv` reachable, which aborted the probe over a healthy tier. Also found, not fixed: the `.st` (157 MiB against the `.kv`'s 178) is read by `load_state` **on the caller at lookup time** — 151 ms of the 1.260 s arm, in neither `fetch_ms` nor `fetch_bytes`, so B prices half the bytes a hit reads. Supersedes the 1.738–1.821x in [wins/2026-09-05-the-ssd-tiers-read-path-did-not-exist.md](docs/experience/wins/2026-09-05-the-ssd-tiers-read-path-did-not-exist.md), honestly measured at `eae658e` and invalidated by `9556b2f` two hours later in the same PR — [wins/2026-09-07-a-ragged-prompt-had-nothing-on-disk.md](docs/experience/wins/2026-09-07-a-ragged-prompt-had-nothing-on-disk.md) · [errors/2026-09-07-the-tier-wrote-and-never-read.md](docs/experience/errors/2026-09-07-the-tier-wrote-and-never-read.md) · [errors/2026-09-07-the-measurement-and-the-code-diverged-in-one-pr.md](docs/experience/errors/2026-09-07-the-measurement-and-the-code-diverged-in-one-pr.md)
- **fix** — **#249's prefix ceiling ignored the host tier, and reported a capacity BELOW the resident count.** `entries_capacity` read `state_bytes // snapshot_bytes`, but `_demote_one` moves a snapshot to the DRAM tier and **leaves the entry matchable** — same tokens, same blocks, still in the index — so the host's bytes are capacity too. On the config `tests/test_e2e.py` already uses (`state_bytes=0` with a tier) the store held **3 resident entries while the key reported 0**: a ceiling below the fill, which tells an operator the store cannot hold anything. Now `min(capacity, (state_bytes + dram_budget) // snapshot_bytes)` → 5 for that config, and the three arms agree (tier only 0+5→5, HBM only 2+0→2, both 2+3→**5**, which is the arm that proves they sum). **The V100's 11 is unaffected** — that server runs no host tier, so the missing term was 0 in every number checked before the merge, which is exactly why the bug shipped: an arithmetic identity holds on a config where a term is zero whether or not the term belongs. The catching config was already in the tree at `tests/test_e2e.py:869`, so the rule is not "test more configs" but: read a derived quantity against a fixture where one of its operands is non-default. New arm `test_entries_capacity_counts_the_host_tier` rather than a tighter assert on the old one, two mutants each red on its own assertion — the shipped `avail = self.state_bytes` gives `(3, 0) == (3, 5)`, and returning `capacity` unconditionally trips this arm *and* the older byte-budget one. 460 passed, 14 skipped — [wins/2026-09-07-the-published-prefix-ceiling-is-not-the-binding-one.md](docs/experience/wins/2026-09-07-the-published-prefix-ceiling-is-not-the-binding-one.md)

- **fix** — **`/health` published a prefix ceiling 372x too high, and the number that binds is now beside it.** `prefix_capacity` reads **4096** on the V100 while the store held **11** entries and evicted on nearly every publish. Both are correct; only one is a ceiling. `capacity` caps the entry *count*; the cap that binds is `state_bytes`, because a GDN snapshot is a **constant 156,893,184 B at every prefix length** — no token axis — so a byte budget divides into a fixed entry count however long the prefixes are: 1,845,067,776 / 156,893,184 = **11**, which is exactly what the live store was holding at 93.5% fill, so the arithmetic is confirmed against an observation and not only against itself. Published as `prefix_entries_capacity` = `min(capacity, state_bytes // snapshot_bytes)`. The same reading explains the eviction shape that prompted the look: **105 evictions against 45 blocks freed (0.43 blocks/eviction) with the block pool at 192/2048, 91% free** — nothing was evicted for want of blocks, every one was state bytes. **Separately settled on the live server: the HBM hit path works, and `prefix_hits 0` after 115 publishes was the absence of repeat traffic rather than the `ssd_hits 0` defect it resembles.** Two identical 86-token requests back to back took hits **0 → 1** with misses unmoved at 4. The probe that could have gone wrong: a **21-token request moves `published` by 0**, because a publish needs a decode step landing on `materialized % BLOCK_TOKENS == 0` (`engine.py:1312`) and at prompt 21 with 4 new tokens `materialized` runs 20→24, never reaching 32 — so a short-prompt probe measures nothing and reads exactly like a broken store. **BLOCK_TOKENS is 16; I wrote it down as 64 from memory in the first draft of the entry and read `kv_cache.py:22` only after.** Gates: `test_prefix_state_budget_evicts` grows the derived-cap assert plus a fixture check that the count cap exceeds the byte cap (or it cannot tell derived from restated), mutant `entries_capacity = self.capacity` → `assert 4096 == 2`; the existing routing gate already covers the new key, confirmed by dropping the `_build_stats` line and watching `test_kv.py:620` fail rather than by inspection. 459 passed, 14 skipped. No card taken and no restart, so the running child (which predates this change) does not carry the new key yet — the derivation is proved in tests and against that server's operands, and the key reaches the wire at the next restart — [wins/2026-09-07-the-published-prefix-ceiling-is-not-the-binding-one.md](docs/experience/wins/2026-09-07-the-published-prefix-ceiling-is-not-the-binding-one.md)

- **accept** — **the fp4 backward was running Ampere MMA on a Hopper card: `backward_secs` 31.339 → 23.194 s, 1.351x, from two values at one call site.** `linear_fp4_bwd` is 18.82% of the GRPO backward and ran at 24.3-24.8 TFLOP/s where a same-shape bf16 GEMM reaches 135-137. The arithmetic never closed: the dequant inside the pipelined loop is 20x redundant (1.78 G element-decodes against 0.09 G unique, exactly the M-tile count) but only 0.16-1.07 ms of a 9.39 ms call, HBM traffic 4.57 GiB = 1.2-1.5 ms, GEMM floor 1.66 ms — overlapping rather than adding, so **at least 5.9 ms had no mechanism**. The emitted CUDA had it: `linear_fp4_bwd_kernel` contains **zero `wgmma`** and runs `mma.sync.aligned.m16n8k16` — the *Ampere* warp-level MMA — doing Hopper data movement (TMA, mbarrier, warp specialization) and then Ampere math. Read per file across ~16k cached kernels: warp-specialized with a **64-thread consumer** → `mma.sync` 25/25, `launch_bounds` 256 → `wgmma` 85/91. Hopper's `wgmma` issues from a 128-thread warpgroup, so at `_THREADS=64` the consumer is 2 warps and **no tile and no `num_stages` could ever have emitted `wgmma`** — two prior probes swept knobs that could not reach it. Four cells on card 6 solo, M=1280, real checkpoint weights, priced on the true call counts (896 gate/up + 448 down): shipped 12.616 s → threads=128 alone **6.525 s (1.933x)** → tile alone 9.904 (1.274x) → **both 4.563 s (2.765x)**, compounding past the 2.463x of independence, and `vs_bf16` 5.47-5.62x → **1.96-2.05x**. Warm end-to-end 23.194 s against the microbench's predicted 1.346x — **0.4% apart**, so the kernel table and the step agree with no fitted term. The change stays local: `_THREADS` is module-global across 20+ unmeasured launches, so the tile and thread count are literals at `backend.py:1126-1134`, and the wide warpgroup is gated on the full tile (`thr = 128 if bM == 128 else _THREADS`) because below 128 rows the tile snaps to 16/32 where nothing was measured. **The probe printed the opposite of the truth and it is recorded, along with two wrong diagnoses of why**: the per-cell `wgmma` read failed, `if hi["wgmma"] and not lo["wgmma"]` fell to its else branch, and all four comparisons printed "still mma.sync, so this is a tile effect" — a failed read rendered as a confident wrong verdict, caught only because a 1.933x from a thread count cannot be a tile effect. The cause took three attempts: "the handler swallowed an exception" (true, not causal), "`get_kernel_source` is a property not a method" (**false**, and published in the entry, the commit and the PR before being tested), and finally the real one — the factory returns a `JITImpl` that **compiles per shape**, so the accessor needs the same arguments as the call. The `verified` flag is why the second wrong diagnosis cost nothing: it printed "MMA class unread, so the cause is not established here" rather than a verdict. The column now agrees with the cache, 4/4 both ways (threads=64 → consumer 64, `mma_sync`; threads=128 → consumer 128, `wgmma`). Also settled, free from the same cache: the **forward** path does not have this bug (prefill and decode fp4 both dispatch to `linear_fp4_fp8`, already 52/60 `wgmma` at threads=128), so prefill's 2558.6 tok/s does not move. sm90 parity 42 passed, 1 failed — `test_paged_attention_prefill_tiled_vs_naive` (`Q device_type mismatch, expected: 1, got: 2`), **pre-existing and unrelated**, established by a revert control in one process on the card rather than by inspection: main's values rc=1, this branch's values rc=1 on that test alone — [wins/2026-09-07-fp4-backward-warpgroup.md](docs/experience/wins/2026-09-07-fp4-backward-warpgroup.md)

- **accept** — **the backward's chunk goes 64 → 128: a further 1.194x, `backward_secs` 41.446 → 34.717 s — and the ladder stops there, with 256 measured and refused.** Four arms on card 6, sequential with the card verified empty between each, the #192 recipe, T=1280, warm step, only `--gdn-chunk` differing: 16 → 80.207, 64 → 41.446, **128 → 34.717**, 256 → 32.667. **Two independent reasons stop it at 128, either sufficient.** (1) *The quadratic term turns at 256.* Through 128 the per-call cost is flat or falling (adjoint 0.931 → 0.955 → 0.889 ms); at 256 every row turns up — adjoint 1.122 (1.26x), recompute 0.674 (1.25x), solve 0.293 (2.4x) — and the solve's **total reverses**, 1.984 → 0.640 → 0.464 s across the first three arms then **up** to 0.562 at 256, which is 1.21x more time for half the calls. (2) *A fixed cost caps what remains.* The unattributed column is 26.952 / 26.922 / 26.918 / 27.061, flat to 0.5% across a 9.5x change in attributed work, because it is the rest of the backward — the ops the chunk does not touch. So `backward_secs` asymptotes on ~27 s: 16 → 64 captured 38.8 s of the 53.3 s available, and **256 buys 2.05 s where no chunk value can ever buy more than ~5.6 s.** Precision does not decide it: at the shipped T=1280 all four are inside the 1e-4 bar (16 4.553e-06, 64 7.478e-06, **128 2.048e-05**, 256 2.787e-05), so 128 costs 2.7x the error of 64 and stays 4.9x inside — re-measured at T=1280 rather than reusing the gate's T=128 fixture, where chunk 128 and 256 are one chunk and give the identical number. The allow-list becomes `(16, 64, 128)`; 256 and 32 both fail it, the second proving it rejects an unmeasured value and not merely a large one. Instrument bounded, not priced: `--no-instrument` at C=128 is 31.339 s warm, so this arm's 7.799 s attributed is an upper bound 3.378 s wide, and there is no per-call cost across arms — fitting one gives −102.8 µs/call, since the arm with *more* timed calls is the faster — [wins/2026-09-07-c128-backward-chunk.md](docs/experience/wins/2026-09-07-c128-backward-chunk.md)
- **accept** — **the backward's chunk goes 16 → 64: `backward_secs` 80.207 → 41.446 s, 1.935x, from one constant.** `_GDN_CHUNK` was 16 since `51e965e`, chosen over upstream's 64 on precision and never re-priced against a step time. Both arms on card 6, uncontended, the #192 recipe (prompt 256, gen 1024, group 8), T=1280, `wy_kernels` forward, `mlp` segment, warm step, `--inside-gdn`, only `--gdn-chunk` differing. **The mechanism is that per-call cost does not move at 4x the chunk:** the adjoint is 0.955 vs 0.931 ms/call and the recompute 0.556 vs 0.560 — within 3% — while the call counts drop exactly 4x (30720 = 384 × 80 → 7680 = 384 × 20), so the seconds follow the calls. The intra-chunk terms are quadratic in chunk length (`KK`, `QK` and the triangular solve are n × n, `A @ d` is n × n × DV), so 4x the chunk is 16x the arithmetic in those terms and none of it is visible: both python loops in `gdn_backward` (`reference.py:927-931`, `:952-956`) were dispatch-bound. Rows: adjoint 28.612 → 7.332, recompute 17.193 → 4.270, prologue+epilogue 5.465 → 2.283, solve 1.984 → 0.640. The unattributed remainder is flat in absolute terms (26.952 → 26.922) and is **not** tape overhead: `--inside-gdn` times three `reference` functions, so every other op in the backward falls into it by construction — registry mode at C=128 attributes 30.789 s and leaves 4.831 s where the same work leaves 26.918 s, with `backward_secs` agreeing within 2.6%. It is the rest of the backward, which the chunk does not touch, so its share rising 33.6% → 65.0% is fixed work against a smaller total, not a regression. **Cost: 3.5x gradient accuracy**, 3.26e-06 → 1.13e-05 on the estimator `51e965e` was priced on, 9x inside the 1e-4 bar — and two independent implementations of that estimator agree (the suite's gate prints 3.3e-06 / 1.1e-05, the probe 3.26e-06 / 1.13e-05). `51e965e`'s pricing was correct; what it lacked was 1.94x of a 27B backward on the other side. No kernel, no padding, no backend edit — the forward is untouched. The gate that guarded the constant is now an allow-list `_GDN_CHUNK in (16, 64)`, because an absolute bar cannot bound the chunk: the error saturates once chunk ≥ T and the whole f32 family sits inside 1e-4 (T=512: C=128 3.9e-5, C=256 9.9e-5) — [wins/2026-09-07-c64-backward-chunk.md](docs/experience/wins/2026-09-07-c64-backward-chunk.md)
- **reject** — **taking the backward's recompute from the WY kernels' stage outputs is REJECTED: 1.09e-02, 109x over the bar.** `gdn_solve_tril` returns `Ai` bf16 and `gdn_state_scan` returns `h` / `V_new` bf16, so reusing `M`, `h`, `W` and `d` instead of recomputing them costs **965x at equal chunk** (1.13e-05 → 1.09e-02 on the same estimator). Per-tensor, one rounded at a time: `d` 1.10e-02, `s` 7.83e-04, `M` 7.80e-04, `W` 1.26e-04 — all four outside 1e-4 alone, so there is no reduced variant and no "skip only the M solve". The `saved` return value #220 added stays as the harmless value it is; the T-padding that existed only to give this arm coverage beyond one prompt length in 64 is dropped. The rejection also opens the question it turns on: **1e-4 was set by fiat**, and the forward this backward is the adjoint of already rounds its state at bf16 (`backend.py:1250-1253`) where upstream's own backward asks for `state_dtype=float32` — [errors/2026-09-07-what-gradient-error-is-acceptable.md](docs/experience/errors/2026-09-07-what-gradient-error-is-acceptable.md)
- **verdict** — **row 42 closes: the queue-and-wait admission is verified live on the V100 in five arms, and the `reclaimable_blocks` guard is measured on BOTH paths.** The first four arms each failed to reach the guard for a different reason and **each failure looked like a result**. Arm 1 (production config, `--max-batch 1`): both clients 200, `waiting` peaked at 1, **B waited 518.6 s** and was admitted within one 2 s sample of A releasing — the queue-and-wait claim, and the **first measured queued wait**, which `messages.py:72` had explicitly left open ("the ceiling for a request the scheduler may hold behind a full batch... nothing has measured that"), 3.5x inside the 1800 s cap. **But `_admit` was never called for B there** — `engine.py:693` is `while self._waiting and len(self._running) < self.limits.max_batch`, so at max-batch 1 the loop body cannot run while A holds the only slot: B waited on the **batch cap**, not on a block decision, and this entry's first claim that the guard "declined on every tick" is retracted. Arm 2 could not isolate it either, because **`--max-batch` also resizes the pool**: `cli.py:133` derives `max_blocks = (ctx * max_batch) // BLOCK_TOKENS`, so 2 lifted `blocks_total` **2048 → 3494** and cut the prefix budget **1845 → 1087 MB**, B fit immediately, and the arm's eviction counts are not comparable to arm 1's. Arm 2b pinned the pool and still missed, on a **fixture defect**: one filler for both clients made B's prompt a literal prefix of A's, so B **adopted 67 blocks** on a hit (211 needed, 144 allocated) and `needed` fell below what was free. Arm 2c gave B its own filler and a distinct leading token — a hit is then impossible **by construction**, since the shared chat header is ~3 tokens against `BLOCK_TOKENS` 16 — and measured the **negative path**: `pool_used` pinned at **1877** for the whole 529.3 s wait, zero blocks reclaimed under A's live retain. Arm 3 warmed 72 blocks of completed requests first and measured the **positive path**. **Warming does not widen the window**: `free + reclaimable` is `pool − A` = **171 whatever is warmed**, because a warm block comes out of free 1:1, so it moves `free` down until the sizing rule lands inside `free < needed <= free + reclaimable` — predicted before the run (`free=99 < B=139 <= 171`) and matched to the block. **The eviction COUNT is not the discriminator in either direction**: arm 3's published−evictions gap climbs 3→11 and then holds at 11 for 540 s with no burst, and arm 3's total evictions (51) are FEWER than arm 2c's (58) — the arm that freed nothing evicted more, so the "one eviction burst" signature agreed before the run would have marked arm 3 a failure. The evidence is `pool_used` as a **level**: B needs 139 blocks on top of 1949, `1949 + 139 = 2088` exceeds the 2048-block pool, so the observed **2037 is only reachable if eviction returned at least 51 blocks**, with `prefix_hits` 0 so none of it adoption — the pool ceiling settles it without sampling, and it has to, because **`waiting` never left 0 in arm 3's 324 samples**: B was admitted on its FIRST `_admit`, so there is no wait window to time and arm 2c is the control that makes the level readable (same pool, same sizing rule, `waiting` pinned at 1 for **529.3 s**, `pool_used` pinned at 1877). **A delta between two polls attributes to whatever happened between them**: a "2037 → 2013 fall" first written here is a 24-block move at t+37.6 s with `running` already 2 since t+5.4 s — after both admissions, not B's. Two instrument defects in the window's own tooling are recorded: arm 2c's rows carry **no `published` at all** (0 of 274) because the field was added to the sampler after the pod had its copy, so an earlier draft reported a "constant gap in arm 2c AND arm 3" for an arm that could not produce a gap — the absent field read as 0 and `0 − evictions` looked like a series; and `engine.py:915`, cited here for decode-growth eviction, is a token-copy line — the eviction is at **`engine.py:886`**, checked against the base sha rather than carried forward. [errors/2026-09-07-a-prompt-that-does-not-fit-yet-is-queued.md](docs/experience/errors/2026-09-07-a-prompt-that-does-not-fit-yet-is-queued.md)

- **accept** — **the sm70 prefill attention cell is ACCEPTED as the next kernel: 49.2% of TTFT at 16k is recoverable.** Four arms of the #218 profiler on the real `qwen38-27b` on the V100 (2,048 / 8,192 / 16,384 / 21,727 tokens), all four reconciling against #213's fit at **1.28 / 1.12 / 1.10 / 1.10x** — the 21,727 arm 2.4x past the fit's largest input, and its 314.485 s within 0.05% of the independently measured 314.33 s Claude Code turn (#212). **The n² is one kernel.** Over the 16,384 arm's 32 equal chunks, `paged_attention` goes 0.0889 → 6.7584 s (**75.98x**) while `linear_fp4` 0.99x, `linear_attn_chunk` 1.01x, `rmsnorm` 1.01x and `rope` 0.99x are flat within 1%. Attention is **100.5 s of 197.6 s (50.9%)** at 16k and **185.5 s of 314.5 s (59.0%)** at 21.7k, still climbing — it crosses `linear_fp4` at prefix 7,168. **The cause is the query tile, not the FLOP.** `backend.py:933` routes every sm70 attention call to `paged_attention_split`, whose grid is `T.Kernel(KVSPLIT, S*H, B)` and whose body opens `Qf[d] = Q[bb, tt, hh, d]` — one query row per thread block, correct for the speculative verify widths ≤ `_MAX_VERIFY_W = 8` it was built for, and re-reading K/V once per row for a 512-row prefill chunk. At 16k the compute floor is **3.36 s (29.9x below measured)** and the tile-1 bandwidth floor **20.16 s (4.99x)**; a 64-query tile drops bandwidth to 0.31 s and moves the kernel into the compute-bound regime. Between the two arms the compute ratio climbs 29.9 → 31.4x while the bandwidth ratio stays nearly flat 4.99 → 5.27x, which is the signature of a bandwidth-bound kernel and is a second, independent confirmation. The KV pool is **f32** on sm70 (`backend.py:353` → `engine.py:1503`), so each removed re-read is 4 bytes, not 2. Recoverable: **97.1 s = 49.2% of TTFT** at 16k against a **15% threshold set before the numbers arrived**; 179.5 s = 57.1% at 21.7k. `linear_fp4`, the other 34–40%, is already **1.56x off the fp32 floor** with no tensor cores on Volta — fp16 `mma.sync.m8n8k4` is named as a later rung, not chased. Ships with a `_report` fix: the per-chunk trend now compares like-sized chunks, because at 21,727 the 223-token tail chunk made the raw line read `linear_fp4` **0.51x**, an op apparently getting cheaper with prefix. [wins/2026-09-07-the-sm70-prefill-n2-is-one-kernel-at-30x-its-floor.md](docs/experience/wins/2026-09-07-the-sm70-prefill-n2-is-one-kernel-at-30x-its-floor.md)
- **fix** — **a store counter that reaches no wire: `blocks_freed` was published into a dict `_build_stats` drops, and two more keys with it.** #221 added the counter, measured it in `_drop` from the free list, tested it, and its PR body said "joins the stats, so this cannot hide again" — **which was true of `kv_cache.py` and false of the endpoint**: verified live, `'blocks_freed' in /health["stats"]` is False. `_build_stats` forwards a hand-picked subset — four named keys plus `**{k: v for k, v in store.items() if k.startswith(("dram_", "ssd_"))}` — so a key matching neither rule vanishes silently, and a counter named `dram_anything` would have arrived automatically. I made the claim by reading the module that publishes the field instead of the outermost consumer; one `curl` was the whole check. **Three keys were dropped, not one**: `entries` and `capacity` are the store's fill and its ceiling, the two numbers that answer "is the prefix cache full" and which nothing else answers. **And the obvious seam test cannot express this seam**: `/health` carries `prefix_hits`, but from the ENGINE's counter (incremented in `_admit`, counting admissions), while the store's own `hits`/`misses` are dropped too — so `f"prefix_{k}" in health` is true for `hits` for the wrong reason, two counters for one concept with the shared name hiding it, and since #221 they diverge because `_match_prefix` runs once per admission attempt so a waiting request inflates the store's. Fixed additively: `prefix_blocks_freed`, `prefix_entries`, `prefix_capacity` forwarded, no rename, no removal; `_STORE_STATS_INTERNAL = ("hits", "misses")` carries the reason the store's pair stays internal — it counts lookups, and it has four in-tree readers, one feeding `bench_harness.py`'s `kv-reuse` gate whose recorded values ARE that definition, so redefining that cell inside an observability fix is the bucketing-key trap and unification is its own task. The gate asserts the **route by value**: each published key either has `/health` carrying the same value under `prefix_<k>`, or matches the splat, or is named internal. **Two of three controls found holes in the test rather than confirming it** — the first reded with `KeyError` (a crash reads as a broken test, not a finding, and would have hidden a second defect), and the both-routes control **passed**, since the test asked "routed somehow" and both branches were routes; fixing that is what surfaced the `hits` coincidence and forced the check onto values. The value check also needed a non-trivial fixture: every counter is 0 on a fresh engine, so a hardcoded `0` passes until the test inserts, looks up and clears and **asserts the counters moved before comparing them**. `450 passed, 14 skipped`. [errors/2026-09-07-a-store-counter-that-reaches-no-wire.md](docs/experience/errors/2026-09-07-a-store-counter-that-reaches-no-wire.md)

- **error** — **a per-op prefill profile of the wrong model printed a clean table, and every sm70 attention share from 2026-09-07 is withdrawn.** `scripts/prof_prefill_ops.py` refuses any model but `tiny` by design (the CPU gate); I handed over a V100 command containing `--model qwen38-27b`, which that guard refuses, plus `/work/tl013/bin/python` and `/work/prefill_ops.json`, paths that belong to the H20 pod — there is no `/work` mount on the V100. The run therefore profiled **2 layers, hidden 64, random weights**, and reported `paged_attention` at 32.6% of a 16k prefill as if it described a 27B prompt. **Nothing caught it because every assertion asked whether the INSTRUMENT worked, and it did**: each tick timed an op, the first chunk's prefix was 0, chunks summed to the prompt, attention was on the timed path — all four are true of a profile of the wrong model. The contradiction was in the output for three hours and one division away: **0.813 s at n=16,384 against #213's own fit predicting 179.7 s, 221x**. A second arm passed the same gate while its first chunk held **5.276 s of `paged_attention` and 4.008 s of `write_tokens`** — TileLang compiling inside the timed region, which would have reported a 42.3% share that was a compile. **I also offered the CPU profile's 64% share as a prior for the 27B, and it is not one**: attention's FLOP crosses the linears' at prefix **640 for tiny and 121,173 for the 27B**, so at 16k the attention/linear ratio is 12.8 against 0.07 — a factor of ~190 in exactly the dimension being measured. Three gates ship: the per-chunk sum must land within **2x** of the fit's `C1·n + C2·n²` (asserted, ratio printed; non-27B runs print `SHARES HERE DO NOT DESCRIBE THE 27B` instead), a **warm-up arm at the largest arm's real shapes** outside the timed region with its own printed line, and a **chunk-0 outlier assertion**. Both gates were exercised on the V100's actual numbers rather than stand-ins: the 5.276 s arm dies on the median comparison, the 0.813 s run dies at 0.00453x, and healthy data passes both. One control did not behave as expected and is recorded — removing the warm-up still passes on cpu, because the CPU target has no JIT to warm, so that gate is unobservable on the machine the suite runs on. The script now loads the served checkpoint through `_build_model`. **What survives is the mechanism, on real sm70 kernels**: only `paged_attention` rises with prefix (0.0006 → 0.0138 s over 0 → 15,872, **22.86x**, R² 0.9999) while every other op is flat within 9%, and the provenance matrix confirms from the compile log that `paged_attention_split` ran, so the CPU-authored maker is registered on sm70 and unreachable. The quadratic is in attention; its share of the 27B's prefill is unmeasured. [errors/2026-09-07-a-profile-of-the-wrong-model-printed-a-clean-table.md](docs/experience/errors/2026-09-07-a-profile-of-the-wrong-model-printed-a-clean-table.md)
- **fix** — **a prompt that does not fit YET is queued instead of refused: the 503 on a 93%-of-pool prompt was an ordering defect, not a capacity one.** `submit` allocated blocks and the state slot up front and had no later tick to retry on, so a request that the pool could serve as soon as a live one finished was refused **permanently**. **The ruling's premise needed one correction read off the code — allocation already evicted**, calling `evict_until_free(needed)` on the line before the refusal, so the defect was never a missing eviction call but eviction running and freeing too little. Two CPU arms at the production ratio separate the two possible causes, and it is **cause 2**: with only completed requests in the store, eviction works (`free_blocks` 34 → 64, freed 30, 6 entries dropped, satisfied); with a **live** request sharing the prefix — production's shape, `running=1` and 119 blocks held — it **dropped 2 entries covering 20 blocks and freed zero**, because `free_block` (`kv_cache.py:94`) is a refcount decrement that returns a block only at refcount 0. So "evict harder" was never available, and **`prefix_evictions` at 76 and rising was not evidence the store was releasing anything** — a drop that frees nothing still increments it. Fixed by moving allocation to the planner: `submit` takes an id and a place in `_waiting`; `_admit` takes slot and blocks **together**, so `_release` has one path and `state_slot is None` is the single test for "never admitted". The admission condition is `free_blocks` after eviction — a measurement, not a forecast — but the eviction is **guarded** by a new `reclaimable_blocks()`, without which a request waiting on a live request would drop every entry on every planner tick and flush every other client's prefix cache for the whole wait. **That predicate's obvious form is wrong**: a refcount above 1 does not mean a live request holds the block, because the store holds one once per entry and a growing prefix republishes — measured, entries at 128 and 160 tokens share their first 8 blocks, so `refcount == 1` predicted **6 reclaimable where eviction delivered 30**, a 5x undercount that as an admission test refuses requests the pool can serve; the test is refcount minus the store's own holders. `blocks_freed` joins the store's stats, measured in `_drop` from the free list — **but it reaches no wire, which I first claimed it did**: verified live, `'blocks_freed' in /health["stats"]` is False, because `_build_stats` forwards four named keys plus a `dram_`/`ssd_` prefix splat and drops anything matching neither, so the claim was made by reading the publishing module instead of the outermost consumer (exposing it is a follow-up; until then the signature is `prefix_evictions` rising while `pool_used_blocks` does not fall); `evictions` is left alone (23 call sites, and `ssd_evictions` would diverge from its sibling). **A fourth defect found in my own change and the one that would have been worst:** `alloc_slot` raising inside `_admit` propagates to `step()`, whose handler fails **every running request**, so one queued request arriving with the slots full would have killed all the live ones — `_admit` now tests `free_slots < 1` by count rather than catching the raise. Head-of-line FIFO (`break`, not `continue`): admitting a smaller request past a blocked larger one starves the larger one for as long as the load lasts. The prefix match moves with the allocation, since a hit found at submit can be evicted before admission, so **hit counts across this change are not comparable** — more hits, not fewer. Three gate arms (two clients at 92.2% of a 64-block pool; a failed admission asserting **refcounts** and the free-slot count rather than "no exception escaped"; an impossible prompt still refused at submit) and three controls, each red on its own assertion: unconditional admission → `insufficient KV blocks for request` verbatim, the guard removed → blocked admissions evicting 1/1/1, the unwind's `free_block` loop removed → every retained block one count too high. **Two versions of one assert were wrong in opposite directions**: `entries >= entries` **passed with the guard removed** (the count rises as the live request publishes its own chunk boundaries), and the global `evictions` counter was **red on correct code** (decode growth at `engine.py:886` evicts legitimately for a running request, and `insert` trims at capacity) — it now wraps `_admit` and asserts per attempt, `[0]*26 + [3]`, the 3 being the admission that succeeded once the first request finished. **Three fixture defects, each producing a plausible answer:** a poll loop that spun without sleeping so the loop thread never ran (0 prefill forwards, 0 published, read as "the store had nothing to evict" — the #208 class), a token id at 320 against tiny's vocab of 320 arriving from `poll()` as an engine error rather than a bad fixture, and arm 1's correct "no 503" which alone would have reported the defect as unreproducible. Five existing tests changed: three mechanical (state now produced at admission, so they need a `step()` first) and two whose assertions are false by design — #209's waiting-request cancel arm, inverted to "the count must not move" with **its original behaviour added back at the admitted level** so that PR keeps its test, and the rollback arm, which **was vacuous as first rewritten** because at `max_new_tokens=1` each request finished inside its own `step()` and freed the slot before the next was considered. **#216 verified live in the same push**: `/health` during a real 30k prefill reads **median 1.24 ms (min 1.16, max 1.36)** against 8.12 s median and 87.66 s max before the fix, at `running=1` and `blocks_used=939`. `448 passed, 14 skipped`. [errors/2026-09-07-a-prompt-that-does-not-fit-yet-is-queued.md](docs/experience/errors/2026-09-07-a-prompt-that-does-not-fit-yet-is-queued.md)

- **bench** — **a Claude Code turn against this endpoint takes 314 s, and 294 of those seconds are prefill with zero tokens emitted.** Question 3 of the agent-turn series, answered from the proxy's rows: 3 turns, all 200, **314.33 / 16.39 / 13.18 s**, 343.90 s total, client exit 0 and `ok` on disk in the two files it was asked to write. The pool sampler (5 s cadence, on the pod) says where turn 1 goes: `tokens_generated` sits at **250 for the first 294 s** while `prefill_forwards` climbs **3 → 46**, and the mechanism is **per-token and quadratic, not per-chunk** — `max_num_batched_tokens = 512` (`engine.py:183`) against a 21,727-token prompt is 43 chunks, matching the observed +43 exactly, but `chunks = ceil(tokens/512)` makes per-chunk and per-token collinear so that arm cannot distinguish them; 25's same-chunk pair (#213) can, and does: 544 → 1024 tokens holds `prefill_forwards` at 2 while TTFT goes 2.57 → 5.16 s. **I stated the per-chunk mechanism first and it is corrected here** — this 294 s is instead the out-of-sample check on their fit, which predicts **287 s against 294 s measured (2.4%)** from a largest arm of 9,483 tokens, against 174 s for a linear model. So `max_num_batched_tokens` is not the lever and the prefill kernel is. Once decoding starts it is fast: 270 tokens over 140 decode forwards in the last 60 s. Turns 2 and 3 are 19x cheaper because the prefix cache hit (`prefix_hits` 0 → 2). **The first attempt measured nothing and that refusal is its own finding:** the turn 400-ed at `request (39472 tokens) exceeds max_total_tokens (32768)`, and attributing it through the server's own `render_prompt` on the pod tokenizer gives tools 18,871 (47.8%) · **this machine's CLAUDE.md 11,090 + session hook 7,462 (47.0% combined)** · system 2,039 — total 39,469 against the server's 39,472, the 3 being `max_new_tokens=1` since `room_for` returned 0 and #207's `max(1, …)` passed it through to be refused. **The 400 is correct behaviour**; what it says is that this repo's own sessions need a bigger context, not that the clamp is broken. With `CLAUDE_CONFIG_DIR` at an empty dir the same client sends **21,727 tokens and fits with 11,041 to spare**. Prompt occupies **66% of the KV pool** (1,358 of 2,048 blocks; peak 1,404, min free 644), so a second concurrent client of this shape still does not fit and the queue-and-wait work is untouched by this. **`/health` median 8.12 s and max 87.66 s during prefill against 0.002 s idle** — four orders of magnitude on the same code, confirming the contention is the engine lock held across `step()`. **Four instrument failures paid for this, three of which produced a clean wrong table:** a char count against a token ceiling (0.76x the server's figure — the server wraps schemas in `<tools>` with instructions, so only `render_prompt` agrees, at 0.9999x); a body captured from a tempdir instead of the sending cwd (CLAUDE.md 8,518 vs **41,482** chars, 5x apart on the deciding part); `--settings '{"hooks":{}}'` suppressing **nothing** (hook still 25,896 chars — only `CLAUDE_CONFIG_DIR` dropped it to 0, checked against a stub *before* booking the card, or arm 2 would have reproduced the 400); and a `--allowedTools` sweep whose three arms all sent 29 tools because the flag gates permission rather than the schema set — preceded by the same sweep printing "arm done" three times having sent **zero** requests, the prompt eaten as a second tool value and the client's output sent to `/dev/null`. [wins/2026-09-07-a-claude-code-turn-is-314-seconds-of-prefill.md](docs/experience/wins/2026-09-07-a-claude-code-turn-is-314-seconds-of-prefill.md)
- **error** — **`/health` waited on the engine lock: median 8.12 s and max 87.66 s during a 21.7k-token prefill, against 0.002 s idle on the same code.** `stats()` took `self._lock`, and `step()` holds that same lock across the whole forward, so a liveness call arriving mid-tick waited for a forward to finish. **Second defect on the symptom #208 fixed, and it survived that fix** — #208 was the event loop starving on a blocking poll, this is the lock, and the two are independent; `peek()` (`engine.py:623`) had already solved this exact shape and says why in its own docstring ("one blocked call covered 325 ms of a 335 ms run"), so `stats()` was the same kind of reader that never got the same treatment. Fixed by having the loop publish a snapshot that `stats()` returns without the lock; the dict is replaced and never mutated, so a reader sees one consistent generation, which is `peek`'s GIL argument. **Two details a red gate found rather than reading:** the publish must happen **before** the forward as well as after — with only the trailing publish the FIRST forward has no snapshot, `stats()` falls back to the locking path, and the gate measured **106 s against a 2 s sleep**; and a **direct-drive caller must get live numbers**, since with no loop thread there is no background forward to wait on and a caller reading between its own `step()` calls sees the previous tick — the full suite went red on `test_a_rejected_submit_does_not_release_the_prefix_stores_blocks` with `no prefix hit; the test is inert`, on a hit that had happened. The trailing publish stays and sits in `finally`, because `_loop` stops calling `step` once nothing runs, so it is the only publish that can carry what the last tick left, including what a **failed** forward left. `DataParallelEngine` needs no change: it calls each replica's `stats()`. Gate uses a **real** `Engine`, since the property under test is which lock `stats()` takes and a double that reimplements `stats()` would assert its own behaviour; `_run_forward` becomes a 2.0 s sleep, 20x the 100 ms assertion, and the gate waits on an event set inside that fake forward so it cannot pass against an engine that was never busy. **Three controls, each red on the elapsed assertion:** reading through the lock again → 2.01 s, exactly the injected sleep; the pre-forward publish removed → 4.02 s, two forwards' worth; and the served path forced onto the direct-drive branch → red, which is the one that matters, because after adding `if self._thread is None` a green could have meant the snapshot was never exercised. Corroborated independently by 25 at a different prompt length: median 20.2 s, max 55.3 s on a fresh 8192 prefill against 1 ms idle, and it is what made their first cancel measurement unattributable. **An idle measurement of a contended path is the unrepresentative one** — earlier the same day I read `/health` at 9 ms on a freshly restarted endpoint and called this work non-urgent on that basis. `444 passed, 14 skipped`. [errors/2026-09-07-health-waited-on-the-engine-lock.md](docs/experience/errors/2026-09-07-health-waited-on-the-engine-lock.md)

- **accept-or-reject verdict** — **the 512-token chunk budget is rejected as the prefill lever: V100 prefill cost is per token and quadratic, not per chunk.** A Claude Code turn spends 294 s of its 314 s before the first token on a 21,727-token prompt, 43 ticks at `max_num_batched_tokens = 512` (`engine.py:183`) — and a per-token kernel and a per-chunk overhead predict that equally well, because `chunks = ceil(tokens/512)` makes them collinear on any plain length sweep. The discriminator is a pair with the **same chunk count** and different token counts, derived by replaying the scheduler's own cut (`engine.py:678-698`) rather than assuming `n/512`: **544 → 1024 holds `prefill_forwards` at 2 while tokens rise 104% and TTFT rises 101%** (2.57 → 5.16 s; repeats at 1024 sd 0.04 s, 0.8%). Residuals of a token-only fit against `forwards` are ≤0.47 s over a 2.57–77.68 s range with no structure, so the chunk count adds nothing once tokens are known. **The real finding is the curvature**: tok/s falls 178 → 122 across 460 → 9483 tokens, a straight line fits at R² 0.9827 with an unphysical −3.87 s intercept, and `ttft = 0.56 + 0.00422·n + 4.117e-07·n²` fits at **R² 0.9999** — the n² term is 9% of the cost at 1k tokens and **68% at 21.7k**. Out-of-sample: that fit predicts **287 s** at n=21,727 against a **294 s** measured by another session on another day (2.4%), where the linear model says 174 s. So raising the chunk budget buys nothing — it regroups the same tokens, lengthens each lock-holding tick, and leaves the n² untouched; **the sm90/sm70 prefill kernel is the target**. Three instrument defects were caught first, each of which returns a plausible number rather than an error: a `/health` poll inside every timed arm (`stats()` takes the lock `step()` holds across a forward, `engine.py:731` — measured median **20.2 s**, max **55.3 s** under prefill against 1 ms idle, so the probe would have timed its own contention), `prompt_tokens: None` on every row because streaming attaches usage only under `stream_options.include_usage` (`server.py:475`), and a quiet gate on `running == 0` that is already true while the previous request's last tick is in flight. **And the first same-chunk pair I chose was wrong in the direction that reverses the answer** — 512 vs 544 do not share a chunk count (1 vs 2), so a flat result there would have read as per-chunk; replaying the cut caught it before the run. [wins/2026-09-07-v100-prefill-is-quadratic-not-per-chunk.md](docs/experience/wins/2026-09-07-v100-prefill-is-quadratic-not-per-chunk.md)
- **error** — **`/v1/messages` is `async def` and polled a blocking wait, so the server answered nothing else for the length of every reply — over 10 minutes per turn on this endpoint.** `_run` waits by `engine.take` plus `time.sleep(0.02)`; on the event loop that starves every other route, `/health` timed out and 17 CLOSE-WAIT sockets piled up, and `take()` also contends for the engine lock `step()` holds across each forward, so the poll competes with the work it waits for. Found by `py-spy` on the live serve child, not by reading: MainThread blocked in `engine.take` (`engine.py:643`) from `messages._run` (`messages.py:215`) inside the route at `messages.py:287`. Fixed with `await asyncio.to_thread(_run, ...)` — the form `server.py:297`'s chat route has had from the start. **Two routes, not the one the report named:** enumerating every `async def` across the three route modules found `/v1/responses` with the identical defect (`responses.py:259` called `_run` directly, its poll sleeping at `:186`), fixed in the same PR — so of the three routes that generate, two were broken and the correct one is the one whose pattern the other two were meant to copy. One `to_thread` covers streaming too: every blocking part (encode, `submit`, the poll, the decode) is inside `_run`, which runs before the stream split, and `sse()` only formats the already-built body. **Measured: `/health` answers in under 1.0 s with both fixes, 1.86 s without `messages`' and 1.80 s without `responses`' — controls run per route, each reding its own arm and leaving the other green**, so a single-arm gate would have passed with one route still broken, which is how this shipped. The control reds the elapsed assertion by name with the in-flight assertion passing first, so the red is the freeze and not a server that was never busy. **Why this endpoint made it unmissable:** Claude Code's system prompt plus tools is about 30k tokens here (`prompt_len 30485`, `budget 2282` — server recorder, one reading), so every turn is a 30k prefill on a V100 with 2.3k tokens of room, and a 10-minute turn is a 10-minute freeze; it is the same prompt shape that produced the 400 fixed in #207. **Fourth defect in this shape on this seam** — `limits`, `room_for`, the hand-rolled clamp, now the missing `to_thread` — each one route over, each found in production. **A single-request test suite cannot see it:** all 436 arms passed throughout, none of them concurrent, so the gate had to hold one request open and issue another. [errors/2026-09-07-an-async-route-that-polls-a-blocking-wait.md](docs/experience/errors/2026-09-07-an-async-route-that-polls-a-blocking-wait.md)
- **error** — **closing a chat socket did not stop the request: 1891 tokens and 104 KV blocks were generated for a reader who had already left.** #200 shipped a stop button and `transport.ts` said the tokens on screen are the reply — true for the page, and it quietly implied the server had stopped too. It had not. `server.py`'s `WebSocketDisconnect` branch called `gen.close()`, which ends `_deltas`' poll loop and nothing else, and **there was no cancel path on the engine at all**: the only removal is `_finish`, reached from completion or a `step()` error. The SSE route did not even close its generator. So an abandoned request ran to its cap, which since #195 is the context remainder. Measured on the live V100 (`aacc936`), idle endpoint, socket closed at **t=2.81 s** after 12 deltas with 23 characters on the client: `running` went to 1 at t=4.01 and stayed there until **t=37.38**, `tokens_generated` 4236 → 6127, blocks 0 → 104 → 0 — it ended by finishing its answer, not by being dropped. Fixed with `Engine.cancel(request_id)` under `_lock`, called from the WS disconnect branch and the SSE generator's `GeneratorExit`. **`_finish` could not be reused as-is:** it ends `self._running.remove(req)` and **raises `ValueError` on a request still in `_waiting`**, and a waiting request is not free to drop either — `submit` allocates its blocks and state slot up front — so the teardown is now shared as `_release` and `cancel` removes from whichever queue holds the id. That case is a tab closed during a queue backup, not an exotic path. A cancelled request is filed under `_failed` so a late `take()` raises rather than returning None; `_failed` growth gets a ponytail line naming a TTL sweep. `DataParallelEngine` gets `cancel` because the seam gate derives its required set from route source. Four controls, each run separately: `_finish` verbatim → `list.remove(x): x not in list`; WS branch not cancelling → `found 1`; SSE `GeneratorExit` removed → `the SSE route needs GeneratorExit`; `cancel` freeing nothing → `blocks not returned: 3 of 3`. **The measurement nearly failed twice** — the first attempt polled `/health` inline after the close and a starved event loop (v100's trial holding it synchronously) made the leak and a dead endpoint indistinguishable, and a `curl -o /tmp/h.json` poll read back another session's healthy body from a shared path while every one of my requests returned 0 bytes. **440 passed, 14 skipped** on the rebase onto #208. **Verified live on the merge sha `09e1e84`:** blocks and `running` back to 0 **0.16 s** after the close (104 blocks for ~34 s before), tokens 220 → 226 against ~47 tok/s, so the cancel landed inside the step already in flight. Two instrument corrections came out of it. The first repeat sampled every 2 s, so the close fell between rows and its 55-token gap was unattributable — a **level** survives a slow sampler, a **delta across an instant** does not. And `blocks_used` cannot prove this alone: it is the counter `_release` itself decrements (`engine.py:1255`), so a `free_block` that never landed would still print 0. The allocator's independent `pool_used_blocks` rose 1 → 7 → 17 across two cancels, which looked like a leak and is the prefix cache — the control is one **normally completing** request moving the pool 17 → 19 with `prefix_published` 7 → 9 and `blocks_used` 0, so completion retains exactly as cancel does. [errors/2026-09-07-a-closed-socket-does-not-stop-the-request.md](docs/experience/errors/2026-09-07-a-closed-socket-does-not-stop-the-request.md)
- **default flip** — **an optimizer step no longer drops the decode graphs: `invalidate_weights()` keeps them and refills the cached casts instead.** Worth **3.87 s off a 133.65 s GRPO step (2.9%)** on H20 card 6 at group 8 / gen 1024 / LoRA-16 / micro 1, arms back to back in one session, four recaptures removed per step; 2.48 s of the delta is decode, where a recapture lands, and the 1.38 s in backward sits inside the 4.61 s span of six dense readings from six trees, so it is noise rather than a term. An in-place update leaves every address a capture baked where it was — both optimizers end `p.copy_()`, `materialize` rebuilds the dict but not the tensors — **except a cached cast**: `_const_f32` refills only when CALLED, and a graph replay calls nothing, so #190 made the refill possible without making it happen and keeping the graphs on that basis alone would have been silently off-policy. `Backend.refill_const_f32()` walks the cache and re-casts each live entry into its existing buffer. **The shipped LoRA recipe cannot exercise the refill** — `rl_step` passes `trainable`, so only `lora_a`/`lora_b` move and no adapter reaches a kernel through `_const_f32`, so the walk correctly refills 0 however long the run goes; a full-parameter AdamW arm on tiny refills **11 of 11** cached casts, the kept graph agrees with an eager engine on the same post-update weights, and the rollout changes. `keep_graphs` is deleted rather than defaulted: no caller wants the old behaviour. **The 14 s/capture I projected onto this was wrong by 14x** — it is V100 sm70 first-capture-with-JIT per that entry's own ponytail line, and 43% vs 3% of a step support different decisions about whether the work was worth doing; the comparable figure in the same entry is 208 s → 19 s for 8 graphs. Two labels lied and are named in the entry: a field called `casts_refilled` held whatever `invalidate_weights()` returns, so the arm whose revert makes that graphs-dropped read "4 casts refilled" against a true count of 0, and `params_moved: 0` counted addresses, where 0 is the premise holding. The per-tick instrument's 1 s floor sits above the ~0.6 s a warm recapture appears to cost, so the per-capture breakdown is reported as **not separately verified**. AGENTS.md drops the 73.62 → 34.09 s figure, which is sound but `max_new_tokens=256` against this recipe's 1024. [wins/2026-09-07-an-update-keeps-the-decode-graphs.md](docs/experience/wins/2026-09-07-an-update-keeps-the-decode-graphs.md)
- **error** — **`/v1/messages` clamped `max_tokens` against one of the two ceilings `submit` enforces, so every Claude Code turn 400-ed on the V100 and the agent trial could not start a single turn.** The clamp computed `max_total_tokens − prompt` by hand while `submit` checks that **and** the KV pool including the `width − 1` drafts a verify tick materializes (`engine.py:517`, `:523`). Bounding one does not merely under-clamp — it lands the request on the pool's edge **exactly**: `budget = 32768 − prompt` makes `total = 32768` for any prompt ≥ 768 whatever its length, and `blocks_for_tokens(32768 + width − 1) = 2049 > 2048` refuses it by `width − 1` tokens. The V100's 2048 blocks hold precisely its 32768-token context, and Claude Code always asks `max_tokens: 32000` behind a multi-thousand-token system prompt, so every turn landed in the failing region. Fixed by asking `engine.room_for(len(input_ids))`, which returns the `min` of both and has existed since #195. **Measured live on `26db7bd`, prompt 1212 tokens (`room_for` = 31555): 31555 admitted, 31556 refused** — so `room_for` is tight to the token, the hand-rolled clamp was off by exactly `width − 1`, and `width ≥ 2` is proven from outside the process (at width 1, 32768 tokens is exactly 2048 blocks and the arm would have passed; `spec.py:292` is `width = depth + 1`, which agrees independently). `/v1/chat/completions` is the control and correct on both arms — it refuses a client-named cap that does not fit, per OpenAI semantics, and calls `room_for` when the cap is omitted — so the defect was one route wide. The gate is a real 32-block engine (512 pool tokens against a 4096 context, so the pool binds by ~9x, the CPU analogue of the V100's shape) that **asserts it binds on the pool** before asserting anything else; a fake with a declared pool and width would restate `room_for`'s formula and let a broken `room_for` pass. The boundary is asserted at the seam rather than through the route, because after the fix a client cap above `room_for` is clamped **down** rather than refused. Negative control red on its own assertion: `a 32000-token ask 400-ed on a pool-bound engine: request (4096 tokens) exceeds KV pool capacity`, the same landing-on-the-edge shape as 32768. **`max(1, …)` is load-bearing**: `room_for` returns 0 when the prompt alone does not fit, and `submit` skips both ceiling checks when `max_new_tokens` is 0. **Third instance on this seam, each one route over** — `limits` missing from `DataParallelEngine` (400 on every turn under `--devices`), `room_for` missing (500 on every omitted cap), now a route that has `room_for` and hand-rolls half of it; the seam gate enumerates what the routes *call* and cannot see the third shape. #195's review asked which routes have an *omitted*-cap case — `/v1/messages` has none, since Anthropic declares the field required — and the **clamp** path needed the same arithmetic, which nobody asked. **A second defect the same trial found, via #201's instrument:** `unknown_fields` went non-null in production for the first time, `{'tool_choice': 'dict{type}'}` — a documented Anthropic parameter this route declared nowhere and therefore answered as if it had honoured. `/v1/responses` has refused it since it landed; the helper moved to `prompt.unsupported_choice` and now serves both routes, taking either spelling (Anthropic's `any`/`tool`, OpenAI's `required`). Six arms, and the three **permissive** ones carry the weight: a fix refusing every `tool_choice` would pass a one-sided test, and `auto` is what a client sends when it means "your choice". **The frequency claim I first wrote here was wrong and is corrected by measurement**: `scripts/capture_client_body.py` drives the real CLI against a recording stub and diffs what it sends against `MessagesRequest.model_fields` — **10 fields sent, 14 declared, 0 undeclared**, and `tool_choice` is in neither captured request, so it is conditional rather than per-turn, which is why it evaded every earlier observation (negative control: hiding `thinking`/`tools` from the declared set makes the diff name both, so the zero is a measurement and not an empty diff). **And a third finding, from the controls:** the failing turns wrote **no recorder row at all** — the refusal is raised inside `submit` before `_record`, so the request log is blind to exactly the failure class worth reading. [errors/2026-09-07-every-claude-code-turn-400s-on-the-second-ceiling.md](docs/experience/errors/2026-09-07-every-claude-code-turn-400s-on-the-second-ceiling.md)
- **accept** — **the checkpoint segment can be the whole decoder layer, and it ships as the shape-picked arm rather than the default: 1.079x on backward where the MLP segment already works, 3.63x off the forward peak, and a gen-4096 step that runs where every previous attempt OOMed.** `autograd.checkpoint` wrapped `_mlp_body` alone, so every layer's attention and GDN activations stayed on the tape for the whole forward — [#204](docs/experience/wins/2026-09-06-checkpointing-covers-the-mlp-only.md) measured 697.8 MiB retained per segment against the 85.0 MiB the wrapper stores. `forward(..., segment=)` now takes `"layer"`, and `_step` selects it by T at `train.py:162` (`_MLP_SEGMENT_MAX_T = 1280`). **Three rows, both arms of each measured in one session on card 6 at `c61c1aa`, because six neighbouring cards ran another team's job at 98-100% the whole time and a same-code arm drifted 133.65 → 140.43 s under it:** gen 1024 `backward_secs` **69.748 → 75.259 s = 1.079x** (warm means of steps 2-3, spreads 0.18 / 0.46 s, so the 5.51 s regression is **11.9x the largest within-arm spread**), gen 4096 forward peak **54.038 → 14.896 GiB = 3.63x** with the accumulation over 64 segments **42.929 → 4.228 GiB** and the per-segment rise **697.8 → 68.7 MiB**, and the gen-4096 step **fits** (`ROW3_EXIT=0`, 518.5 s: backward 269.375 + rollout 249.034, reconciling to 0.0012 s). **68.7 MiB/segment is below the 85.0 MiB of one retained `[T,hidden]` input**, so the layer segment now retains less than the MLP one stored, and #204's 88%-by-arithmetic claim is confirmed by removal. **The pairing changed the number, not just its error bar:** the same control read **67.77 s** on a quiet box and 69.748 s here, a 1.029x offset that is **36% of the effect** — citing the earlier figure would have reported 1.11x, right in direction and wrong in size, which is what lets that class of error survive. **Why neither arm is deleted:** cap 4096 at group 8 was ruled unreachable on one card by [#192](docs/experience/wins/2026-09-06-one-grpo-step-is-54-percent-backward.md) and again by [#202](docs/experience/errors/2026-09-06-chunked-attention-was-the-wrong-term.md) after chunked attention failed to reach it; this reaches it, and taxing every shape we actually run by 1.079x to buy one we do not run yet would be the mistake available here. GDN is the only impure layer, so its state and conv window are gathered outside the segment and handed in — a replay that re-gathered would read what its own forward advanced — while the scatter stays inside, untaped and recomputing the same values. Full attention is pure under training via **two** gates, not one: `write_tokens` at `model.py:341` is in the `else` of `if kv.dense`, and the fused sm90 `attn_prep`, which also writes KV, is separately gated at `:282`. **A guard that could never fail, found by running the arm rather than reading it:** the `win_parity` assertion held a reference to the live pool tensor (so it compared it with itself) behind a `[num_slots]` shape that raises above one slot — and `_training_kv` sizes `num_slots` by batch, so the batch-1 test helper was the one shape where both defects are invisible. Selecting the arm at `train.py:162` and running the real `grpo_loop` turned **11 tests in `test_rl.py` red**. Controls: 27 named gradients identical between arms (rel 0.0) with a source mutation of `_gdn` moving 26 of 27 (worst 8.087e-01), the parity flip now failing with `moved [0, 0] -> [1, 0]`, the selector red both ways (hardcoded segment names the call site, a threshold past 4352 names T), and every arm printing its file sha and `segment=` count at launch with both reverts `diff`-verified byte-exact. **ponytail: the crossover is somewhere in 1280..4352 and the threshold sits at the bottom, so shapes between pay 1.079x** — both arms at 2048 and 3072 move it. [wins/2026-09-07-a-layer-wide-checkpoint-segment.md](docs/experience/wins/2026-09-07-a-layer-wide-checkpoint-segment.md)

## 2026-09-06

- **error** — **a `max_tokens` too small to escape the reasoning block returns `finish_reason=length` with `content: ""`, which a client renders as a blank bubble — the reported "dropped stream" with no drop.** The thinking model emits reasoning into `reasoning_content` first, and the two share one budget with no reservation, so a cap below the reasoning length never reaches the answer. Measured on the live V100 (`--max-batch 1 --max-ctx 32768 --depth 1`), non-streaming for an exact body, 3 prompts × 7 caps: **the smallest cap producing any content is 32 / 64 / 128** for "Name one colour." / "What is 17 times 23?" / "…why the sky is blue." — the threshold is how much the model thinks about that prompt, so **no fixed cap is safe for all prompts**. At cap 8 the reasoning was 35/51/41 chars with the full cap consumed: the model was working, the budget died inside the thought. **No server change, deliberately**: `content:""` + `reasoning_content` + `length` is the OpenAI reasoning convention, and the page already renders it as truncated (`protocol.ts:73` `outcome()`, `render.ts:187` `settle()`), gated by `tests/test_chat_ui.py:526` which drives real WS frames at `max_tokens` = the reasoning length. #195's omitted-cap default closes the other route in. **Two candidates refuted, both recorded so they are not re-opened:** concurrent queueing at `--max-batch 1` is real (TTFT **3.19 s** vs the first request's **3.40 s** total) but drops nothing — 2/3/4 concurrent arrivals against a 120-line stream all completed, zero errors, long stream 0.99x its quiet control; and WS idle without ping is wrong twice over — `cli.py:202` passes no ws arguments so uvicorn 0.52.3's **`ws_ping_interval=20.0`** applies (read off `Config.__init__` on the pod interpreter, not from docs), and an idle socket held **90.0 s, 4.5 ping intervals, no frame and no close**, then served a request on the same connection. A 0.81 s stall (19.8x the quiet 40.8 ms max gap) **did not reproduce** — 0 stalls above 5x the quiet mean across 4 spaced arrivals — and is recorded as unreproduced, not as a mechanism. **The rule this cost:** a probe reporting `arrivals 4, stalls 0` read as a clean negative while **all four arrival arms had failed** — they hit this very mechanism, emitted zero content frames, left `ttft` at `None`, and my completeness check tested `ttft` rather than the error key, so a zero from an arm that never ran was indistinguishable from a zero from an arm that saw nothing. [errors/2026-09-06-a-cap-spent-on-reasoning-renders-as-a-dropped-stream.md](docs/experience/errors/2026-09-06-a-cap-spent-on-reasoning-renders-as-a-dropped-stream.md)
- **reject** — **chunked attention is REVERTED: it cost 1.37x-1.45x on backward and the OOM it was built for is not in attention.** #196 replaced the training path's dense `[B,H,Tq,Tk]` with a flash-style tiled forward and backward, on the premise that the T² score matrix is what makes `gen 4096` OOM at group 8 — a premise I refined (27.1 → **10.16 GiB** peak live, since the tape saves q/k/v and never `att`) instead of testing. **gen 4096 still OOMs with it, and the traceback never enters attention**: it dies at `model.py:434 _mlp` → `autograd.py:45 checkpoint` → `backend.py:461 _epilogue`, and the failed allocation names itself — **290.00 MiB requested against one `[4352, 17408]` f32 MLP intermediate at 289.0 MiB**, with `intermediate_size=17408`.
