Chunked freed up to 10.16 GiB and the step still needed 95.10 of 95.22 GiB. **Cost, gen 1024 group 8 LoRA-16 micro 1 on H20 card 6, same probe sha256 `f1c4b6d6dd86` on every tree**: backward 97.94 s chunked (warm mean of 3, spread 0.9%) against **67.77 s** on the pre-#196 parent 36bfe6f (warm mean of 2) = **1.445x**, step 1.230x; against #192's 71.53 it is 1.369x. **The figure is a range because the two dense runs differ by 3.76 s (5.3%) on identical code** — 1.37x-1.45x depending on the reference, and both are given rather than the flattering one; the direction is not in doubt at +30.2 s against a ±3.8 s spread. **The control also exonerates #190**, which sat between the trees and whose cast path does run inside the backward window (64 recomputed MLP forwards): 36bfe6f vs a16ff9c is **0.947x** on backward, nothing outside noise, and `backend.py:1424`'s early return means only the first call after a version bump copies. Revert verified by content: all four files byte-identical to `766666b^`, zero references to the chunked symbols left. **The code is shelved, not wrong** — correct, gated, parity-checked to 6.2e-07, and PR #196's diff is where to find it if a shape ever makes T² bind. **No sm90 cell**: it would speed up a path that buys nothing at the shapes we run. The 4096 cap decision is unchanged (unreachable at group 8 on one card), for a different reason than #192 states. Still open: what holds 91.7 GiB during a gen-4096 forward — `checkpoint` retains each layer's input by construction (`args = (layer_idx, x, kv, backend)`, `[4352, 5120]` f32 = 85 MiB/layer), and whether 64 are simultaneously live is unmeasured, which is exactly the size-times-layers mistake this entry is about. [errors/2026-09-06-chunked-attention-was-the-wrong-term.md](docs/experience/errors/2026-09-06-chunked-attention-was-the-wrong-term.md)
- **default flip** — **the chat page paints incrementally, follows the stream only when the reader is at the bottom, and can be stopped.** Third of ckl's UI items. `paint` used to re-parse the whole answer and replace every node on every frame — O(reply²) over a stream, and it discards the DOM under the reader's selection; `lastBlockStart` now marks where the block still being written begins, settled blocks are inserted once before the tail and never touched again. Fences are counted first, since inside an open one the whole block is provisional (blank lines included), so the boundary is that fence's own start. **Scroll position is measured BEFORE the paint and applied after** — once the new tokens are in the DOM the old position is no longer at the bottom, so a check after the fact cannot tell "following along" from "reading back"; the threshold is 32px because sub-pixel layout and zoom leave a remainder. Stop closes the socket and **resolves rather than rejects**: the tokens already on screen are the reply, so the turn settles with its text instead of an error note. The cut-off notice is unchanged. gzip 4798 → 5155 (+357). Five gates, each control run separately: always-scroll → `the page yanked a reader who had scrolled back`; never-scroll → `a reader at the bottom stopped following the stream`; append instead of insertBefore → `the streaming tail is not last`; stop-rejects → `a user stop rendered an error`; and a typed budget spent inside the think block must name the typed number, not usage — the existing arm drove the cap through the server with the box empty, so `cap ?? usage` was only ever tested on one operand. **The DOM stub had no `insertBefore` at all**, so the page threw a TypeError and the tests failed as "the page threw" rather than as a wrong render; it was given real ordering semantics rather than an `appendChild` alias, which would have hidden exactly the tail-position bug one control produces. No bench entry: nothing on the hot path.
- **default flip** — **the assistant turn renders markdown.** Headings, paragraphs, lists, links and fenced code with a copy button, on top of the inline bold/italic/code the page already had. Still `createElement`/`createTextNode` only, and no parser library — ~60 lines of grammar against marked's 30-40 KB gzipped on a 3.9 KB page. Fences are split off **first**, so a `#` or `-` inside a code block is never a heading or a bullet, and the pattern ends `` (?:```|$) `` so a half-arrived block renders as code while it streams rather than flipping from paragraph to code when the closer lands. **`createElement` closed attribute breakout; it does not close `javascript:`** — `a.href = "javascript:…"` is a live handler however the node was built, so links are allow-listed to http/https/mailto/relative and anything else renders as its own text. gzip 3909 → 4798 (+889). Three gates, each control run separately: block grammar removed → `<h2> missing`; allow-list removed → `a javascript: href became a link`; closing fence required → `an open fence rendered as prose`. An existing gate caught a real regression on the way — a second `prefers-reduced-motion` block for the copy button shadowed the caret's, so the two are now one block. No bench entry: nothing on the hot path.
- **error** — **a method added to the engine seam is missing from `DataParallelEngine`, and this is the second instance.** `limits` 400-ed every Claude Code turn under `--devices`; `room_for` **500-ed every request that omitted `max_tokens`**. The red output named test *doubles* (`_PromptKeyedEngine` in `tests/test_api_sdk.py`, 16 failures) — loud, harmless, and in a different file from the defect: a `getattr(engine, "room_for", …)` fallback in the route would have silenced it and left the 500 shipping. **One arm per method cannot catch the next one**: `test_the_clamp_survives_the_data_parallel_wrapper` is a good test written for the `limits` defect and it passed throughout this one. Gate is `test_every_engine_the_routes_accept_implements_what_they_call`, which **derives** the required set from the route modules' own source (`engine.<name>` plus the `getattr(engine, "<name>")` form) instead of carrying a list, so a route that starts calling something new extends the requirement with nobody adding an arm — today `submit take peek stop_text logprobs stats room_for limits`. **Two controls, both red for the reason under test:** dropping `room_for` from the wrapper names it, and a call to a method that exists nowhere names `brand_new_seam_call` — the second is what shows the gate catches the class and not the two known instances. **The gate was wrong twice before it was right:** `hasattr(Engine, "limits")` is **False** (assigned at `engine.py:358` in `__init__`) while the wrapper declares a `@property`, so a class-level probe failed on the correct implementation and passed on the one with the history — it builds instances now; and the regex demanded `_thread`, which appears only in a `server.py:236` comment explaining why `/health` deliberately does NOT check loop liveness. The derived set is asserted non-empty and to contain the 8 names, since a regex matching nothing would pass every implementation. **Still open on this seam:** `/health` cannot check loop liveness under `--devices` at all — the only signal is `Engine._thread`, so health reports `ok` from `stats()` alone with every replica's loop dead. [errors/2026-09-06-a-seam-method-missing-from-the-wrapper.md](docs/experience/errors/2026-09-06-a-seam-method-missing-from-the-wrapper.md)
- **default flip** — **an omitted `max_tokens` now means the context remainder, not a flat 512.** ckl on the V100 endpoint: the default should be the ceiling, not a flat cap. Two independent caps produced the same "replies are cut" symptom, one per side of the wire: the chat and Responses routes defaulted an omitted cap to **512** so any longer answer ended at `finish_reason=length`, and the demo page sent `max_tokens` **unconditionally** (`main.ts:30`, `value="512"` in the markup) so the server half was unobservable from it — tilerl-25 fixed the page in #194. Server side, `Engine.room_for(prompt_tokens)` returns the two ceilings `submit` already enforces, `min(max_total_tokens − prompt, BLOCK_TOKENS × usable_blocks − prompt − width + 1)` floored at 0, so neither route re-derives them; `by_pool` is the term a caller would omit and carries both `usable_blocks` (net of the captured tick's pad row) and the `width − 1` drafts a verify tick materializes. **Tight on both terms of the `min`, at a 40-token prompt:** `blocks=256 max_ctx=512` gives 472 and refuses +1 with `exceeds max_total_tokens (512)`; `blocks=8 max_ctx=4096` gives 88 and refuses +1 with `exceeds KV pool capacity`. `/v1/messages` is unchanged — Anthropic declares the field required, so it has no omitted case. **The seam widening exposed a live defect:** `DataParallelEngine` did not forward `room_for`, so `serve --devices` **500-ed on every request that omitted the cap** — the same class as the missing `limits` that 400-ed every Claude Code turn, one route later; fixed at `parallel.py:95-98` with `min` across replicas for the same reason `limits` takes the min. Negative control: dropping the forwarder gives `{'plain/omitted': 200, 'wrapped/omitted': 500}`, red on that assertion alone. The `_ScriptedEngine` double returns a flat **64**, deliberately not the real formula, since re-deriving it would let a broken `Engine.room_for` pass all 40 SDK arms. **413 passed, 14 skipped** on a clean tree — the first suite run was discarded for overlapping the control's mutation of `parallel.py`. No perf claim: this is admission arithmetic. Not yet exercised against the live V100 server; 25 checks the end-to-end arm after the redeploy. [wins/2026-09-06-an-omitted-max-tokens-means-the-context-remainder.md](docs/experience/wins/2026-09-06-an-omitted-max-tokens-means-the-context-remainder.md)
- **reject** — **the SSD tier is REJECTED on the serve path: 1.652x worse per turn at 12 sessions, and it served 0 of 132 offers, so it has no credit side to pay for that.** Closes #152's criterion — the tier ships only if wall clock per turn is not worse at the session count where HBM overflows. Six cells, two arms, **one server process per arm** (a first-position JIT has produced a clean false table here before), `--max-ctx 49152 --slots 3`, 12 conversations x 3 growing turns, `compiles` **0 in all 132 rows and asserted, not hoped for**. Per turn, off → on: 2 sessions 1.447 → 1.582 (1.093x), 8 sessions 1.631 → 1.682 (1.031x), 12 sessions **1.971 → 3.256 (1.652x)**. **Only the third is a difference.** Welch on 36 turns/arm at 12: diff 1.285 s, se 0.331, **t = 3.88**, 95% CI on the ratio **[1.32, 1.98]x**, medians harsher at 1.562 → 3.078 = 1.971x. The other two are **not** differences — t = 0.53 and t = 0.22, CIs spanning zero — and quoted as point estimates they would have read as "a small penalty that grows with load", a story wrong in shape and not just magnitude. **Both thresholds, because they are not the same threshold:** the block pool saturates between 2 and 8 (28.4% → **99.8%** → 99.5%), so HBM overflow is at **8**, while the tier's byte budget starts thrashing between 8 and 12 (`ssd_evictions` 0 → 10 → **79**). At 8 — the session count the criterion actually names — the tier is **1.031x, indistinguishable from free**. So the 1.65x is not the cost of spilling under HBM pressure; it is the cost of spilling into a full tier that then discards the entries. Had I run 12 alone the verdict would have been right and its reason wrong. **Why it can only cost:** `ssd_hits` is **0** across all 132 rows, which is not a discovery — `AGENTS.md:162` already says "today's store publishes and never serves", the reason the RL path runs `NoPrefixStore()`. Checked before claiming a mechanism. A hit rate of 0 means every measurement is pure cost. **The reject is narrow:** it convicts this tier, whose load path is unreachable, not the idea; the block-granular store changes the credit side and must be re-measured rather than inheriting this verdict. **Instrument defect found in my own probe:** `prefix_entries` is not a key the engine emits, and `.get(..., 0)` returned a plausible 0 that I briefly read as "the store holds nothing" — a claim about the system inferred from my own typo. The real keys are `prefix_hits`/`_misses`/`_published`/`_evictions`/`_state_bytes`/`_state_bytes_budget`/`_demoted`; the threshold argument rests on `prefix_evictions` and `pool_used_blocks`, which are real. Second time today a `.get` default stood in for data. Limitations: serial (`--max-batch 1`), three turns to ~8.4k prompt tokens, one pool size — and the 8-session null is a failure to detect a difference, not evidence of none, its CI admitting +0.50 s = 31% of the off mean. [errors/2026-09-06-the-ssd-tier-is-165x-worse-at-12-sessions.md](docs/experience/errors/2026-09-06-the-ssd-tier-is-165x-worse-at-12-sessions.md)
- **bench** — **one GRPO step is 54.4% backward and 42.9% decode ticks, so the next RL lever is backward, not the rollout — and the OOM on the way there killed the obvious wrong fix.** 27's step-decomposition ask, at `qwen38-27b` group 8 gen 1024 `--blocks 2304` micro 1 LoRA-16, warm means of 2 steps on H20 card 6. **Three of the six buckets already existed and had never been read at a real recipe**: `rl_step` writes `backward_secs`/`optimizer_secs` into a `timings` dict and `grpo_loop` seeds it with `rollout_secs`; only the rollout was undivided, because `_drain` is `engine.step()` in a loop. `scripts/prof_grpo_step.py` reimplements just the rollout and attributes each tick by **which forward counter moved**, calling the real `rl_step` so backward and optimizer are the shipped path. Step **131.579 s** = backward **71.529 (54.4%)** + rollout 59.965 (45.6%, of which decode 56.484 over 1023 ticks = **55.21 ms/tick** and **6.902 ms/row-token** at B=8, mixed 3.181, prefill 0.275, unattributed 0.025) + optimizer 0.085 (0.06%) + reward ~0. **The decomposition closes to 0.0006 s and 0 ticks were unexplained.** Backward is **1.193x the entire rollout**, against a decode path that has already had graph capture, the recapture waiver and the cast work spent on it. Cold step 0 is 183.62 s, 1.396x warm, all of it backward (115.1 → 71.5) where `linear_fp4_bwd`/`gemm_tn` compile. **Sync convention measured, not assumed:** a `cuda.synchronize()` per tick costs **37 µs/tick, 0.028% of the step** — negligible at a 55 ms tick and NOT transferable, since the launch-floor entry measured 10.10 vs 21.23 µs on one kernel from convention alone; subtract it before comparing against an unsynced number. **Two OOMs, and the first fix was wrong in a recordable way:** at gen 4096 the failing allocation was **1.69 GiB in `reference.py:418`**; cutting the pool `--blocks` 4096→2304 freed **3.63 GiB** and OOMed at the same 1.69 GiB with **0.36 GiB MORE in use** — the freed pool never became headroom, so the pool was never the binding term and "reduce the KV pool" is the plausible wrong fix. What binds is the training forward materializing a **T² attention matrix per full-attn layer**: 24 heads × 4352² × f32 = **1.69 GiB each, matching the allocation exactly**, quadratic in generated length. **Corrected 2026-09-06: the peak is 5.08 GiB (3 live in forward) / 10.16 GiB (6 in backward), not × 16 layers = 27.1 GiB** — `autograd.py:149-166` saves q/k/v and never `att`, so each matrix dies when its call returns; measured by refcount death through a `TorchDispatchMode`. The OOM is real, the per-layer attribution was not, and **a per-call size times a layer count is not a live-bytes figure**. **This bounds the rollout cap from the training side**: the run-3 recipe's 4096 is not reachable through this path on one 95 GiB card at group 8, and needs the matrix not materialized rather than a bigger card. **A memory hypothesis needs the arm where the freed bytes fail to appear.** Limitations: gen 1024 not 4096 (and backward carries the quadratic term, so 54.4% is a floor for longer generations); 2 warm steps; `micro=1` with LoRA so `acc` holds only adapter grads, not the 50.1 GiB full-parameter path; the rollout is the TRAINING path (dense `_training_kv`, no draft plane) so 55.21 ms/tick is not a serving tick; measured on a16ff9c before #188, whose changed branch was unreachable here (no draft ⇒ every tick a full on-grid B=8 W=1, 0 capture-failure lines). [wins/2026-09-06-one-grpo-step-is-54-percent-backward.md](docs/experience/wins/2026-09-06-one-grpo-step-is-54-percent-backward.md)

- **default flip** — **the chat page is warm paper with one terracotta accent, and its budget box defaults to empty.** Restyle per ckl, who asked for a designed, low-saturation page in Claude's style: off-white #faf9f7 ground, warm near-black text, #bd5d3a accent on the send button and links, serif on the wordmark only, 16px/1.6, 720px column, 12px radii, no shadows; the assistant speaks on the paper and only the user's turn is a card. The page was dark-only before — it now ships **both** schemes, which is where the bytes went: raw 9038 → 10466, **gzip 3353 → 3909 (+556, +16.6%)**. Second change, user-visible: the budget box is empty by default and the ask **omits `max_tokens`** rather than sending the page's own 512, so the server's context remainder decides — a page that keeps sending 512 gets 512 and v100's server change does nothing. Two new gates: every `var(--x)` must resolve in the bare `:root` (a colour defined only inside the dark block renders as nothing in light mode and passes every other test), and an empty box must send no `max_tokens` key with a typed-number arm as its control. No bench entry: no runtime path changes.
- **error** — **the `max_pending=32` cap is not the queue that binds: arrival peaks at 4 in flight, and the device is 5.08x oversubscribed where no counter can see it.** Closes the arrival-rate half the [ssd_save_ms entry](docs/experience/errors/2026-09-06-ssd-save-ms-is-page-cache-time.md) left open. Measured on H20 card 6, 12 conversations x 3 turns served serially, 72 offers, 0 poll gaps: **0.926-0.952 offers/s**, `ssd_pending` peak **4** against a cap of 32, **0 refusals**. **Two measured numbers contradicted and neither shipped until it resolved:** arrival 0.95/s against a drain of 0.748/s is 1.27x sustained for 74 s, which should have filled the queue and started refusing. Both candidates tilerl-27 named were real and they compound. **(a) The drain rate was measured on the wrong entry.** 1337 ms was a 320.6 MiB entry; under this workload the mean is **585.0 MiB** (435.3 `.kv` + 149.6 `.st`), **1.82x**, and an entries-per-second rate cannot carry between workloads when what binds is bytes. **(b) What empties `_pending` is not the device.** `_flush_loop` pops an entry when `torch.save` RETURNS, which is a page-cache accept: 164 ms per 292.5 MiB save = **~1784 MiB/s**, against **541.5 MiB/s** of arrival, so the queue drains 3.3x faster than it fills. 240 MiB/s describes the device and was never the queue's service rate. The device counter (`/proc/diskstats` vda2) says **106.5 MiB/s**, so the real number is **5.08x oversubscription at the device**, absorbed by 386 GiB of allowed dirty pages (`dirty_ratio=20` of 1928 GiB) — a full 32-deep queue is 18.3 GiB, **4.7%** of that. **Verdict: the cap is not harmful and not load-bearing**; what would bind first is `max_bytes` (37 of 72 offers evicted in the run), 660 entries before dirty-page throttling. Byte accounting closes: `saves` 144 for 72 offers = exactly 2.0, and 35 surviving + 37 evicted = 72. **My own burst metric was an artifact and I reported it to a peer before catching it:** max(offers in one poll)/interval read **7.95/s at `--interval 0.25` and 19.54/s at 0.05**, both exactly (1 or 2)/interval, on a workload whose mean interarrival is 1.07 s — **21x the poll window**. A per-poll rate climbs without bound as the poll shrinks and reads as a discovered burst. Replaced with a count in a fixed wall-clock window, invariant across both intervals (**3 / 12 / 54** at 1 s / 5 s / 42.8 s, identical) and asserted so in the selfcheck; the artifact is still printed, labelled, so the next reader sweeping `--interval` watches it move while the counts hold. **54 was almost published as a required queue depth** — it assumes zero drain during the window, so it is a bound; the measured depth is 4. **Two complete 12-session runs produced zero numbers by crashing AFTER collecting everything:** an `HTTPError` propagating instead of breaking (and `urllib`'s `str()` is only `HTTP Error 400: Bad Request`, discarding the body that named the real `--max-ctx` limit), then `self._stop` shadowing `Thread._stop` so `join()` raised `'Event' object is not callable` after all 3 turns finished. The selfcheck passed both times because it built the sampler with `__new__` and never called `start`/`stop`/`join` — the three methods carrying the bug; it now drives the real lifecycle, verified by reintroducing the shadow and confirming the control went red *inside `join()`*. **A probe's measurement is not delivered until teardown returns.** Also caught by asserting rather than assuming: `_sectors()` parses a file this Mac lacks, and my first field assertion named field 6 as sectors-read when it is field 5 — sampling 6 would have mixed reads into the write rate and still looked plausible. Limitations stated: serial so B=1 throughout (concurrency would raise arrival and, per tilerl-48, cross the sm90 mma8/WGMMA16 dispatch boundary), one workload shape, and the device counter is whole-disk with a measured 0.027 MiB/s idle baseline. [errors/2026-09-06-the-max-pending-cap-is-not-the-queue-that-binds.md](docs/experience/errors/2026-09-06-the-max-pending-cap-is-not-the-queue-that-binds.md)

- **fix** — **the last route to a CUDA graph capture inside a live request is closed.** `precapture` enumerates buckets only (`{1,2,4}` at `max_batch=4`), but `_run_decode_graph`'s pad-row fallback set `B = n` — a 3-row tick then asked for the off-grid key `(3, 1)` and `_graph_for` captured it on the spot, under exactly the pool pressure that had removed the pad row. It now returns False and the tick runs eager: ~10x on one tick against a capture measured at **19 s for 8 graphs warm, 208 s cold**, and the two a warmup missed costing **14.0 s and 11.7 s** on their own requests (`wins/2026-09-02-precapture-the-decode-graphs.md`). Not permanent — `_pad_slot` stays None, so the next tick retries and picks the graph back up. Reachability is narrow (`num_slots == max_batch` and the retry also failing); shipped as a correctness hole, not a perf win. Gate spies on `_graph_for` so it runs on cpu; the negative control names the off-grid key. [errors/2026-09-06-off-grid-graph-key-captures-mid-request.md](docs/experience/errors/2026-09-06-off-grid-graph-key-captures-mid-request.md)
- **reject** — **the 112 f32→f16 casts cost 0.349 ms/token, and the reason they do not hide is stream order, not occupancy — a mechanism I published and then had to retract.** Task #21's last live descendant. The OPEN.md row said "blocked on capacity": true of `prof_decode_budget.py`, which loads a 19 GB model against 4.4 GB free, and **not of the question** — a cast and its GEMV are two kernels and one 6144×5120 f16 weight is 60 MB, so three captured graphs at the real widths answer it in **244 MiB**. 112 casts alone 0.366 ms, 112 GEMVs alone 9.393, the pairs 9.739: the cast **adds 0.346**, 3.09 µs each against 3.27 alone. **The 6% that looked hidden is not a measurement** — re-running the first arm *last*, same process, same casts, gave 0.309 against 0.366, an **18.4% same-arm spread, 3.3x the 5.6% between arms**, so it is reported unresolvable rather than as a small effect. A 0/28/56/112/224/448 count sweep gives **3.12 µs marginal per cast** and a 13.1 µs empty replay, so 112 × 3.12 = **0.349 ms/token** and the previously published 0.366 was 5% high for the ordinary reason a bound is high. Against the served token: **1.33% at ctx 8192**, 0.93% at 16384, 0.54% at 32768. **Then the mechanism, tested rather than asserted:** I wrote that the cast does not hide because the GEMV has no idle SMs. Untested, and wrong — kernels issued into one stream run in issue order, so a cast placed after a GEMV cannot overlap at **any** occupancy. Putting the casts on a **second stream** inside one captured graph (GEMVs consuming pre-cast X, so no data dependency forces the order) recovers **66%** of the add: 9.496 against 9.719, with the GEMV arm re-run last reading 9.380 vs 9.379 — a **0.0% same-arm spread, effect 993x the noise**. The GPU had room all along. **An additive result names no mechanism**: a serialization has at least two candidate causes, software ordering and hardware saturation, and an experiment varying neither cannot choose. **The reject stands regardless** — the shipped decode graph is one stream, so it pays the full 0.339 ms, and `backend.py:663` says the pre-packed f16 X **replaces** an in-kernel per-block cvt worth 4.2x at M=32 and 1.1-1.45x at M=1, so there is no no-conversion arm and "the casts may be removable" had the sign wrong. What it opens: a side-stream cast is a real 0.22 ms/token lever needing no dtype change — 0.85% of a token for a second stream inside a captured graph, whose capture-order and event-dependency surface is far larger than the win. Not pursued, recorded as the branch. Limitations stated: dense f16 matmul rather than the fp4 kernel (occupancy turned out not to be the binding variable, so the substitution does not touch the serial figure; it does bound the 66%), and 4 weights per shape cycled, asserted larger than 4× L2. [errors/2026-09-06-the-casts-do-not-hide-and-are-not-worth-removing.md](docs/experience/errors/2026-09-06-the-casts-do-not-hide-and-are-not-worth-removing.md)

- **phase exit** — **the gated-delta layer runs under context parallelism.** A recurrent layer cannot split by sequence the way full attention can: a chunk needs the state the chunk before it produced, and under zigzag that chunk is on another rank. Two cross-rank dependencies, both keyed by **chunk id and never by rank** — the all-gather returns rank order and only the ids carry sequence order, so `affine_prefix_scan` ships `(A, B, id)` and sorts on the id, and `cp_halo` builds an id→(rank, index) owner map. **Dropping the conv halo corrupts the whole chunk, not its first rows**, because the bad k/v feed the recurrence: the gate's own `--no-halo` arm gives chunk 3 **1.2e+00** on the first 3 rows and **5.3e-01** on everything after them, chunk 2 2.9e-01 / **7.2e-01** — the tail worse than the boundary, which is why a spot check of the boundary rows sees the smaller error. `_gdn_cp` does **not** round the composed prefix: each backend arm marshals its own operand (the CPU reference takes f32, sm90's WY scan takes bf16 and says so at `backend.py:1243`), and rounding in the caller diverged from the sequential path at **rel 3.0e-05 against 8.8e-07**. Three gates, all rc=0: `gdn_world2` (out 3.2e-07, state 1.4e-07), `gdn_halo_world2` (0.0e+00 on all four chunks, expected rather than suspicious — with the right left context the conv inputs are bit-identical), `cp_model_world2` (a split forward matches the unsplit one on both layer kinds, logits rel 8.8e-07 / 9.7e-07). pytest 406 passed / 14 skipped. **Every number is the CPU arm; cp=2 only; forward and backward through the gates and no training run**, so no loss curve, no step time and no throughput claim for CP. Two figures in the first draft of the entry were wrong in a way review would not have caught: the halo cost was **inherited from a source comment rather than measured** (0.61/0.79, against the 1.2e+00/5.3e-01 the arm actually prints), and the indexing trap I described as `parts[r]` **is not what the code does** — both replaced by what a run and a read say. **None of the three new gates was in CI and the step's floor could not see it**: `ci.yml` listed six paths by hand, skipped absent ones with `[ -f ] || continue`, and floored at `ran > 0` — so 6 of 9 passed exactly as 9 of 9 would, and this entry would have landed on gates nobody runs. Now globbed with the skip removed and the floor at `>= 9`, both arms exercised. **An op gate cannot see a defect in the layer that calls it**: three gates held the recurrence to 1e-7 while the model's residual was wrong by its own magnitude, because none of them reaches `_add_via` (fixed in #182). [wins/2026-09-06-gdn-under-context-parallelism.md](docs/experience/wins/2026-09-06-gdn-under-context-parallelism.md)
- **error** — **`ssd_save_ms` is page-cache time, the durable save is 1337 ms on the pod, and three conclusions drawn on this Mac inverted there.** `kv_cache.py:518` wraps `torch.save` with no `fsync`, so the timer stops when the kernel accepts the bytes: on the pod's `/work` one 320.6 MiB entry reads **273 ms as timed and 1337 ms durable, 5.75x**. The **641.8** cited in five places was this Mac, whose volume writes **26x** faster (4865 vs **185 MiB/s** sequential, medians of 3 — one sample read 8954 then 4742, so a single rate measurement is not a rate). **Every conclusion the Mac gave was wrong for the pod, and one had a patch attached:** fsync adds nothing (0.98x) → 5.75x; `torch.save` is 11.6x a raw write so the tier is *not* disk-bound → the raw arm is the **slowest** of five there (1708 ms) and `save+fsync` is **0.81x** it, i.e. disk-bound at 187.6 MiB/s = 1.02x its own volume rate; and serialize-to-RAM-then-one-write at **122 vs 537 ms, a 5.24x win, same format, no dtype change** → **1430 vs 1337, 7% slower** than what ships. That third arm was the one I would have opened a PR on. Also checked before claiming it: the two paths are **not** byte-identical (4,196,133 vs 4,196,125 — zip metadata), though `torch.load` returns equal tensors, and "byte-identical so it's a safe swap" was the sentence about to be written. Durable drain is **240 MiB/s**, so a full `max_pending=32` queue takes **42.8 s** against 20.5 at 641.8 — understated 2.1x, in the direction that makes the cap look safe. **The cap is left unchanged and still unsized**: a drain rate with no arrival rate is a bound set without measuring what it bounds, and nothing in the tier reports offers/second. The OOM framing in the cap's comment is dropped as measurably wrong: 32 × 320.6 MiB = 10.0 GiB against 1928 GiB total / 1473 available. **A storage measurement does not transfer between hosts and the direction of the error is not predictable** — measure the host ratio first, and run the arm that would change code on the target before writing it up. [errors/2026-09-06-ssd-save-ms-is-page-cache-time.md](docs/experience/errors/2026-09-06-ssd-save-ms-is-page-cache-time.md)

- **fix** — **`init_tp` gave a cp-only mesh `tp_world = world`, so the `o_proj` residual all-reduced across the CP ranks.** The `else` branch at `backend.py:193` (and its twin in `testing.py`) inherited the world whenever `tp_groups` was absent — correct for its one original caller, the bare `init_tp(world, rank)` of the TP gates, which means "the whole world is one TP group", and wrong the moment a second axis owns the world. `_add_via` then summed across two **CP** ranks: correct operands, corrupted sum, **every shape local, so nothing looked wrong anywhere** — the only symptom was the number at the end. No caller on main passes `cp_groups` yet, so nothing shipped is currently wrong; the branch does, and the trap is in the shipped code. **Four cells, because two defects were live at once** (`cp_model_world2.py`, cp=2, rank 0 logits rel, gate 1e-5, `__pycache__` cleared between runs): inherit+bf16 prefix **1.2e+00**, inherit+f32 **1.2e+00**, `tp_world=1`+bf16 **3.0e-05**, `tp_world=1`+f32 **8.8e-07**. **The all-reduce hides the rounding entirely** — rows 1 and 2 are identical, so a run that fixed only the dtype would have shown zero movement and read as "not the cause" — and **fixing only the all-reduce lands 3x over the gate**, which is the number that reads as "CP is nearly right, loosen the tolerance". The second defect is in `_gdn_cp`, which does not exist on main, and ships with the GDN-under-CP branch. The new assertion goes in `tests/cp_world2.py` rather than only the model gate because that gate is an **op** gate — it calls `linear_attn_chunk` and never reaches `_add_via`, so it could not have seen this by its own numbers; reverted, it fails with `cp-only mesh set tp_world=2: the residual add will all-reduce across the CP ranks`. Five world gates rc=0 on a clean `2a15b5e` worktree. The 1e-5 and the 8.8e-07 are **CPU-arm** numbers; on sm90 the WY scan rounds the composed prefix on entry, same as the sequential path at every `_WY_CHUNK` boundary, and the CP gate's 1e-5 has not been run there. **A probe that validates one arm is not a result about the mechanism**: I settled the bf16 question on the CPU arm, where `reference.gdn_chunk_core` takes `_f32(state)`, and `_gdn_wy_core` passes `_bf16(state)` with a comment at `backend.py:1243` saying the gemm operand rounds on entry either way — the same probe answers the opposite there. [errors/2026-09-06-a-cp-only-mesh-inherited-the-world-as-tp.md](docs/experience/errors/2026-09-06-a-cp-only-mesh-inherited-the-world-as-tp.md)
- **fix** — **the sandbox-confinement gate had no coverage: both arms parsed zero tool calls, so the suite's one isolation test could not fail.** Reported by tilerl-25 as a control that does not fire on clean `a08b1c2`. Cause: Claude Code sends `thinking: adaptive`, so the 27B template opens `<think>` **in the prompt**, and `split_think(opened=True)` (`prompt.py:131`) prepends `<think>` to a reply with no closer — routing the **entire** reply to reasoning and leaving `text` empty, so `messages.py:217`'s `_parse_tool_calls(text, req.tools)` got `''`. Everything reported success while this happened: `is_error: false`, `returncode: 0`, `subtype: "success"`, `stop_reason: "end_turn"`, `num_turns: 2`, `permission_denials: []`, two records served. **Two wrong causes died on the way:** `{**os.environ}` at `rollout.py:176` leaks the spawning session's `ANTHROPIC_MODEL` into the child, which printed `[claude-code:unrecognized_model]` — real, alarming, and refuted by unsetting it alone (error gone, file still unwritten); and "the CLI never dispatched" was the symptom one level up, settled by instrumenting 25's suggested seam, which showed `_parse_tool_calls` called twice with 29 tools and `text_head: ""` both times. Fixture now emits `"</think>" + render_tool_call(...)`, the shape a model under this template actually produces. **Both arms now parse 1 call** (sandboxed: blocked, file absent; unsandboxed: file written) against **0 and 0** before. **A test whose fixture bypasses a production transform is green on a path nobody ships** — and **an error in stderr is a candidate, not a cause**: vary the suspect alone before writing it down. **Dated by execution:** reading `prompt.py` out of git at three revisions and running the live call site's own argument, the escape survived to the parser at `0dfe018` and stopped at `eccac47` (#151, which added `opened=` to `strip_think`), so the window is **09:37:32 → 16:29:18 +0800 = 6.9 h** with no sandbox coverage. The test predates that by four days (`a6a88b6`); #151's change was correct and simply did not carry the fixture along with the invariant it introduced. **Three sessions restated the failure without opening it** — "environmental on this Mac" (27, in #159's body and twice more), "pre-existing on clean main" (48), "the negative control does not fire" (25) — all true, none a cause, and each restatement made the next reader less likely to look; **a failing test described the same way by N people is one observation, not N.** The env leak is a reproducibility defect and stays open separately. [errors/2026-09-06-the-sandbox-control-was-swallowed-by-think.md](docs/experience/errors/2026-09-06-the-sandbox-control-was-swallowed-by-think.md)
- **error** — **three live stop-sequence arms could never fire, and it took three live runs to get one pass.** On the V100 run of `753da30` all three SKIPPED with "reply too short to cut inside", so #174 shipped with zero automated coverage against real weights; 27 established the behaviour by manual probes instead. **The skip was guaranteed by construction** — the arms cut their stop from the reply to a prompt demanding a one-word answer, so no deployment would ever have fixed it. Two things hid it: the skip printed no number, so it read as a property of the run rather than of the arm, and **a proportional skip floor cannot see a dead arm** (it passed correctly at 3/14, because a bound on how many arms skip says nothing about whether one can ever run). **Then the control found a second defect in my own arms: two of three PASSED on a stop of `'/thi'`** — the byte tokenizer has no `<think>` token, so the reply carries a literal `</think>` and the slice cut the stop out of that markup; the chat arms asserted truncation at the closer, which tests nothing, while the messages arm failed correctly because the engine's reasoning gate refuses to match before it. The passing arms were the wrong ones. **The live re-run then failed a third way**, named by the new skip message: `"0 chars of prose"` and `AssertionError: max_tokens` — thinking is on by default and `max_tokens` went inside `<think>`, so the reply was reasoning and no prose. Fixed thinking-off rather than with a larger cap, which would have passed that run and broken the first time the model thought longer. Third run, `c8ca8d2` against `753da30`: **rc 0, 14/14, 0 skipped.** Probe-only change to a dev script, no runtime change. [errors/2026-09-06-three-live-arms-skipped-by-construction.md](docs/experience/errors/2026-09-06-three-live-arms-skipped-by-construction.md)
- **phase exit** — **the chat page is a build, not a 23,898-character Python string, and it talks WebSocket.** `_CHAT_UI` held markup, CSS, a markdown renderer and a streaming state machine in one constant, and two failures shipped out of it in three days because Python only measured that string's length. TypeScript under `web/`, built to `src/tilerl/static/`: **28,311 → 9,038 raw bytes, 9,814 → 3,471 gzipped**, `ui_assets.py` 755 → 97 lines. The transport is a new `/ws/chat`, because the page SENDS as well as reads and EventSource is receive-only. `_deltas` yields `(kind, payload, tokens)` and both routes frame it, so the think-split, the partial-closer holdback and — after the rebase onto #174 — the stop cut exist once; `render.ts` builds DOM nodes, so the attribute-breakout class is absent rather than gated. **Effect dropped on measurement**: 182.79 KB of runtime against a 3.07 KB stub probe, 98.3% of the bundle. **Two gates caught what a green suite could not**: `TestClient.websocket_connect` fakes the transport, so the WS suite passes with no ws library while the deployed route 404s — the assertion for that then went red on CI, which runs `uv sync --dev` and installs no extras, so `websockets>=17.1` is declared in the `dev` group too. **My first control for the stop fold passed because it ran the wrong files**: deleting the cut left `test_server.py` + `test_chat_ui.py` green at 48, while `test_api_sdk.py` shows each half failing alone (whole cut → `The answer is`, holdback alone → `The answer `). 404 passed, 14 skipped, ruff clean. **`serve_v100.sh` needs `uv sync --extra server`**. Served on the V100 at `363de2a`: **46.5 tok/s** at 1500 tokens (ttft 0.57 s, finish `length` — the truncation branch on real weights) and **50.4 tok/s** at 491 (ttft 0.78 s, finish `stop`), client-side timing, one request each. Coalescing is **1.84 and 1.93 tokens per frame** against the 1.83 the SSE path measured, which resolves a 3.5 chars/frame reading from a 74-character reasoning block as the short-block case rather than a second regime. The SSE page's 34.0 tok/s stands beside these without a ratio — different day, card state and harness, and no one has run both arms in one session. [wins/2026-09-06-the-chat-page-becomes-a-build.md](docs/experience/wins/2026-09-06-the-chat-page-becomes-a-build.md)
- **fix** — **a rollout child inherited all 20 of this machine's `ANTHROPIC_*`/`CLAUDE*` variables, two of them a live channel back into the spawning agent session.** `rollout.py:176` built the subprocess env as `{**os.environ, ...}`, so a child's behaviour depended on which session started it — an agent machine's `ANTHROPIC_MODEL` reached the CLI and it printed `[claude-code:unrecognized_model]`. **Enumerated rather than listed from memory:** the OPEN.md row named "ANTHROPIC_MODEL and the four sibling model vars", the five visible in that error; `env | grep` reports **20**, and two are `CLAUDE_CODE_MESSAGING_SOCKET` and `_TOKEN` — this session's cross-session address and credential, handed to a sandboxed rollout. Nothing was observed using them; the point is that an allow-list of five remembered names leaves both in place. Scrubbed by **prefix** (`ANTHROPIC_`, `CLAUDE`), then the four the rollout needs are set. Gated on the env dict passed to `subprocess.run`, since the CLI's own `CLAUDE_CODE_SUBPROCESS_ENV_SCRUB` acts one level further in and is unobservable from here; an unrelated `TILERL_KEEP_ME` must survive, so an over-broad scrub is red too. Negative control restores `{**os.environ, ...}` and fails naming all four planted vars. **An allow-list of the names you remember is not a scrub.** [errors/2026-09-06-the-sandbox-control-was-swallowed-by-think.md](docs/experience/errors/2026-09-06-the-sandbox-control-was-swallowed-by-think.md)

- **default flip** — **`--micro` defaults to 1, not 0**, and `pod_sync.sh` stops wiping `runs/`. The old default puts all 8 group rows through one backward: measured on one H20 at group 8 / cap 2048 it peaks **88.21 GiB and OOMs on step 2**, against **44.55 GiB and 3/3 steps** at 1 — a 1.98x difference in peak, so the default was the arm that does not fit the production shape. It costs 2.75x on the backward at the same width (22.228 vs 8.070 s), which is the price of fitting. Both shipped recipes already set 1, so this changes only hand-typed runs; `test_rl.py:253` passes 0/1/3 and asserts identical weights to 1e-6. Second half: the sync's `find . -mindepth 1 -delete` now carries `! -path './runs' ! -path './runs/*'` — **not** `-path ./runs -prune -o -delete`, since `-delete` implies `-depth` and that disables `-prune`. **The two `find`s diverge there and my first table reported one of them as the mechanism's consequence:** GNU 4.9.0 (pod, ubuntu CI) refuses outright — rc=1, nothing deleted, printing `the -delete action automatically turns on -depth, but -prune does nothing when -depth is in effect` — while BSD find accepts it and deletes `runs/` silently, so it reads as protection on a Mac. **My own PR's ubuntu leg caught it:** the test asserted `runs/` was deleted, true on BSD and false on GNU, and it now asserts what holds on both — `-prune` never yields a working exemption. Two compounding errors behind the bad row: the Mac probe ran through a shell function that resolves to **`bfs`, not `find` at all**, and the arm I did run on the pod was the shipped spelling, never the `-prune` one, so "confirmed through the pod's own find" was true of a different row than the one it was attached to. The shipped spelling behaves identically on both implementations, which is what the fix needed. [errors/2026-09-06-the-oom-was-micro-zero.md](docs/experience/errors/2026-09-06-the-oom-was-micro-zero.md)
- **fix** — **a failed stamp wrote a blank commit into `bench-baseline.json`, and the `"unknown"` branch written for that case was unreachable.** `pod_sync.sh:14` was `git rev-parse --short HEAD > .synced_commit 2>/dev/null || true`: the shell truncates the redirect target **before** git runs, so a git failure leaves the file **empty** rather than leaving the previous stamp, and `|| true` swallows the code. `bench_harness._git_commit` then keyed its fallback on `stamp.exists()` — and an empty file exists — so the value read back was `''` and `Gate.check` wrote `{"commit": ""}`, a row that looks provenanced and is not. **It never fired: 0 of 41 baseline rows carry a blank commit** (5 carry the literal `unknown`, which is a pre-stamp row, not this signature), and the stamp is written on the Mac inside the checkout where `rev-parse` does not fail — **found by reading, latent, and the entry claims no more than that.** Worth fixing because it is silent on both sides of the seam and corrupts the one field every perf claim's provenance rests on: a blank `commit` is indistinguishable from a row nobody stamped. Both halves fixed — the write happens only inside `if sha=$(git ...)`, the read is `(... if exists else "") or "unknown"` — and `tests/test_bench_commit_stamp.py` gates each with a **red control running the old spelling**, the shell half extracting the block from `pod_sync.sh` rather than copying it. **One of those controls was itself broken:** my "did the control apply" guard grepped for `or "unknown"` and matched the *comment* I had just written above the return line, so it reported not-applied while applying correctly; now it asserts `count(<the exact return line>) == 1`. **A redirect truncates before the command runs, so `cmd > f || true` yields an empty file, not an unchanged one** — and any `exists()` fallback is then unreachable in exactly the case it was written for. [errors/2026-09-06-a-failed-stamp-wrote-a-blank-commit.md](docs/experience/errors/2026-09-06-a-failed-stamp-wrote-a-blank-commit.md)
- **fix** — **the bench-baseline rescue failed quiet, and quiet meant losing the row it exists to rescue.** `pod_sync.sh` pulls the pod's `bench-baseline.json` rows, wipes the remote checkout, then extracts this tree's tarball over it — and `bench-baseline.json` is tracked, so **step 3, not the wipe, is what loses a pod-raised row**. Simulated all three steps with a row the local tree has never seen: pull working leaves the pod with **2 rows and 140.0**, pull failing silently leaves **1 row and 100.0**, exit status 0 either way. Two halves made it invisible: `baseline.pull()` ran `subprocess.run([str(Path.home() / "bin/pod"), ...])` unguarded, and **`subprocess.run` on a missing executable raises `FileNotFoundError` rather than returning non-zero** — measured with `Path.home()` pointed at an empty dir — while the caller was `... pull >/dev/null 2>&1 || true`, which swallows a raise and a returned 1 identically and discards the traceback naming the cause. `~/bin/pod` on this Mac is a **symlink into another repository**, so it is absent whenever that repo moves. Fixed both: `pull()` catches `OSError` and returns 1 with the path on stderr, and the sync line drops `|| true` so `set -euo pipefail` aborts **before** the wipe; `SKIP_BASELINE_PULL=1` still short-circuits, so the deliberate overwrite is unchanged. Checked that `set -e` fires here at all, since an `||` list's failure only trips it when the failing command is last — rhs exiting 3 stops the script, `SKIP=1` continues, `|| true` appended continues, the third arm being the defect reproduced. `tests/test_baseline_rescue.py` gates both halves and the loss itself, with two red controls; the exception-name control asserts `raised:FileNotFoundError` rather than a bare non-zero exit, because non-zero is also true of an import error, and the shell control's route was verified (`python3` present, fake exits 1) so the missing marker is the pull's failure and not a missing interpreter. **A rescue that fails quiet is worse than no rescue**, because what it protects is exactly what the next step overwrites. [errors/2026-09-06-the-baseline-rescue-failed-quiet.md](docs/experience/errors/2026-09-06-the-baseline-rescue-failed-quiet.md)



- **verdict** — **the GRPO OOM was `micro=0`, not #140's buckets, and the probe's own trace refutes the bucket mechanism rather than merely displacing it.** My snapshot wrote "cap 2048 group 8 does not survive step 1 on one H20" as a capacity fact; review refuted it with MATH run 2 (`0f7006c74ea0`, **45 steps** at that group and cap) and proposed allocator fragmentation from per-step bucket widths. Both of us had missed an operand: `recipes.py:36` gives `grpo-math-27b` **`micro=1`**, my runs took the CLI default **`micro=0`**, and `train.py:137` (`rows = micro if 0 < micro < b else b`) turns that into one backward over all 8 group rows instead of one row at a time. Rerun at `--micro 1`, everything else identical: **3/3 steps, peak 44.55 GiB against 88.21 (1.98x, the 8-rows factor), median 67.3 s/step** — while widths went **256 → 512 → 512** as rollouts grew 174 → 241 → 291 tokens, so **the bucket did widen mid-run and nothing OOMed**; a varying width is not sufficient to cause it. Cost of the setting that fits: **2.75x on the backward at the same width** (22.228 vs 8.070 at width 256). This also restores the comparison the snapshot twice declined: **67.3 s/step median vs P1's 56.88 — 1.18x**, same task, same group, same micro, differing only in cap 2048 vs 256, so the cap costs ~18% at this rollout length. **Run 2's own peak would have answered it in one line and it was never written:** per tilerl-25, run 2 died on SIGTERM at step 45 and `write_manifest` lived only inside `_finish` until `8388cbf`, so no manifest existed — **I published that my sync destroyed it, which was wrong in the direction that made my own error look worse, the one direction that does not get re-checked.** Both fixes land here anyway, and neither is a warning: **`--micro` now defaults to 1** (both shipped recipes already set 1; `test_rl.py:253` passes 0/1/3 explicitly and asserts identical weights to 1e-6, so this flips which arm is default without changing any arm), and **`pod_sync.sh` exempts `runs/`** with `! -path './runs' ! -path './runs/*'` — not the tempting `-path ./runs -prune -o -delete`, because **`-delete` implies `-depth`, which disables `-prune`**, so that spelling deletes `runs/` anyway; measured in a temp tree and again through the pod's own `find`, with the current wipe as a red control. The forward-looking point is that since `8388cbf` a run writes its manifest before the eval arms, so the next killed run does leave one, and any peer's sync would take it. **67.3 is not comparable to run 2's 229.2** — MATH at a 1434-token mean against gsm8k at 291, 4.9x the rollout length; the only valid pair is 67.3 vs 56.88. **A shared shape needs every operand enumerated, not the ones both parties remember** — and a mechanism can be sound and still not be the cause. [errors/2026-09-06-the-oom-was-micro-zero.md](docs/experience/errors/2026-09-06-the-oom-was-micro-zero.md)
- **accept** — **the "~60 µs eager launch floor" cited in five files is 10.1 µs, and the 6x is a timer convention, not an error in one direction.** Measured on the V100 with the gate's own control passing (util n=150, **median 0%, max 2%**, 27,306 MiB resident): `empty` 11.28, `tiny_add` 10.13, `inplace` 7.58 µs, and the element sweep **flat at ~10 µs from 1 to 262,144** before going work-bound at 2^20 (710 → 805 GB/s). So **"regardless of shape" was the correct half** — the half nothing had tested separately — and the magnitude was wrong. **Where the 6x comes from:** `benchkit.timeit` records CUDA events around a loop of N calls and divides by N, so the CPU enqueues call k+1 while the GPU runs call k — amortized, not one launch's latency. Same two kernels, three conventions: amortized 10.10 / 100.21, per-call-sync 21.23 / 120.53, isolated-events 25.60 / 119.81 µs (tiny_add / gemv 1x5120x6144) — **2.1x on a trivial kernel and 1.2x on a real GEMV, the same fixed ~11-15 µs in both, which is what a floor means.** **No convention reaches 60**, so it is not a sync-convention difference and stays unsourced. For #21 the premise survives smaller: **~10% of a 100 µs microbench reading is harness cost, not the ~60% the retired figure implied**, so measuring in-graph first is still right and is a smaller correction. **The probe had never run because its gate tested the wrong quantity** — it refused >1 GiB resident while its own justification said contention, and the V100 permanently holds a 27 GB endpoint at 0% util, which made the OPEN.md item unsatisfiable as written; it now samples `nvidia-smi -lms 200` for 30 s and refuses on median util > 5%, with residency as context. **Two instrument failures caught before publishing:** the first sampler ran in a Python thread and collected **n=2** over a 40 s run (the measurement loop holds the GIL, each `nvidia-smi` costs ~1 s) and its 34% median was as plausibly my own gemv as another process; and the gate's negative control refused **both** arms until I read *which* refusal fired — it was "no samples", because stubbing `sleep` to a no-op left the log at 0 bytes. Fixed control: util=0 passes and reaches the measurement, util=85 refuses on the util message. Sampler's measured cost on the quantity measured: **1.006x**. **A floor quoted without its timer convention is two numbers wearing one label.** **One adjacent question the floor settles and one it does not:** the f32→f16 casts are **launch-bound**, so pro-rating their cost by count is legitimate — widths 5120 / 6144 / 17408 read 9.93 / 9.87 / 9.76 µs at M=1 (17408/5120 = **0.983x against a 3.40x byte ratio**), flat across every width a real cast uses; but the cost of #21's 112 remaining casts (`o_proj` 16, `out_proj` 48, `ab` 48, counted off the checkpoint's `text_config` — 16 full-attention and 48 linear layers, both projections [6144→5120]) first read as **two bounds 1.9x apart**, 0.60 ms/token by count pro-rate and 1.13 by floor×count, **and I published that as an unresolved conflict with a mechanism that was wrong in both halves** (`bk.timeit` is already back-to-back, so 10.1 µs/call *is* the overlapped rate; and the 1.64 ms came from a profile of the **captured graph**, which pays no per-call launch cost — the premise of #21's own instruction, stated three sections up in the same entry and not applied). Measured instead of argued: the same 112 casts at the real widths read **9.76 µs/cast eager against 3.27 in-graph, 2.99x**, the eager arm reproducing the floor and the graph arm sitting *below* the pro-rate. So the bounds were an eager number and an in-graph number, the shipped-path figure is **~0.37 ms/token**, and what stays open is narrower than claimed: whether the real graph, which interleaves these casts with GEMVs, hides part of that. Blocked on capacity rather than a job (5,500 MiB free against a 19 GB model). Also: **an empty result with exit 0 is not an absence** — my first two reads of the model dims printed nothing and returned 0, which reads as "no such keys"; they are under `text_config` and I was querying the top level. **Two numbers that disagree are not automatically an open question:** check whether they were measured under conditions the document already distinguishes. [wins/2026-09-06-the-launch-floor-is-ten-microseconds.md](docs/experience/wins/2026-09-06-the-launch-floor-is-ten-microseconds.md)

- **feat** — **stop sequences work on all three routes, matched on decoded text — and the fix the error entry named cannot work.** That entry proposed `SamplingParams.stop_ids`: stop strings pre-encoded to id tuples, compared against the tail of `req.output` like `end_think_ids`, single-token first through the existing `stop_token_ids` and multi-token after. **Both halves are dead, for one reason: a stop string need not begin at a token boundary** — `"is"` inside `" is 4."` may be carried by a token spelling `" is"`, so the ids differ while the text agrees, and which token carries the first character is not known until generation. Matching therefore happens on decoded text (`stop_texts` on `SamplingParams`, checked in `_commit` after the append), which collapses the single/multi-token split into one path and leaves nothing for the staged order to stage. Contract as settled: generation stops at the token that COMPLETES the match, `completion_ids` keep it, the returned text is cut at the match start, `/v1/messages` reports `stop_reason: "stop_sequence"` plus the matched string, and `/v1/responses` stays `completed` because a stop is not a truncation. The concession is one docstring: the engine was "tokenizer-free" and now takes `Engine(decode=...)` for this alone — `submit` **refuses** `stop_texts` without it rather than accepting a stop that can never fire. **The `k` the entry left open, measured:** `_stop_hit` decodes the last k tokens where k = the longest stop in characters (a token carries at least one character, so k tokens always cover a k-character match), costing **0.55 / 0.99 / 2.64 µs per token at k=3 / 16 / 64** against a 1345 µs tick — 0.04-0.20%; the end-to-end paired delta was **-13 µs/token**, i.e. noise, and is reported as a bound rather than a speedup. **The control is the finding: with the engine's matching deleted, all six route arms still passed**, because `_ScriptedEngine` honours `stop_texts` itself — a canned double that implements the behaviour under test makes every gate above it agree with itself, so the real gate lives in `test_e2e.py` and forces the stop from the model's own first byte. **A stop matched from token 0 killed every thinking-on request, caught in review:** the reasoning block is part of `output`, so `"\n\n"` fires on the reasoning's first paragraph break — reproduced on a real engine, the request **ended at token 1** with a truncated thought, no answer, and a `stop_sequence` reason to explain it. Fixed by reusing the `thought_closed` the engine already tracks for `end_think_ids`: nothing matches until the closer, then `_stop_hit` reads `output[reply_from:]`. **The canned double needed the same rule, which is this entry's finding a second time** — the route arm passes with the engine's gate deleted, so there are two arms, one per layer, each red only when its own layer is reverted. Streaming needed a second mechanism and my first one was wrong: a holdback of `len(longest)-1` chars covers a forming match but not a completed one, and the frame before the last carried `"The answer "`, the leading space of `" is"`. 390 passed, 1 pre-existing env failure (`test_sandbox_confines_writes_to_the_rollout_dir`, same negative-control assert as before this branch). Three live arms added to `scripts/api_e2e.py` derive the stop from the model's own reply, smoke-tested against the canned server only — **V100 `pending-remote`**, and whether a multi-character stop lands mid-token on the 27B's real tokenizer is precisely what that run establishes. [wins/2026-09-06-stop-sequences-matched-on-text-not-token-ids.md](docs/experience/wins/2026-09-06-stop-sequences-matched-on-text-not-token-ids.md)

- **fix** — **every field the API surfaces accepted and ignored now returns 400, and three of them were never received at all.** A field taken and ignored is a format lie with a delayed cost: the client's NEXT request assumes the first honoured it, so `store=true` implies a `previous_response_id` that would 404 and a hosted tool type implies the server ran the search. One `prompt.refuse_unsupported(**fields)` raising `ValueError` — the path all three routes already turn into their own 400 envelope, same mechanism as the image-block rejection. Refused: `store`, `previous_response_id`, `include`, `truncation="auto"`, `tool_choice` beyond auto/none on both OpenAI routes, the `context_management` edits we do not perform, and ten hosted tool types. **Refusing all of `context_management` broke the live client and the rollout gate caught it:** Claude Code sends it on every request, captured from the CLI's own body as `{"edits":[{"type":"clear_thinking_20251015","keep":"all"}]}`, and that edit asks for behaviour `blocks_to_text` already has — so the refusal was the mirror of the defect this fixes, a 400 for a field we DO honour. `messages_shim_stage1.md:25` had listed the key since 09-02 and I read that file without connecting it. Now refused per edit, not per field. **The finding: `previous_response_id`, `include`, `truncation` and `stop` could not be refused because they were never declared** — pydantic drops an undeclared field without a trace (measured: `model_extra` is None), so the value never reached the handler and the client got a 200 with no sign it vanished, which is worse than ignoring because nothing in the code can be grepped for. Each had to be ADDED to the request model in order to be rejected. Three controls, and the third earns its place: a blanket refusal passes every arm asserting a 400 while breaking plain `chat non-stream`, so "the refusals fire" and "the refusals are narrow" need separate checks. `stop`/`stop_sequences` are refused but tracked in OPEN.md rather than listed as unsupported, since they are core fields of both APIs and `engine.py:1129` already honours `stop_token_ids` — `prompt.py:57` just never plumbs a request field to it. [wins/2026-09-06-accepted-and-ignored-is-a-format-lie.md](docs/experience/wins/2026-09-06-accepted-and-ignored-is-a-format-lie.md)
- **feat** — **`/v1/responses` serves the OpenAI Responses API, and the SDK will not tell you a required field is missing.** Third surface on the same engine, tokenizer, `render_prompt` and `split_think`; only the wire shape differs (a flat typed `output` list, reasoning as its own item). Schema read off `openai.types.responses` rather than the docs: `Response` declares `parallel_tool_calls`/`tool_choice`/`tools` required and every stream event a `sequence_number`, none of which appear in a doc example. **Then deleting `parallel_tool_calls` from the body broke no test** — `Response.model_validate` rejects that body but the client builds replies with `construct`, so a required field arrives as `None` and nothing raises. "The SDK accepted it" is therefore not evidence the body is complete; the gate now asserts six field names on the raw JSON, since a parsed object cannot tell absent from null. **This corrected a claim I had already written** into the module docstring, that the SDK would reject such a reply. Also closes the replay gap: a thinking block sent back in the next assistant turn is dropped by `blocks_to_text` (wanted — the template re-opens `<think>` only after the last real user query), previously untested and so indistinguishable from a silent format regression. `scripts/api_e2e.py` runs the same checks against a live `--base-url` and **first reported "all checks passed" with 5 of 11 arms skipped**; it now exits 2 above a third skipped and names them. Route costs 1.01 ms non-stream / 4.02 ms streamed on the canned engine, between the two existing routes; no per-request claim, V100 `pending-remote`. Stateless only — `store` is ignored, no `previous_response_id`. [wins/2026-09-06-responses-api-and-the-construct-gap.md](docs/experience/wins/2026-09-06-responses-api-and-the-construct-gap.md)
- **fix** — **`splitlines()` cut an SSE payload mid-JSON, and the count I wrote to catch it agreed 11-to-11.** #158's ubuntu gate failed on `Unterminated string at column 145` while macos passed and a rerun of the same sha went green — intermittent, but a rerun identifies nothing, so the mechanism took two probes. **Nine separators `splitlines()` splits on beyond `\n`; exactly three break the parse** — `\x85`, U+2028, U+2029 — because `server.py:93` writes `ensure_ascii=False` and passes them through verbatim, while `\v \f \r \x1c \x1d \x1e` are ASCII controls `json.dumps` escapes so no literal byte is emitted. Second probe, the other half of the claim: `_ByteTokenizer.decode` is byte-level UTF-8 with byte→id+3, so **3/3 are reachable from sampled ids** (U+0085 = bytes 194,133 → ids 197,136, inside `tiny()`'s vocab of 320) — the failure needs one unlucky adjacent pair, which is why it is intermittent and platform-dependent. **Not claimed:** a rate; uniform-draw arithmetic gives 2.3e-04 over 16 tokens but the model is not uniform. Fixed at all four parse sites (`test_server.py:257/:356/:402` and the failing one, plus `scripts/probe_sse_deltas.py:39`) with `split("\n")`, which is the spec-correct reader regardless of which separator fired. **My own guard against a vacuous test was itself vacuous:** `len(split("\n")) < len(splitlines())` FAILED on a stream that does carry `\x85` — cutting at it moves the frame's remainder out of the `startswith("data:")` filter the parse uses, so the totals match by construction. **A count over a filtered set cannot see a cut that moves an element out of the filter.** The control now executes the old parse under `pytest.raises(JSONDecodeError)` on the same response. Between those states I read the equal counts as transit re-encoding and tested latin-1 and a UTF-8 round-trip for a transform that did not exist — two probes said 3-vs-3 and 11-vs-11 and I read the second as refuting the first when only my metric was wrong. `ensure_ascii=False` stays: escaping would inflate every non-ASCII reply and the 27B's Chinese output is the common case. 34 passed, ruff clean. Dev-only test/probe change, no runtime change, so no bench entry. [errors/2026-09-06-splitlines-cut-an-sse-payload.md](docs/experience/errors/2026-09-06-splitlines-cut-an-sse-payload.md)
- **snapshot** — **dated H20 snapshot at `faae3c8`, real 27B NVFP4, idle card 6; every row moved off the 09-03 numbers.** Decode B=1 **94.3 tok/s** (10.61 ms/tick), prefill **3020.1 / 2870.8 / 2719.6 tok/s** at 512/2048/8192 (was 2689.8 / 2671.7 / 2558.6 at `9e3836b`, so **+12.3% / +7.5% / +6.3%**), B=8 aggregate **354.8 tok/s** (23.7 ms/tick, was 328.4 at `6e260b8`, +8.0%, and all eight replies semantically correct rather than a degenerate stream), one GRPO step **25.3 s warm** = 16.959 rollout + 8.070 backward + 0.241 optimizer, against **118.1 s cold** for the identical config — **4.67x of a single-step run is its own JIT and graph-capture warmup** (4.32x rollout, 5.54x backward), which I published as a step cost while this same snapshot's decode round printed `warmup pass 1: 25.2s / pass 2: 0.2s`. The per-token cross-check is what catches it without a rerun: 0.420 s/token cold against a known 0.077 is **5.5x**, the suspect-the-instrument threshold; warm reads **0.097, 1.3x**. **Step 2 died with CUDA OOM** (`296.00 MiB` wanted, `221.56 MiB` free, one process holding 95.00 of 95.22 GiB), so **at cap 2048 / group 8 this configuration does not survive past step 1 on one H20 and no steady-state median exists** — a capacity finding, not a timing one. **No comparison to run 2's 229.2 s:** that is MATH level 5 at a 1434-token mean against gsm8k at 174, and #140's buckets put the backward at width 256 against 2048, so the ratio would measure the task and the bucket. The one same-task reference is P1's 56.88 s/step (gsm8k, group 8) at cap **256**, and 25.3 s is a warm first step rather than a median — both differences push the same way, so no valid s/step comparison exists yet at this cap. The tied-group FAIL stands and is not an invalid run: P1's shipped run fails `groups_untied` too, at 0.81. Split 67/32 rollout/backward warm, peak 88.21 GiB on the cold run, adapter 124.8M. **No row is attributed to a PR:** the prefill window `9e3836b`..`faae3c8` has nameable candidates but choosing one without a bisect is a guess wearing provenance, and a 27B bisect costs more card time than the answer is worth — rows read *moved, unattributed*. **The decode delta is against `commit: "unknown"`** dated 08-28, so +2.1% is not quotable; what this supplies is the first fully-provenanced value for that shape and the old row is superseded. **Three readings that would mislead:** the GRPO run exits 1 and the ledger says FAIL on `groups_untied` (1.0 vs threshold 0.5) — all eight rollouts tied so the step carried **no learning signal**, yet rollout/backward/optimizer all executed, so 118.1 s measures what a step COSTS and not a step that learns; `0.0 step/s` in the summary is one-decimal formatting of 0.00847, not a failed timing; and no `baseline-candidate.json` was written because the row is a **SEED** (`t2048` key never existed) and `bench_harness.py:117` writes candidates only when there is something to beat. `bench-baseline.json` untouched — per #112 a promotion is deliberate. Pod version came from `.synced_commit` plus content markers, since the pod checkout is not a git repo. [wins/2026-09-06-h20-snapshot-at-faae3c8.md](docs/experience/wins/2026-09-06-h20-snapshot-at-faae3c8.md)

- **fix** — **five spec deviations across both API routes, found by driving the official SDKs instead of asserting on dicts.** `openai` 3.8.0 and `anthropic` 1.4.0 as dev deps, one uvicorn over the canned engine: the chat non-stream path dropped the `reasoning_content` the stream returns (#159's asymmetry, three hours old), chat `tools` was not a field at all so the raw `<tool_call>` XML reached clients in `message.content`, `/v1/messages` `thinking` emitted no thinking block, and both routes answered a bad field with FastAPI's 422 `detail` list instead of their own error envelope. One `RequestValidationError` handler fixed both envelopes (the Anthropic arm went green without that route being touched); the tool path reuses `messages._parse_tool_calls` and `render_prompt(tools=)`, so the only new code is a 12-line shape flattener. **The control that was missing is the finding:** deleting the code that renders schemas into the prompt broke NO test, because a canned reply carries the tool call whether or not the prompt defined the tool — a reply-side assertion cannot gate the request side. Adding the SSE thinking block also turned `messages.py`'s two-way block branch into a `KeyError` inside the generator, which reaches the client as an incomplete chunked body rather than an error. **No perf claim:** repeat runs of the new `scripts/bench_api_routes.py` move the medians by 0.96 ms against a 0.31 ms delta, so nothing here resolves outside run-to-run spread; V100 `pending-remote`. `/v1/responses` still 404s and streaming tool-call deltas are not implemented. 360 passed. [wins/2026-09-06-sdk-e2e-found-five-api-deviations.md](docs/experience/wins/2026-09-06-sdk-e2e-found-five-api-deviations.md)
- **error** — **four sites divided a speculative tok/s by a dense weight roofline, and updating the stale denominator would have made every one of them worse.** The `64 tok/s` figure was retired on 09-02 as remembered, so the obvious repair is swapping in the current number; against the era-correct 56.1 the depth-3 52.7 reads **94%** and against the f16-era 62.3 it reads **85%** — both *higher* than the published 82%, which is the tell that the stale framing hid a dimensional error. **A weight roofline is bytes/forward over bandwidth, so it bounds forwards, not tokens**, and speculation exists to break that identity: at the 2.95 tok/forward measured beside the 52.7, the applicable ceiling is 126-166 tok/s and the real share is **32-42%**, so 82% overstated by 2.95x. The correction needs no byte count — `% of the spec ceiling = (% of dense) / (tok per forward)` — and the bracket's width is the one thing still unmeasured: nothing buckets a **draft** step's streamed bytes the way `check_scale_f16.py` buckets the trunk's, so 32% (draft free) and 42% (draft charged 4.95 GB) bound it from both ends and both reject 82%. Four other citations of 64 stand and are untouched: two label themselves as a retired estimate, and two use it one-sidedly to reject a 289 tok/s warmup artifact — **a bound only has to be exceeded to fire, so a one-sided test tolerates a denominator an equality cannot.** Docs-only, no runtime change. [errors/2026-09-06-a-spec-rate-over-a-dense-roofline.md](docs/experience/errors/2026-09-06-a-spec-rate-over-a-dense-roofline.md)
- **fix** — **the link gate scanned `docs/` only, so CHANGELOG.md's 321 `.md` links were never checked — and the gap had already let one through.** `CHANGELOG.md` carries `merge=union`, so cherry-picking onto main copied #157's CHANGELOG line into #158's branch verbatim, citing an entry that was not on that branch; the gate ran and passed, and I caught it by hand. Two arms, same injected dead link, baseline green first: **`docs/` rc=1 and the failure names the injected link, CHANGELOG.md rc=0 and silent.** Fixed by scanning root-level `.md` beside `docs/**`. **My first repair silently weakened the gate:** widening the scan turned AGENTS.md's entry skeleton `` `errors/YYYY-MM-DD-slug.md` `` — a literal template — into two dead links (AGENTS.md and its CLAUDE.md symlink), and requiring `20\d\d` in `_TICK` to suppress it dropped **130 → 125 tick-shaped hits across 349 files, 3 of the 5 lost being real hits on `TEMPLATE-bench.md`**. A tightening that fixes a false positive by dropping true ones is not a fix; the exemption is now the placeholder string. **Both guards were made to fail on purpose** — dropping the `"/" not in p` operand reddens `test_a_root_level_md_is_scanned` on "a root-level .md is not in the scanned set", dropping the exemption reddens the main resolver on "unresolved markdown references", each with the other tests still green and `__pycache__` cleared between arms. Dev-only tooling, no runtime change, so no bench entry. [errors/2026-09-06-the-link-gate-skipped-the-changelog.md](docs/experience/errors/2026-09-06-the-link-gate-skipped-the-changelog.md)
- **fix** — **a finished run with no gates DEFINED read `pass`, and `tilerl merge` is every row of it.** #148 fixed the unfinished case; a finished manifest with an empty gate list still fell through to pass/FAIL, and `gates_pass([])` is `all([])` = **True**. Found by probe, not by reading — a real `tilerl merge` on cpu: `144b31c31f4d merge <ts> pass tensors=1` with `gates=[]` and `finished` set. `cmd_merge` writes its manifest directly and never through `_finish`, so it appends no gates, and `grep -n gates` confirms the only three writers are `cli.py:594`, `cli.py:806` and the empty init. A fourth verdict **`none`** now covers it — **not `skip`**, which in this tree means a gate existed and was suppressed (`gates_skip_after`, and the drift gate that `tilerl train --allow-short-rollouts` bypasses); ckl ruled on the naming after I leaned `skip`. `gates_pass` is untouched, so `_finish`'s exit code is too. Four negative controls, each red on its own assertion, with controls 3 and 4 failing *different* tests than 1 and 2 — so the new branch absorbs neither neighbour. **My first control 1 was invalid:** replacing `elif not m["gates"]:` with `elif False:` drops into the branch I had just edited, where the `m["gates"] and` guard was gone, so `all([])` printed `skip` — a state I constructed rather than main's `pass`. Restoring main's exact two branches reproduces it. The end-to-end assertion sits in `test_merge.py` on the test that already runs `tilerl merge` through `main()`, not in a new fixture. [wins/2026-09-06-a-gateless-run-read-pass.md](docs/experience/wins/2026-09-06-a-gateless-run-read-pass.md)

- **fix** — **the chat page split the reply on a `</think>` the server had stripped since #151, so every HTML reply landed in the reasoning fold over an empty bubble.** The stream now carries the reasoning as `reasoning_content` and the answer as `content`; the page folds on the field and names a reply cut off inside the block. [errors/2026-09-06-the-page-split-on-a-closer-the-server-had-stripped.md](docs/experience/errors/2026-09-06-the-page-split-on-a-closer-the-server-had-stripped.md)
- **error** — **the spill has six stage timers and none of them wrapped the save, which is the stage with the number: `~100 ms` in five places, measured 641.8.** The comment above those timers says why they exist — "three guesses at the per-publish cost were wrong in a row, so the spill reports where its time goes instead of being guessed at a fourth time" — and the instrument built to stop this skipped the largest stage in the function. `ssd_save_ms` and `ssd_saves` are now published together, both, because the claim is a **per-save** cost and a total alone cannot be divided by a count the caller never sees. Measured with `scripts/probe_save_ms.py` driving `spill_state` directly (no GPU, no model — the save is a daemon write of a CPU blob), distinct contents per entry: **641.8 ms/save for one real 320.6 MiB entry at 499.6 MiB/s, and 54.7 ms at 32.0 MiB** — 10.02x the bytes costs 11.74x the time, so cost is byte-dominated and extrapolating by bandwidth is sound in form. **6.4x the asserted figure on THIS device; the pod's write rate is unmeasured** (`/work` is inside the container namespace and a write probe contends with the live endpoint for exactly what it measures). **Two of my own derivations were wrong first, both larger:** 8.6x from 156.9 MB, dropping the 167.8 MB of KV the same entry carries; then 17.6x from 320.6 MiB over **182.6 MiB/s — which is a READ rate**, measured on the two files one fault-in reads, and substituting it into a write path has no basis. **A second defect was found and withdrawn:** `spill_state` has no capacity check, no `_healthy` check, and returns `None` so it cannot refuse, and a probe with a red KV control arm read **32 blobs against a cap of 4** — but `insert:875-877` reaches `spill_state` only inside `and self._ssd.spill_kv(...)`, so the KV refusal gates both halves; the real pair reads peak 4 / 5, stable over three runs, and the +1 is the two tables draining on different clocks. **Not fixed:** `max_pending=32` still holds 10.0 GiB of a 31 GB host and was sized against the 100 ms; that needs the pod's rate. Runtime change is two counters, no behaviour change. [errors/2026-09-06-the-save-stage-had-no-timer.md](docs/experience/errors/2026-09-06-the-save-stage-had-no-timer.md)

- **reject** — **the snapshot tier crosses the state-byte ceiling, demotes 7 times, and stops permanently: a demotion returns bytes and zero blocks.** First run above the crossover (H20 card 6, 12 sessions x 3 turns, `--max-ctx 49152` = 3072 blocks, 17.05 GiB budget, real 27B): state bytes hit **99.4% while blocks were at 91.9%** and 7 demotions fired — then blocks reached 99.4% and every insert after took `evict_until_free`, which calls `_evict_one` directly and cannot demote. **262 evictions, 7 demotions, 0 promotions, 12 hits over 351 publishes, `dram_demote_ms=635` (91 ms each) all wasted** since nothing was promoted back. The two ceilings arrive **1.4 points apart**, so enlarging the pool is useless rather than insufficient: at any size where state binds, blocks are a hair from binding too, and demotion relieves one of them. Prior "wrong above 9 concurrent sessions" came from hit counts on a block-bound pool where the tier never ran. **Validity criterion for any future arm is `dram_promotions > 0`, not `dram_demotions > 0`** — this run satisfies the latter and measured nothing. **Two of my own numbers withdrawn:** a "shorter prompts favour the tier" table (it multiplied per-entry blocks by entry count, but **blocks are refcounted across nested prefixes and snapshots are not** — measured per session: 4 publishes, 598 MiB of state, **86.7 blocks, not 4x89**; correct operands give budget 29.2 sessions vs pool 35.4, state binds, as observed), and a 40,459-token crossover solved from turn 0 that held for 1 request out of 36. `dram_bytes=0` stays the default, now with a measurement past the crossover instead of an inference below it; the 2/8/12 wall-clock sweep is **not run and should not be**, since both arms would measure the same path. [errors/2026-09-06-the-tier-crosses-the-ceiling-and-stops.md](docs/experience/errors/2026-09-06-the-tier-crosses-the-ceiling-and-stops.md)

- **fix** — **both API routes returned the model's reasoning and a bare `</think>` ahead of the reply.** The template opens `<think>` in the prompt, so the reply carries only the closer and `strip_think` never matched; the self-check asserted the two-tag case the live path never produces. `strip_think(opened=)` on all four live call sites, gate with opened / thinking-off / bare-turn arms, control fails on the old regex.
