[errors/2026-09-06-the-reply-carried-the-reasoning-and-a-bare-closer.md](docs/experience/errors/2026-09-06-the-reply-carried-the-reasoning-and-a-bare-closer.md)
- **fix** — **three of four probe rounds asked a question `/health` had already answered, and the one key that WAS missing resolved a cross-card contradiction.** `--dram-bytes`'s first wall-clock arm on H20 card 6 came back `demote=0` in all four arms with 27 evictions each; the `INVALID` was 4 compiles in the first arm only (`on_1` at 1.491 s sits inside `off`'s 1.476/1.506, so the 0.825x was first-position JIT). The binding operand is BLOCKS: the pool drained to **74 free of 512** and then evicted, while state bytes peaked at **2.78 of 17.68 GiB — 15.7%**, never a trigger. `evict_until_free` cannot demote by design, so **`demote=0` is correct behaviour**. **`pool_used_blocks` was already published** (`engine.py:677`) and said this in round one — my probe printed a hand-listed `keys` tuple that omitted it (`grep -c pool_used` on round one's log: **0**), so it reported a partition of the data rather than the data; it now prints `sorted(st)`. A `pool_free_blocks` key I added before noticing was **removed again** as a third name for the same number. The real gap was **`prefix_state_bytes_budget`**: `state_bytes` comes from `mem_get_info()[0]//4` at build time and is unknowable from outside at any number of rounds, and it settled a contradiction — the V100's budget is 1697 MiB, the H20's is **17.68 GiB, 11.5x**, so a threshold measured on one card had been substituted into the other's condition. **One number is withdrawn:** an earlier draft multiplied 23.05 average blocks/entry by 121 entries and reported that state binds at 44,624 tokens of pool. Blocks per entry is not a rate — 21.67 → 23.83 → 26.67 as the prompt grows, since an entry's blocks are its own prefix (`d_free=65` against `ceil(1011/16)=64`) — so the average understates the requirement, in the direction of "cheaper than it is". What holds without extrapolation: 19 entries retained **438 of 512 blocks, 86% full**, so the pool must grow >5x and the exact ceiling needs a run reading both operands. [wins/2026-09-06-health-could-not-name-the-pressure.md](docs/experience/wins/2026-09-06-health-could-not-name-the-pressure.md)

- **feat** — **`tilerl serve --dram-bytes` exposes the host snapshot tier, and two things had to be fixed for the gate to mean anything.** The tier was reachable only by editing `build_engine`'s default, so the multi-session case the [two-variable condition](docs/experience/errors/2026-09-05-a-two-variable-condition-read-as-a-dead-end.md) describes needed a source patch to run at all; the flag defaults to **0** and its help names the condition (`sessions > HBM snapshot budget`, 9 on a 32GB V100) and the cost below it (**1.51x worse wall clock**, 43 demotions and 0 promotions on one conversation). **The tier was nested under `if backend.device.type == "cuda"`** — inert on the CPU target, which is the only target CI runs, so a gate would have gone green with the tier never constructed; it is now a sibling of the `state_bytes` branch, for the same reason the SSD tier is not gated on cuda. **`/health` published the wrong operand**: `dram_bytes` is bytes *held*, so it reads 0 both with the tier off and with it on before the first demotion; `dram_budget` is new. The gate drives `cmd_serve` end to end with `uvicorn.run` monkeypatched to hit `/health` in process — `--dram-bytes 12345678` asserts `dram_budget == 12345678`, the control asserts the key **absent** rather than 0. Four negative controls, each red on its own message: dropping the forward and re-nesting under cuda both give `dram_* served: {}`; publishing only the old key gives all seven `dram_*` keys with `dram_bytes: 0`, which is exactly what an equality on it would have accepted; constructing the tier unconditionally fails the **control** arm with `dram_budget=4294967296`. [wins/2026-09-06-dram-bytes-flag-and-health-gate.md](docs/experience/wins/2026-09-06-dram-bytes-flag-and-health-gate.md)
- **fix** — **`tilerl ledger` printed `pass` for a run that was killed mid-training, and the test asserting it was the defect's alibi.** Making an interrupted run visible (#146) created a way to misread it: gates are written by `_finish`, so an unfinished OPD manifest carries an empty gate list and `gates_pass([])` is `all([])` = **True**. Measured on cpu, real `tilerl train --opd` with SIGTERM on a `ps`-verified pid: `e069c8ff28b7 train running pass` before, **`killed`** after. grpo escaped it only because it pre-seeds `rollouts_within_cap`, which makes the list non-empty and reads `skip`. `format_run` now checks `finished` before any gate — display layer only, so `gates_pass` and `_finish`'s exit code are untouched, and a finished run's verdict is computed exactly as before. **`test_manifest_round_trip_and_lineage` built a manifest, never set `finished`, and asserted `pass`** — encoding the bug as the expectation and passing throughout; it now asserts `killed` unfinished and `pass` once `finished` is set, with the negative control (drop the `finished` check) failing at `assert 'pass' == 'killed'`. [wins/2026-09-06-an-interrupted-run-reported-pass.md](docs/experience/wins/2026-09-06-an-interrupted-run-reported-pass.md)

- **phase exit** — **GRPO writes `runs/<id>/rollouts.jsonl`, one row per completion, because the claim run 2 was making could not be made from what it recorded.** `grpo_loop` yielded `np.mean(rewards)` and `np.mean(len(c))`, so length and reward were averaged over the group before anything saw them — and the advantage is computed *within* a group on one prompt, so run 2's length-vs-reward evidence is a cross-step correlation over 45 different prompts, confounded by difficulty. `per_rollout` now carries `step/g/tokens/reward/advantage` per rollout (the `per_problem=` out-parameter idiom already used by `gsm8k_accuracy`), written even when the drift guard breaks the loop, since those rows are the collapse. `metrics.length_reward_r` is Pearson r pooled over **within-group deviations**: on a fixture carrying only the confound — hard prompts long and low-reward, easy short and high, no within-group relation — the pooled view reads **−0.8959** and the centered one **+0.0000** on the same rows, while longer-always-wrong still gives −0.9922 and longer-always-right +0.9975. `None`, not `0.0`, when a group has no reward variance to correlate. Three mutations, each red on its own assertion: dropping the centering gives r=+0.484, returning 0.0 fails `assert 0.0 is None`, and recording group means — the run-2 defect reintroduced — fails with "2 rows for 2x2 rollouts". [wins/2026-09-06-per-rollout-length-reward.md](docs/experience/wins/2026-09-06-per-rollout-length-reward.md)

- **default flip** — GRPO stops when its last five steps average above 0.8 times the rollout cap, saves the adapter and a failed `rollouts_within_cap` manifest gate, and skips after-eval. `--allow-short-rollouts` bypasses both length guards. [wins/2026-09-06-periodic-rollout-guard.md](docs/experience/wins/2026-09-06-periodic-rollout-guard.md)
- **analysis, no code** — **a length term in the GRPO reward reaches at most 17 of run 2's 19 zero-gradient steps, cannot reach 2 of them at all, and its coefficient has a closed form rather than needing a sweep.** 19 of 45 steps had `tied == 1.00` and so produced no gradient, costing **1.08 h of 2.71 h (39.8%)** — but the split is **5 floor ties** (reward 0.000) against **14 all-pass ties** (reward 1.000), so `tied` alone cannot trigger anything. At steps **41 and 44** the group mean equals the cap, so all 8 rollouts are clipped to the same width and any function of length is constant within the group: those two are recoverable only by raising the cap. **λ is bounded, not tuned:** a wrong answer outranks a right one iff `λ·(len_right − len_wrong)/cap > 1`, so ordering holds for all `λ < cap/gap`, and the worst reachable gap is `cap − 1` — **λ ≤ 1.0 is safe for any lengths**, with bisection on the worst case (one correct at the cap, seven wrong at length 1) putting the first inversion at **λ = 1.000488550** against the closed form `2048/2047 = 1.000488520`, agreement to eight digits. **A random sweep cannot find that boundary and its silence is not evidence:** 8 uniform lengths give a gap above `0.99·cap` in 0.260% of groups, so a 50 000-group search reports 0 inversions at λ = 1.001 while the bound is exact. The first draft of this entry cited "114 inversions at λ = 1.01" from such a sweep and called it the boundary; that was luck, and it is corrected here. And because `group_advantages` divides by the group std, **λ cancels completely on an all-pass group** — 0.1 and 1.0 give the identical advantage vector — so it does nothing on 14 of the 19 target steps and anyone tuning it against the tie fraction will see no movement. **One claim in the run-2 entry does not survive:** its 0.893-vs-0.458 reward split by length reproduces exactly (n=21/24) but is a *cross-step* correlation, while GRPO's advantage is within a group on one prompt; each step drew a different prompt, so prompt difficulty confounds it and the mechanism sentence is an inference, not a measurement. [wins/2026-09-06-what-a-length-term-can-recover.md](docs/experience/wins/2026-09-06-what-a-length-term-can-recover.md)
- **default flip** — GRPO pads completions to power-of-two length buckets instead of the rollout cap; true-length masks are unchanged. CPU loss/gradient parity gate; 27B timings pending-remote. [wins/2026-09-06-grpo-length-buckets.md](docs/experience/wins/2026-09-06-grpo-length-buckets.md)

- **reject** — **MATH run 2 killed at step 45 of 100: GRPO with a correctness-only reward lengthens its rollouts until they hit the cap, and then every group ties at the floor.** The run cleared `_refuse_short_rollouts` at launch (before-arm mean 1029 tokens against a 2048 cap) and collapsed onto that cap at step 41. Ten-step windows, mean tok / mean reward / floor ties: 890/0.887/0, 1122/0.662/0, 1198/0.700/1, 1277/0.625/1, then **41-45 at 1923/0.225/3** — five floor ties in all, the five longest steps at the time they ran, two (**41 and 44**) sitting exactly on 2048 with all eight completions cut before the `\boxed{}`. **#131's line said the guard made this 'impossible by construction'; that is wrong and is corrected here** — it runs once, before step 1, and cannot see the policy lengthening as it trains. **The drift is superlinear and my first published fit understated it:** slope 7.74 tok/step over steps 1-40 predicts 1312 at step 45; the measured 41-45 mean was **1923**, and refitting over all 45 gives 15.92 — double, which is how a straight line says it is the wrong model. **Mechanism, from the data rather than inferred:** reward is 0.893 for steps under 1200 tokens and 0.458 at or above (n=21/24), so short rollouts score nearly twice as well and the policy still moves toward long ones. `boxed_match` is pure correctness with no length term, so nothing in the objective prefers the shorter of two correct answers or penalises running to the cap for zero. **What the run did establish:** MATH level 5 does not exhaust the way GSM8K did — at step 35, P1's comparison point where GSM8K sat at 0.87 tied and reward 0.975, this run was **0.34 tied** at reward ~0.70, ties from difficulty rather than from the task being solved. Cost at a 2048 cap **229.2 s/step median** (45 steps, 2.71 h), 4.0x P1's 56.88 at a 256 cap, and the backward is **cap-bound not token-bound** — median 126.9 s over a 117.4-177.6 range while `tok` spanned 242 to 2048, because `train.py:428` pads every row to `prompt + max_new_tokens`. Killed rather than finished: ~4.5 h more of gradient-free 2048-token rollouts plus ~3 h of after-arm, for a number already known, on a card run 3 needs. Card released, exit 143, 0 MiB. **`eval-before.jsonl` survived the SIGTERM with all 500 rows** — #131's other half working. Fixes in order: padding buckets, a periodic drift guard, then a length term in the reward, which is the actual cause. [errors/2026-09-06-the-rollouts-grew-into-the-cap.md](docs/experience/errors/2026-09-06-the-rollouts-grew-into-the-cap.md)

## 2026-09-05

- **fix** — **MATH run 2's eval file is levels 3-5 mixed, not level 5, so its 80.0% base was read against a 45.8% expectation from a different set.** The before-arm came back 400/500 and the recipe comment said "LEVEL 5 ONLY, 45.8% (11/24)"; a 34-point gap is a data hypothesis before it is a model one. Joined back to the parquet shards by problem text: `math_tr_5.jsonl` is **2304 rows, all Level 5** — the training data is right — while `math_test500.jsonl` is **Level 3: 165, Level 4: 157, Level 5: 178**, built without the `--level 5` its siblings got. Neither number was wrong; putting them in one sentence was. **The run stays valid** — both arms score the same file, so the paired delta holds; it answers "did level-5 training help on levels 3-5" and now says so. The "mean completion on level 5 is 1038 tokens" line came from the same mixed file (this run measures **1029**, 514554/500); the rollout-cap decision it justified is unaffected, since 1029 against a 512 cap truncates either way. **`scripts/math_jsonl.py` now writes `level` into every row and prints the histogram of what it actually wrote**, not of the filter it was asked for: the file carried only `prompt` and `answer`, so its name was the only claim about its contents and nothing downstream could contradict it. [errors/2026-09-05-the-eval-file-was-not-the-level-it-was-named.md](docs/experience/errors/2026-09-05-the-eval-file-was-not-the-level-it-was-named.md)

- **reject** — **GSM8K is retired as a GRPO task: the no-think 27B already scores 88.0% on it uncapped, so 81% of groups tie and the run buys almost nothing.** 100 steps, group 8, `grpo-gsm8k-27b` on card 0 — **56.88 s/step median (32.6 / 140.6 min-max), 99.4 min total, 39.78 GiB peak**, which is the one number this run establishes and the budget every later GRPO estimate should come from. Two of five gates FAIL: `groups_untied` at **0.81** against threshold 0.5, and `reward_rises` (0.800 → 0.775). Of the 81 tied steps **73 tie at the CEILING**, and the fraction climbs 0.50 → 0.87 over the run, reproducing [P1 ties at the ceiling](docs/experience/errors/2026-09-03-p1-ties-at-the-ceiling.md)'s 0.86 at a different seed and slice. **The task result, restated honestly after the eval cap was removed: +5.6 points, 95% CI [+2.0, +9.2]** (base 440/500 = 88.0%, adapter 468/500 = 93.6%, n=500 seed 0 greedy, same test file). **The capped pair's +53.4 was 90% broken control** — lifting the eval cap from 256 to 2048 is worth **+49.6 points to the BASE arm and +1.8 to the trained one**, because the base policy averages 238.7 tokens against a 256 ceiling; that is [the eval cap measured itself](docs/experience/errors/2026-09-04-the-eval-cap-measured-itself.md) at a second seed. **The real effect is length, not accuracy: tokens per correct answer 394.0 → 143.8, 2.74x**, which is what a reward already satisfied 81% of the time can still teach. The interval is **unpaired** although both arms scored the same 500 problems, because the eval logged only totals — #125 and #131 write per-problem rows so the next comparison can use McNemar. Two numbers that are NOT results: MMLU 75.2 → 75.7 (±3.8 at n=1000, no movement), and the manifest's reward/token windows, both inside the per-step spread of a run where 81% of steps carried no gradient. **CE cannot be read alone** — it appears to climb 1.52 → 5.09 and corr(CE, completion tokens) is **−0.99**, since CE is a per-token mean and the short completions are the high-CE ones. The adapter, measured rather than estimated: 2086 keys / 170.76 M params, of which **1090 keys (52%) are dead** — and this run predates the fix at `863a257`, so all 1090 rode through 100 steps at two AdamW moments each. No `bench-baseline.json` row: that file is SOTA-only and run 2 with the decode graph on will beat this. [wins/2026-09-05-p1-grpo-27b-run.md](docs/experience/wins/2026-09-05-p1-grpo-27b-run.md)
- **default flip** — **the before-arm eval is cached on the base model's identity, so changing a training flag no longer re-scores an unchanged base.** `$TILERL_RUNS/eval-cache/<sha256>.json` holds the before metrics, the per-problem rows and the mean completion length the rollout guard reads; a hit restores `eval-before.jsonl` and records `cache_hit` plus the key in the manifest. The after arm is never cached, and loaded adapters and drafts bypass it. **The key's `weights` entry is present in every branch or there is no key**: the 27B hashes its checkpoint files' paths, sizes and nanosecond mtimes, `tiny` records an explicit `None` because `build_random` is a pure function of `--seed` and the seed arrives through the sampling params, and any other model **refuses to cache** — a key that omits the weights serves one base's before-arm for another, and the omission is invisible. `--tp` reaches the key twice, through the already-sharded `cfg` and as its own field, so a change in call order cannot silently drop it. **Existing run ids change:** `eval_max_new_tokens` and `load_adapter` join the id inputs, so a rerun of P1's flags retrains rather than returning `1fa1e58388a2` — checked, that is the only manifest on the pod. Gates in `tests/test_eval_rows.py`: hit/miss on a learning-rate change (eval sampling calls 4 → 2, identical rows), a miss on an eval-cap change, a bypass on `--load-adapter`, a checkpoint mtime change at constant size, and one asserting what the key must and must not cover — `--seed`, `--eval-n`, `--eval-max-new-tokens`, `--temperature` move it; `--lora-rank`, `--steps`, `--lr` do not. **Each new assertion has a control that fails**: dropping the explicit `tp` field, making the unknown-model branch return a key, omitting `weights`. **Not established:** the hub `snapshot_download` branch is unexercised (the pod's source is a real directory), and `--max-think-tokens` cannot move the key on the tiny path since `sampling()` drops it when thinking is None. 27B validation pending-remote. [Before-eval cache](docs/experience/wins/2026-09-05-before-eval-cache.md)

- **fix** — **MATH run 2 died at step 6 with every group tied at the FLOOR: the rollout cap was 512 and the base policy's mean completion on level 5 is 1038 tokens, so no rollout ever reached its `\\boxed{}`.** Steps 2-6 all read `reward 0.0000 tied 1.00 tok 512` — the cap, exactly; step 1 is the tell, `tok 259 reward 0.7500 tied 0.00`, the one group whose completions fit. **Third form of one family, now named:** P1's GSM8K tied at the CEILING because the task was solved (88.0% base), the eval cap scored the CAP instead of the policy (38.4% vs 88.0%), and this tied at the FLOOR because the rollouts were truncated — all three a length or difficulty parameter set without measuring the quantity it bounds. `grpo-math-27b` goes to `max_new_tokens=2048`, and **`_refuse_short_rollouts` refuses this configuration at launch**: the before-arm has already measured the mean completion when training starts, so a mean above 0.8x the cap exits with both numbers named (0.8, not 1.0 — a mean that fits exactly still truncates half). **This line first read "impossible by construction", which is wrong and is corrected here** — the guard runs once, before step 1, and cannot see the policy lengthening as it trains; run 2 drifted +10.4 tok/step into the same cap by step 41 ([errors/2026-09-06-the-rollouts-grew-into-the-cap.md](docs/experience/errors/2026-09-06-the-rollouts-grew-into-the-cap.md)). `--allow-short-rollouts` for the smoke recipes, which want the truncation. **Also fixes a defect in #125 the same run exposed:** `_write_eval_rows` returned early unless `runs/<id>/` existed, and `_finish` creates that directory AFTER both eval arms — so the per-problem rows shipped one PR ago wrote **nothing**, on every run. Gates in `tests/test_eval_rows.py`, including one asserting the guard is CALLED between the before-arm and the loop: every other assertion passes with the call site deleted. 330 passed. dead code: none introduced.
- **accept** — **the first CP op: K/V all-gather per full-attn layer, whose backward is a `reduce_scatter` and not the slice the design specified.** `docs/design-parallel.md` said "`reduce_scatter`-free since the backward of a gather is a slice", which is right for the vocab gather — each rank owns a distinct slice of the OUTPUT — and wrong under CP, where every rank's queries read every rank's keys and each rank's dK is a partial sum over the whole sequence. Measured against a dense single-rank reference before any op was written: at cp=4 slicing is off by **1.032 against a 1.78 peak (58% of full scale)**, summing matches to **2.4e-07**; cp=2 gives 0.649 vs 3.0e-08; dQ is local either way. The mechanism is visible in which positions each rank writes dK to — contiguous chunks at cp=4 give rank 0 positions 0..7 and rank 3 positions 0..31, so a slice keeps only rank r's own contribution to its own chunk. **Rank `cp-1`'s chunk is the one case the slice gets right**, which is how a spot check passes and hides it. **Chunks are zigzag, not contiguous:** a causal mask makes rank r score against chunks 0..r, and the gather is a barrier, so the slowest rank is the step; rank r takes chunks `r` and `2cp-1-r`. Scored q·k pairs at T=32: contiguous **[36, 100, 164, 228]** (6.3x) vs zigzag **[132, 132, 132, 132]** (1.0x) at cp=4. That puts the gathered K/V in **rank order, not sequence order**, so `dense_attention` now takes `q_pos`/`k_pos` — its `triu` assumed q and k are the same length, which fails the moment the queries are one chunk — and `k_pos` is derived locally from `(T, cp)` rather than gathered, since every rank can compute every rank's chunk. Gate `tests/cp_world2.py` (two gloo ranks, forward + all three gradients vs the unsplit reference: 3.0e-07 / 4.8e-07 / 4.8e-07 / 3.6e-07), with two controls that fail with **distinct signatures**: `--slice-bwd` breaks dK/dV only (2.216, 2.156) and leaves the forward and dQ exact, `--seq-mask` breaks all four. **Doc arithmetic recomputed rather than adjusted:** the backward doubles bytes and calls for *both* schemes, so the ring-vs-all-gather ratio is unchanged (cp=8: 224 MiB, ring 4816 µs vs all-gather 688 µs, still 7x); memory takes a flat **+12.5%** (gathered K/V live for all 16 layers, dK/dV one layer at a time), moving the ring crossover ~64K → **~58K** tokens. **Not established: CP is unusable on the 27B** — 48 of 64 layers are GDN and `_gdn` refuses `cp>1` rather than start a chunk from a zero state, so this is the attention half and the model-level claim waits on the GDN `(A, B)` all-gather; the paged path refuses `cp>1` too (serving holds whole sequences); world=2 CPU/gloo is the largest exercised, no NCCL, no 27B run (`pending-remote`), and no perf number — the collective is unbucketed and unoverlapped. [wins/2026-09-05-cp-attention-gather.md](docs/experience/wins/2026-09-05-cp-attention-gather.md)

- **fix** — **the MATH eval scored with the number matcher while the reward scored with `boxed_match`, so a wrong answer counted: `\\frac{3}{2}` against gold `\\frac{1}{2}` both read as last-number 2 and the arm reports 100%.** `--reward boxed` switched the training reward and never the eval; **One table, not two conditionals**: `eval.MATCHERS` maps the `--reward` name to the single matcher both the reward and the eval read, because a second `if args.reward` agreeing with the first is the shape that produced this. A gate asserts the table covers every `--reward` choice, so a third reward cannot ship half-wired. Also writes **one row per problem** to `runs/<id>/eval-{before,after}.jsonl` — P1's GSM8K comparison had to use the unpaired interval (+5.6 pts [+2.0, +9.2] at n=500) because only totals were kept, and McNemar needs the discordant pairs. `grpo-math-27b` gains the explicit `eval_max_new_tokens=2048` that `grpo-gsm8k-27b` already had, so the eval is not scored at the 512 rollout cap. **Level chosen by measurement, not guess:** sampled on the 27B, levels 3-5 score 75% (6/8) and level 5 alone **45.8% (11/24)** — GSM8K's 88.0% base is what made 81% of groups tie, so run 2 takes level 5. Gate `tests/test_eval_rows.py`; both halves controlled — ignoring the matcher fails the first test, dropping the rows fails the second. dead code: none introduced.
- **accept** — **the clip fix reached the accumulating path; the 27B's optimizer returns before it and was clipping by each shard's own statistics.** #126 made `clip_grad_norm` TP-global, and full-parameter SFT never calls it: `Adafactor` sets `streams = True`, applies each gradient as backward finalizes it, and clips by that **update's own RMS** — a whole-tensor quantity that a shard does not have. A peer asked whether that per-update clip was global; measured, it was not. **Adafactor tp=2 vs tp=1 on tiny: 32 tensors off by >1 bf16 ulp, worst 304911 ulp.** The same comparison with AdamW gives **0 tensors, 1.0 ulp** — AdamW is elementwise, has no such statistic, and structurally cannot show the bug, which is what proves this is the optimizer's and not the gradients'. **The audit is wider than the two RMS calls, and depends on which axis is sharded:** a mean along the sharded axis has only this rank's terms, one along the other axis is complete everywhere. `u.mean(dim=1)` is whole on a dim-0 shard and partial on dim-1; `u.mean(dim=0)` is the mirror; `r.mean()` is a scalar over all rows, so partial on dim-0 only; both RMS are over every element, so both axes hit them; `v` and the beta1 moment are elementwise and need nothing. That table is in `autograd.py` beside the code, because a bool cannot express "reduce `c` but not `r`" — `shard_dim(key)` returns the axis, and `tensor_parallel.py`'s `__main__` checks it against what `shard_params` actually did for all 59 params. **After the reduce: 2 tensors at 4.0 ulp**, not zero, and measured rather than waved at — the reduced inputs agree to 2.9e-07 (gradient ssq) and 6.1e-08 (param ssq), and Adafactor divides by `rsqrt` of those, so a few rounding steps come out the other side; the gate allows 4 ulp against a defect of 304911. The arm lives in `tests/tp_world2.py`, since the dp gate cannot see this at all — both its arms are tp=2, so a per-shard statistic cancels — and its `--local-stats` control fails on **both** axes (`down_proj`/`o_proj` dim-1 at 478 and 526 ulp, `gate_proj`/`k_proj` dim-0 at 195 and 171). **A third streaming optimizer turned up while fixing this:** changing the signature broke `tests/test_iso.py`, and `--optim iso` reparameterizes each 2D weight by **its own SVD** — a shard's singular vectors are not a slice of the full matrix's, so no reduce recovers them. ISO refuses a sharded param, and that refusal is a measured statement about a factorization rather than a guess (contrast the untested one earlier today); `test_iso_refuses_a_sharded_param` holds it and fails when the check is deleted. **Not done:** world=2 CPU/gloo, no NCCL, no 27B run (`pending-remote`); the reduce adds two small collectives per sharded tensor per step plus one for the factored mean, unmeasured and unbucketed. [errors/2026-09-05-adafactor-clipped-by-the-shard.md](docs/experience/errors/2026-09-05-adafactor-clipped-by-the-shard.md)

- **accept** — **dp gradients are averaged across replicas, and the first version issued its all-reduce in a different order on every rank.** `Mesh.dp_group()` shipped with #123 and nothing called it; `--tp` refused `dp>1` because no gradient reduce existed, so each replica would train on its own rows and diverge with finite plausible losses. `Backend.dp_reduce` all-reduces one gradient over the dp group and divides by `dp_world` — **mean, not sum**, since each replica's loss already averages its own rows and summing is a silent `dp_world`x learning-rate change. It runs **before the clip**, so the clipped norm is the whole batch's rather than each replica's own, the same failure the tp shards had. **The bug:** the loop was `for g in acc.values()`, and `acc` is keyed by `id(param)` in gradient-completion order — a per-rank property — so rank 0 all-reduced `down_proj` while rank 1 all-reduced `embed_tokens`. gloo **aborts the process** on the size mismatch (`EnforceNotMet` in `pair.cc:253`) instead of raising, so the only visible message is `process 1 terminated with signal SIGABRT` and no traceback points at the loop. Two earlier diagnoses were discarded by measurement, not argument: a second `mp.spawn` in a gloo-initialized process (the arm aborts alone, with one spawn) and a `MASTER_PORT` collision (distinct ports changed nothing). Fixed by iterating `sorted(params)`, the one order every rank agrees on. **I then turned that diagnosis into a refusal without testing it**, making `dp>1` with a streaming optimizer raise on the theory that the tape applies gradients in a per-rank order too. It does not: a probe found the tape's sequence **identical on all four ranks** (27 params), because it walks entries in record order, which is the model's graph. The refusal would have made dp unusable on the 27B, whose optimizer is Adafactor with streamed updates (`cli.py:240`; Adam's m+v is 200.4 GiB there). The streaming path now reduces each gradient where it applies it, and `_order_agrees` re-checks the assumption instead of trusting it — each rank hashes its apply order and all-gathers it over the dp group, once per step, behind `TILERL_CHECK_DP_ORDER` so gates pay for it and training does not. Gate `tests/dp_world4.py`: four gloo ranks as (dp=2, tp=2), each replica running a real `train_step` on its own half of a 2-row batch, compared against a dp=1 tp=2 step on the whole batch — **1.0 ulp** with AdamW and **1.0 ulp** with streamed Adafactor (each against a reference using the same optimizer, since a cross-optimizer comparison would measure the optimizers), against **83671 ulp** for the `--no-dp` control. **The `--scramble` control passed at first for a bug in itself:** it reversed one rank's order on `rank % 2`, but the check compares within the dp group, which for rank r is {r, r+2} — same parity, so it reversed both sides of every comparison. Keyed on `dp_rank` it reports. **Not done:** world=4 on CPU/gloo is the largest exercised, no NCCL and no 27B run (`pending-remote`), and the reduce is one call per gradient with no bucketing or backward overlap — unmeasured at 27B parameter count. [wins/2026-09-05-the-dp-gradient-average.md](docs/experience/wins/2026-09-05-the-dp-gradient-average.md)
- **accept** — **`--tp` selects a layout, and the clip norm it clips by was this shard's own.** The TP gradient gate stopped at `tape.backward`; the next statement in `_step` reads a number that spans the whole model. `clip_grad_norm` summed only the gradients this rank holds, so at tp=2 on tiny the two ranks got **2399.73 and 2288.83** — 4.85% apart — against the true **2641.80**, and with `max_norm=1.0` clipping fires every step, scaling the two halves of one weight matrix differently on every update. Summing the local totals across ranks is not the fix either: the replicated tensors are identical everywhere, so a plain all-reduce counts each `world` times and overshoots to **3316.24**. `is_sharded(key)` splits the two, one all-reduce of a 2-vector combines them, and both ranks now read **2641.796301** against world=1's 2641.795815 (1.8e-7, and identical to each other). **A LoRA pair splits on one side only and the side flips** — on a column-parallel base `lora_b` is narrow and `lora_a` is full, on a row-parallel base the mirror — so the first classifier was wrong on 3 of the 6 adapter tensors per layer, which is exactly what `--tp` with `--rl`/`--opd` trains. **Two things nearly made the new gate vacuous:** comparing bf16 weights absolutely cannot work, since one rounding step is 7.8e-3 at magnitude 1, so any absolute tolerance is either tighter than one ulp (2 tensors read as failures) or four steps wide — the comparison is in ulps, and the `--local-clip` control then shows 8 tensors wrong, up to **261 ulp** on `embed_tokens`, against 0 for the fixed arm; and the refusal check runs in the parent after the spawned arms set `MASTER_ADDR`, so a wrongly-accepted dp=2 would **hang** in `init_process_group(world_size=4)` rather than report — its probe backend raises on `init_tp` instead of joining. `--tp` refuses dp>1 outright: no dp gradient all-reduce exists anywhere in the tree and `DataParallelEngine` is a serving object (N engines on N devices in one process), not this axis, so such a run would train a different model per replica with plausible losses and no error. **Not done:** the K/V-replica case (`replicas > 1`) is unmeasured — tiny at tp=4 is refused by `linear_num_value_heads=2` and the 27B needs tp=8 on hardware; cpu/gloo world=2 is the largest exercised; no 27B run (`pending-remote`). 310 passed. dead code: none introduced. [errors/2026-09-05-the-clip-norm-was-this-shards-own.md](docs/experience/errors/2026-09-05-the-clip-norm-was-this-shards-own.md)
- **accept** — **a `(dp, tp, cp)` mesh, and the tp all-reduce now stays inside its dp replica.** `init_tp(world, rank)` treated the whole world as one TP group, which is right for pure TP and wrong the moment `dp > 1`: an ungrouped `all_reduce` sums across the dp replicas and averages away the independence dp exists for. `Mesh(dp, tp, cp, rank)` carries the design's **cp-fastest** layout so a tp group is contiguous; `cp>1` raises, because the axis has to be in the arithmetic (renumbering every rank later is worse) but no context-parallel op exists and a mesh accepting `cp=2` would run tp-only math on a sequence it claims to have split. `init_tp` takes **every** tp group, not just this rank's — `dist.new_group` is itself collective, so a rank that skips one deadlocks the first time it is used. **The first gate was vacuous and its own control caught it:** comparing dp0's and dp1's gradients looked like the test for a leaked all-reduce, but `embed_tokens` keeps a local scatter term over each replica's own ids, so they differ (max|d| 3.4e+02) even with the collective wrongly spanning all four ranks — the `--ungrouped` control PASSED. Replaced with a direct probe on the collective, which has no local term to hide behind: grouped gives `{0:1, 1:1, 2:5, 3:5}`, ungrouped gives `{0:6, 1:6, 2:6, 3:6}`, and the control then fails. **Not done:** nothing selects a mesh yet — no flag, no recipe field, and `DataParallelEngine` still builds replicas independently of it; world=4 on CPU is the largest exercised; no 27B run (`pending-remote`). 310 passed. dead code: none introduced. [wins/2026-09-05-the-mesh.md](docs/experience/wins/2026-09-05-the-mesh.md)
- **accept** — **six `/v1/messages` completions came back 1, 1, 1, 1, 4 and 8 tokens long with `stop_reason: max_tokens`, and attributing that took a token decode plus arithmetic no row carried.** The cap is a `min(req.max_tokens, budget)` whose other operand is derived (`engine_limit - prompt_len`), so a short completion says nothing about which side bound; establishing that `budget` was five digits — 32755, 32644, 32303, 31847, 31165, 29290 — and therefore that the client's own `max_tokens` was the cap needed `cli.py`'s `--max-ctx` read by hand and the completion ids decoded to see they were the model's thinking monologue cut mid-word ("The", "The user has sent a very long message"). Every operand is now in the row: `asked_max_tokens`, `budget`, `engine_limit`, `prompt_len`, `effective_max_tokens`, plus `stream` and `thinking`, which change the prompt. Gate `test_the_record_says_which_operand_capped_the_completion` asserts all six exist and that `effective_max_tokens` equals one of the two operands; deleting `budget` from `_record` fails it by name. **The other half of this is an errors entry, not a fix:** those six rows were served by a tree I had been restarting all afternoon with my own uncommitted DRAM tier in it (`5bfbfe9 dirty 5`, `dram_demotions: 108`, three restarts in one hour). "Is the GPU free" and "is anyone using the service" are different questions and `nvidia-smi` answers only the first; the endpoint is no longer a measurement rig. [errors/2026-09-05-the-endpoint-a-user-was-using-was-my-rig.md](docs/experience/errors/2026-09-05-the-endpoint-a-user-was-using-was-my-rig.md)
- **accept** — **the vocab-parallel loss reduces to scalars per row instead of gathering a 1.89 GiB logit tensor.** A sharded `lm_head` leaves each rank with `[B, T, V/world]`, and reusing the unsharded CE means all-gathering to `[B, T, 248320]` in f32: a row is **0.947 MiB** at the 27B's vocab, so **1.89 GiB per rank per step at B=8, T=256** (3.79 GiB at T=512), on top of the weights and the tape. Computed for this op rather than borrowed — the 08-30 OOM cited elsewhere as 8.5 GiB was three `(3072, 248320)` f32 tensors from `lm_head` over every position at B=32, a different failure. `cross_entropy_sharded` instead takes a local max and local sum-exp and combines them with **three `[B*T]` all-reduces** (max, sum, and one that selects the target column, which lives on exactly one rank and contributes zero elsewhere); the gradient comes back already sharded. At V=248320 that is 248320:1 in bytes moved. **The memory claim gets its own control, and bytes could not carry it:** `tracemalloc` reports 80 bytes for a 4 MB torch tensor, so allocated-byte assertions measure nothing on CPU — measured before relying on it. A `TorchFunctionMode` records the widest last dimension any op produces, and `--gather` runs the all-gather implementation, which is **numerically correct** and fails on that assertion alone (`formed a tensor 64 wide, shard is 32`). Restores `Backend.tp_rank`, which #106 deleted as unread, with a comment naming the reader. Adds `forward(sharded_logits=)`: serving gathers because it samples from a full row, training does not. **Not done:** the mesh, so nothing yet selects a (dp, tp, cp) layout; gate runs world=2 only; no 27B number (`pending-remote`). 309 passed. dead code: none introduced. [wins/2026-09-05-sharded-cross-entropy.md](docs/experience/wins/2026-09-05-sharded-cross-entropy.md)
- **accept** — **seven negative controls written in one day were vacuous: each passed with its guard removed, by a second route the author did not know existed.** A second module object (`importlib` builds a new one, so a monkeypatched `bh._BASELINE` never reached the copy the code executes), cached bytecode, an assertion true whether or not the parent waited, a block-aligned warm length that let the whole-prompt publish stand in for the mid-chunk one (150 instead of 144 isolates it: `% 16` publishes 3, `% 64` publishes 0), and a re-measurement that hardcoded the first measurement's categories and so could only agree, and a CI step whose zsh subshell ate the gate's exit 1 and reported 0 — the gate found the defect, printed it, and the harness above it called that a pass. Four were caught by deleting the guard and running; the fifth by `--load-adapter` refusing 1090 unknown keys; the sixth by running the same command under bash; the seventh, the only one caught before shipping, by replacing a compared tensor that carried a local term (`embed_tokens` differs between DP replicas whether or not the collective is grouped) with a rank-valued probe that has none. **All seven were downstream of the defect** — the assertion sat where something else could contribute to the same number; a control must be sensitive to the defect, not merely downstream of it. **Rule: run every negative control in the failing state at least once and record that it failed** — delete the guard, clear `__pycache__`, confirm FAIL, restore, confirm PASS. Two of five written that day would otherwise have shipped as proof. [errors/2026-09-05-a-control-that-cannot-fail.md](docs/experience/errors/2026-09-05-a-control-that-cannot-fail.md)
- **accept** — **two more mechanisms of the SSD tier had no gate: the one word worth 36 percentage points, and a `drop()` that resurrects the prefix it dropped.** Continuing the mutation sweep: (1) `spill=False` on the mid-chunk publish IS the difference between 8.96% and 45.3%, and dropping the kwarg left all 328 tests passing — a one-word regression invisible locally, since the number is measured on a machine this suite never runs on. Gated at `prefix_published >= 3, ssd_offered == 1` over a 48-token budget and 240 tokens; without it, 5 and 5, the same 5.0x byte waste seen on the 27B. Both operands, because the warm-up length decides whether either is reachable: a ragged one never fires the prompt-complete publish, so the expected offer count is 0 and "only the last spilled" cannot be told from "nothing spilled" — the first draft of that test was exactly that, and its own assertion caught it. (2) `drop()` clears the pending tables and unlinks, but the daemon is already past that point holding the blob: its `torch.save` completes AFTER the drop and rewrites the file the drop removed, so `_flush_loop` re-checks `table.get(k) is blob` and unlinks again. **Two guards, and the numbers I first published belonged to the wrong one**: forcing `still_pending` True indexes the dropped key (`ssd_entries` **0 → 1**, so a lookup can serve it — after `invalidate()`, KV from the previous weights), while deleting only the `os.remove(dst)` leaves the index at **0** and one unpaired `.kv` that the next `_recover` drops anyway, since it adopts by pair. The mutation I ran replaced the whole `if still_pending` block and so removed both, and I attributed the resurrection to the unlink; **codex caught it reviewing #132**. Fixed by one assertion per guard, ordered **index first** — pytest stops at the first failure and both mutations leave a file, so asserting the file first makes the index assertion unreachable in every arm, which is precisely why the wrong claim stood: the assertion whose message said "indexed again" never executed in the run I quoted. Now verified with three separate mutations, each failing its own assertion. **That gate's own first two versions were both broken, in opposite directions:** waiting with `_flushed` polls the pending tables `drop()` had just emptied, so it returned before the save finished and **the negative control passed**; waiting on `_q.unfinished_tasks == 0` can never succeed because nothing calls `task_done()`, which gave the right answer after **6.46 s of pure timeout**. A wait already satisfied and a wait never satisfiable are the same defect and neither reports a failure. Waits on the save actually in flight instead — one, not two, since the state half is skipped at the `is blob` check without calling save — then polls the directory: 0.01 s, control red. **And one mutation that survived is redundancy, not a gap:** the eviction victim search's pending-skip filters 6 of 12 candidates in a forced-eviction probe, yet orphan files, half pairs and byte accounting come out identical with it deleted, because the `is blob` re-check covers the same race a layer down. Left in place and recorded — "the suite stays green" means either a missing gate or dead weight, and only comparing observable state says which. 330 passed. [wins/2026-09-05-the-ssd-tiers-read-path-did-not-exist.md](docs/experience/wins/2026-09-05-the-ssd-tiers-read-path-did-not-exist.md)
- **accept** — **three of the SSD tier's four gates each covered one branch of a two-branch fact, and all three were found by mutating rather than by reading.** A peer deleted `load_kv`'s truncated-file `except` and the test still passed: `_fault_in` reads the state first and returns on its miss, so truncating every spill file can only ever reach `load_state`. Two more of the same shape fell out of mutating the rest: `recovered == 0 and lookup() is None` on a fingerprint mismatch is **one claim written as two** (a mismatch indexes nothing, so `resident()` is False, so the lookup half cannot fail while the first holds) and it never asserted the mismatch **unlinks** — deleting that loop leaves all 327 tests green, while it is the tier's only disk reclamation, since `invalidate()` writes one marker instead of walking 20 GiB inside a training step; and `resident()` is `_lru or (pending and pending_st)` with only the `_lru` operand tested, so the **~100 ms every entry spends in the queue** — no file on disk, both loads reading the pending table — was unasserted, and getting it wrong silently re-prefills a prefix that is in memory. Fixes: the truncation test is parametrized one arm per suffix and the `.kv` arm asserts `ssd_faults == 1`, since "it did not raise" is also satisfied by never getting there; the mismatch is asserted on the **directory listing**, because `recovered` is 0 either way; the queue window is gated by blocking `torch.save`, asserting no file exists yet, then asserting the probe and both loads serve from memory. **Six negative controls, each failing only its own assertion** (drop `load_kv`'s guard → `.kv` alone; drop `load_state`'s → `.st` alone; drop the unlink → "left 2 spill files"; drop the `or` operand; drop each load's pending read). The generalization is not "test both operands of an `or`": these were an early `return`, a side effect beside a return value, and an `or` — what they share is one fact needing two assertions where a plausible test satisfies one. Also: `pod_sync.sh`'s PATH export lived inside the `run` branch only, so the plain entry point ran the container's tilelang 0.1.8 while `run` ran 0.1.13 from the uv venv — one script, two environments, and the plain path is what a quick check uses. 328 passed. [wins/2026-09-05-the-ssd-tiers-read-path-did-not-exist.md](docs/experience/wins/2026-09-05-the-ssd-tiers-read-path-did-not-exist.md)
- **accept, off by default** — **write-through cost 45.3% of a prefill and now costs 8.96%; four guesses at why, three wrong, and the stage split settled it.** Offering every publish to the disk tier put a synchronous pageable D2H inside the prefill that published it: measured on H20 card 6, one 2729-token prompt, `--ssd-path` the only variable, arms alternated, `ssd_hits` 0 in both arms — **+0.925 s on a 2.041 s request**. Two mechanisms, both in the tier. A **GDN snapshot is a CONSTANT ~157 MB at every prefix length** (four .st files 156896685-156901293 B, a 4.6 KB spread), so the prompt's six publishes spilled 941 MB of state + 683 MB of KV = **1624 MB to serve one 325 MB entry, 5.0x the bytes that can ever be read**; `insert(..., spill=False)` on intermediate chunk boundaries keeps them in HBM and takes it to 325 MB. And `.cpu()` is a synchronous pageable copy, replaced by a pinned destination with `non_blocking=True` and a recorded event the flush daemon waits on. **The four guesses:** `cudaHostAlloc` per spill — **wrong**, a (numel,dtype) buffer pool measured *worse* at 0.421 s against 0.383 s with the lengths repeating so the pool was hitting; pool too shallow — **wrong the same way**, a two-slot arena measured **1.640 s** because six publishes outrun a depth the daemon frees only after `torch.save` and 16 of 18 spills fell back to pageable; the 171-slice `torch.stack` gather — **wrong**, stage timers put it at **1 ms**; the D2H copy — **right**, `ssd_copy_ms` **170 ms of a 180 ms** cost. **Both hand-written pinned pools were deleted**: torch's `CachingHostAllocator` already reuses pinned blocks and both layers on top of it lost to it, which is the rule this leaves — do not pool above torch's pinned allocator. Shipped: last publish only, pinned, **+0.180 s = 8.96%**, n=4, spread 0.025/0.088. `--ssd-path` **stays off by default** — 8.96% on every publishing request against 1.821x recovered once per restart is a deployment property, not something this bench settles. `ssd_gather_ms`/`ssd_copy_ms` stay on `/health` so the split is read rather than guessed a fifth time. Also fixed: the restart bench re-sent an identical prompt, making the longest entry a full-length match that `_match_prefix` treats as a miss — turn 2 now appends a follow-up, which is what a chat client does. 320 passed. [wins/2026-09-05-the-ssd-tiers-read-path-did-not-exist.md](docs/experience/wins/2026-09-05-the-ssd-tiers-read-path-did-not-exist.md)
- **accept** — **the SSD prefix tier had no read path at all, and the first three numbers for the one I built measured the host page cache.** `spill_kv`/`spill_state` had a caller in `PrefixStore.insert`; `load_kv`, `load_state` and `has` had **zero call sites anywhere in the tree** (grepped, not inferred), so every lookup missed by construction and the disk accumulated bytes nothing read. `lookup` now asks the tier at each prefix length it fails to match in memory, and a hit faults the prefix into fresh blocks and hands it to `insert` — one code path owns retain, eviction and byte accounting afterwards. The candidate filter is `resident()`, a dict probe: without it a 2729-token query pays one or two `torch.load`s **per prefix length** to answer what the index already answers, and the same probe stops a fault-in re-spilling its own read (`ssd_offered == 0`, asserted). Wired as `--ssd-path`, keyed on **every** config dataclass field — the hand-picked first draft named `cfg.num_heads`, which does not exist (`num_attention_heads`), so the list was wrong when written and a missing field is how a restart serves KV from other weights. **Two scenarios, both measured on H20 card 6:** a process restart empties HBM and leaves the host page cache alone, so the fault-in legitimately reads from memory — **1.738-1.821x** over four consecutive runs (1.690-1.732 s against a control's 3.004-3.087 s); a host reboot or an evicted spill pays the disk — **1.543-1.565x**, composed from a measured 1.756 s standalone read (320.6 MiB at 182.6 MiB/s) plus 0.188 s of prefill for the 169 tokens the hit did not cover. Break-even bandwidth **112.3 MiB/s** against the device's 182.6, i.e. 1.60x of headroom. **The instrument was wrong three times and every arm-order control passed each time.** 2.585x came from a 6-compile JIT gap in the cold window alone (fixed with a throwaway JIT-warming start); then 1.738x and 1.821x read as disk numbers while 320.6 MiB at 182.6 MiB/s is 1.756 s against a 1.690 s arm — the arm was faster than its own disk traffic, so it never did it. What caught it was dividing bytes by measured bandwidth, not another arm; fsync+fadvise per file only moved the probe from 4477.8 MiB/s to ~509, since DONTNEED skips pages nothing else references, so the disk figure is composed rather than chased. Also recorded: I wrote `3` to `/proc/sys/vm/drop_caches` on a shared host before reasoning about it, evicting other teams' cached weights. Controls: empty-tier directory 0.987-1.030x, compiles 0 in every window, saved 46.9-47.4% of a 2.85 s ceiling from the actual 2560-token match. 317 passed. [wins/2026-09-05-the-ssd-tiers-read-path-did-not-exist.md](docs/experience/wins/2026-09-05-the-ssd-tiers-read-path-did-not-exist.md) · [errors/2026-09-05-the-ssd-benchmark-never-touched-the-ssd.md](docs/experience/errors/2026-09-05-the-ssd-benchmark-never-touched-the-ssd.md)
- **phase exit** — **TP training's tape half lands: sharded gradients now match unsharded, gated by 54 tensors on two gloo ranks.** `_BWD` registered no collective, so every shipped collective was inference-only and nothing in the tree trained sharded. Three entries, not the design's two: `all_reduce` (identity backward), `all_gather` (backward keeps this rank's chunk), and **`tp_fork`** — identity forward, all-reduce backward — the dual `docs/design-parallel.md` did not name, where the column-parallel dX sum lives. **The gate compares all 54 gradient tensors, sharded included**, by shipping the world=1 gradients through `shard_params` and requiring rank r's to equal slice r; the obvious version compares only the nine replicated params and passes with every sharded gradient wrong. rtol 2e-3 against a measured bf16 spread of 1.4e-4, so a factor-of-`world` miss clears it by four orders; `--no-fork` deletes the collective and the gate FAILS. **Four defects it caught, none findable by reading:** `all_reduce` returned its own input, so backward wrote the gradient under `id(output)` and popped it as consumed — dX came back `None`, silently (`Tape.record` now refuses any op whose output is an input, a tape-wide hazard); the **tied head must not fork**, since `tie_word_embeddings=True` makes it the replicated embedding table where every rank has the whole dX — forking scaled the entire model below it, 54 of 54 wrong at sum-ratio exactly **2.0000**; `q_norm`/`k_norm`/`gdn_norm` are replicated weights over **sharded** heads, so each rank holds a partial sum (confirmed directly: rank0+rank1 == world=1, exactly); and **`mp.Manager` deadlocks on torch tensors** — 0% CPU on every rank for four minutes, while the same probe returns in 3 s with `.tolist()`. The loss was correct in every one of these runs. **Not done:** the mesh and the sharded CE; no 27B number (`pending-remote`). 309 passed. dead code: none introduced. [wins/2026-09-05-tp-training-tape-half.md](docs/experience/wins/2026-09-05-tp-training-tape-half.md)
- **accept** — **every bench run has been raising its own baseline, so 38 SOTA rows were self-ratcheting.** `Gate.check` wrote `self.baseline[key]` on a beat and `finish()` saved it, which means a fast run promoted itself and the next slow run was then measured against the record it had just replaced — a regression cannot be seen once the thing it regressed from is gone. A beat is now a **candidate**: printed, and written to `runs/<id>/baseline-candidate.json` for review, never into `bench-baseline.json`. Adds `_timing_snapshot` at the end of every train so a run's speed is compared without anyone typing it. **Recorded as steps/second, not seconds/step:** every row is `tok_s` and all three comparisons are `>`, so raw seconds make a run twice as SLOW read as a new record — the gate asserts exactly that inversion. **Two defects found in this change by its own controls:** the first version seeded on a missing key, and since pytest runs `grpo-tiny-smoke`, **five junk `train-run/tiny-*` rows landed in the tracked json**; and the test written to catch that was **vacuous** — `_timing_snapshot` loads the harness through `importlib`, so monkeypatching `bh._BASELINE` never reached the copy it executes, and the test passed with the guard removed. It now asserts on the real file. dead code: none introduced.

- **default flip** — **sm70 was running the CPU cell's `gdn_prep`, where every one of 128 threads computed the same 128 columns: the 11K request goes 163.1 s → 100.2 s, 1.63x.** Found by profiling a prefill tick for unrelated reasons — `gdn_prep` was **53.5% of its GPU time** (3121 of 5831 ms, 48 calls, 65 ms/layer at T=2048). **The expected culprit was 1.5%:** sm70 has no `gdn_state_scan`, so the chunkwise-WY core runs as `reference.gdn_chunk_core`, a torch bmm loop, and I predicted that would dominate — it is 87 ms, because those bmms are large matrix multiplies. `_resolve` showed the real cause: sm70's `gdn_prep` maker and sm90's are **different objects**, sm70 getting `kernels.make_gdn_prep`, whose body wraps `T.serial(DK)` loops while the launch passes `threads=128` and the body binds no thread index. Separated redundancy from read traffic by holding the work fixed: **threads=1 is 4.89x faster than threads=128 doing identical work** (54.04 vs 264.33 ms), while halving the conv taps moves it only 1.51x — so the fix is the schedule, not the memory pattern. sm70 now takes sm90's one-thread-per-column schedule with the IO dtype parameterised (`make_gdn_prep_bf16(t, "float32")`), **not a third copy** — the two cells differ in six dtype literals and nothing else; the CPU cell stays as the CPU twin. Kernel **66.49 → 0.17 ms at T=512 (397.9x)**, prefill tick **5830.7 → 2773.5 ms (2.10x)** against a **2.15x Amdahl bound** from prep's own share, and the live 11019-token request **163.1 → 100.2 s warm** (14.68 → 9.00 ms per prompt token), first-request JIT 19.4 → 0.5 s, peak VRAM 27036 → 26668 MiB. Parity PASS on sm70 vs `reference.gdn_prep` (worst `max|delta|` 1.192e-07) and vs the old kernel (1.907e-06). **398x is the shape of a kernel that did not run, so that was checked**: all six outputs 100% non-zero with sane magnitudes. `fp4 gemv` is now 77.1% of the tick. **Not reconciled:** the tick says 2.10x and the server 1.63x; the gap is work outside the profiled window (HTTP, template, tokenize, sampling, chunking) and is not attributed — both are reported as measured rather than one derived from the other. `docs/support-matrix.md`'s sm70 row goes `(23,1,8,14)` → `(23,2,8,13)`, caught immediately by the test guarding that table. [wins/2026-09-05-sm70-ran-the-cpu-cells-gdn-prep.md](docs/experience/wins/2026-09-05-sm70-ran-the-cpu-cells-gdn-prep.md)
- **reject** — **a vulture sweep at 60% named 11 dead symbols; 10 had live callers, and both of its 100%-confidence hits were load-bearing.** Verified by call-site grep on `9b3b88f`: `rmsnorm_bwd`, `attn_prelude`, `iso_merge`, `is_shared`, `write_block` and `roles` are called from `tests/`; `_trunk_logits`/`_verify_chains` and `init_tp` from `scripts/`; `allow_tf32` sets two different torch flags (`backends.cuda.matmul` and `backends.cudnn`), not one twice. The two **100%** hits were `types` in `__torch_dispatch__` and `__torch_function__` — **required positional parameters of a torch protocol**, so removing them fails at call time, not import. One real find, deleted: **`tp_rank`** is written at `backend.py:141,152` and read nowhere (`tp_world` is read in five places). The seven scripts nothing references all survive `scripts/README.md`'s rule and stay: the five `fla`/state-scan probes back `errors/2026-08-29-gdn-state-scan-port-wip.md`, whose status is UNFINISHED and parked, `pod_matrix.py` is a launcher (explicitly exempt), and `bench_h2d.py` landed with #100 hours earlier. The rule this adds to `scripts/README.md`: a scanner's confidence ranks how sure its parse is, not how sure the repo is — grep `tests/` and `scripts/` for every candidate first. dead code: removed 1 symbol (`tp_rank`).
- **accept** — **GSM8K is too easy for the no-think 27B, so run 2 moves to MATH; the number matcher would have rewarded wrong answers on it.** P1 measured `tied_group_fraction` climbing 0.50 (steps 1-10) → 0.50 (11-20) → 0.87 (21-35), **22 of 23 tied steps at reward 1.0** — the ceiling, reproducing [P1 ties at the ceiling](docs/experience/errors/2026-09-03-p1-ties-at-the-ceiling.md) (0.86, gate FAILED). Second independent measurement after the 09-04 control's 96.6% of training prompts solved at cap 2048. Adds `scripts/math_jsonl.py` (hendrycks MATH, `--level` filter, `--seed` shuffle, rows with no `\boxed{}` dropped rather than given an empty answer — an unscorable row is a permanent tie), `src/tilerl/math_answer.py` (brace-balanced `extract_boxed`, normalise, `boxed_match`) and the `grpo-math-27b` recipe. **The matcher is not a convenience:** `eval.last_number` reads the *last* number, so `\frac{1}{2}` parses to **2.0** — and `\frac{3}{2}` does too, which means a wrong rollout scores 1.0 against a `\frac{1}{2}` gold. `--reward number|boxed` selects; the gate asserts the two matchers disagree on exactly that pair, so a flag choosing an equivalent function fails it. Exact match after normalisation, never a float compare — `\frac{1}{3}` and 0.333 are different answers. **The prompt carries the boxing instruction** and that is load-bearing, not cosmetic: `render_chat` (`prompt.py:18`) emits bare ChatML and the recipe runs thinking off, so nothing downstream asks for a box — without it a no-think model boxes nothing, every rollout scores 0 and every group ties at the **floor**, which `--level` cannot fix because it is not a difficulty problem. It lives in the generator so the JSONL is self-contained across `--data`, `--eval-gsm8k` and a re-score. Two negative controls, each failing alone: recipe to `reward="number"` FAILS, dropping the instruction append FAILS. 303 passed. dead code: none introduced.
- **default flip** — **`tilerl train --rl` and self-OPD now keep the decode graph and recapture it after every update: the 27B GRPO step goes 73.62 → 34.09 s, 2.16x, and a 100-step run 123 → 57 min.** #94 made a kept graph recoverable; this measures what it is worth and turns it on. Four arms on card 6 (27B NVFP4, group=8, gen=256), **each run in both orders** because the arms share a process, step 0 dropped, n=10 pooled per arm: baseline 73.62 ± 1.59, prefix 74.83 (+1.21 s, 0.98x), graph **34.09 ± 0.92**, both 34.09. `graph` and `both` pool identical — the prefix store's cost vanishes once the graph is on. **And it is not fast because it is stale:** with graphs held (`graphs_held_before_update=1`, so the check is not vacuous) a zero-perturbation control reproduces the rollout exactly while a real perturbation changes it, so a recaptured graph resamples under new weights. `clear_prefix` stays OFF: the store publishes **739 entries and serves 0** in every prefix arm, both orders, because publish is gated on `materialized %% BLOCK_TOKENS == 0` AND decode phase (`engine.py:1100`), so the 8 rollouts of a group never share their prefill — it waits for v100's block-granular store. **Two measurement errors are recorded rather than dropped:** the forward order alone gave prefix +3.16 s at t=7.2 with a tight 95% CI, which the reversed order does not replicate (73.38 ± 2.71, declining monotonically across four steps) — pooled it is +1.21 s at t≈2, and the wrong number reached the board before the control ran; and on cpu the same ordering effect is 400–1500x, whichever arm runs first, which read in one order says "baseline costs 40 s/step and every waiver is free". Gate `test_the_training_engine_keeps_its_decode_graph` asserts both halves — graphs on AND the waiver passed — since either alone is a silent regression; negative control fails. Baseline row `train/27b-grpo-step-g8-t256/sm90` = 34.09 in `bench-baseline.json`. [wins/2026-09-05-recapture-after-update.md](docs/experience/wins/2026-09-05-recapture-after-update.md)

- **find** — **the `_pad2d` cost is in the BACKWARD at M=74, not the rollout, and my analytic pass was wrong in both directions.** Every `_pad2d` call on one real 27B GRPO step, bucketed by site and M: **175,424 real pads copying 40.1 G elements**, plus 192,344 no-ops (52% of calls already return their argument). Forward 14.4 G / backward **25.7 G**; M=74 alone is **23.0 G = 57%** and appears at four `autograd.py` sites and **zero** forward sites. The `M=8 → 16` rollout row pad I predicted was the lever is 4.8 G = **12%**. Activation-shaped M is 74.5%, weight-shaped 25.5% — so my first pass ("every real pad is an activation") and my corrected pass ("weights are ~38%") are both wrong. **No fix proposed:** 40.1 G is a volume and 14.77% is a time, and nothing here shows they distribute alike; revisit against #101's phase seconds on the MATH run. [errors/2026-09-05-the-pad-cost-is-in-the-backward.md](docs/experience/errors/2026-09-05-the-pad-cost-is-in-the-backward.md)
- **default flip** — **the prefix cache never hit on chat traffic because a prompt whose length is not block-aligned published nothing at all: 3265 tokens go 20.63 s → 14.19 s, 1.45x, and the live server goes 0 hits to 13.** `_finish_prefills` published only when the WHOLE prompt length was block-aligned (`prompt_len % BLOCK_TOKENS == 0`), and **15 of every 16 real prompt lengths are not** — the live V100 reported `prefix_hits: 0` with `prefix_published: 4`, all four from the decode path, none from prefill. The gate was not arbitrary: the state pool is exact only at a chunk end, and a truncated entry pairs KV for N tokens with a state that absorbed more. Fixed by cutting the **first** prefill chunk at a `_PREFILL_BUCKET` (64) boundary, so the chunk end IS a publish point where the pool is exact; the tail becomes a second forward, a launch and not extra tokens. `_PREFILL_BUCKET == _WY_CHUNK == 64`, so the width bucket does not move and the `Divide by zero` an earlier chunk-shrink attempt caused cannot recur. **Two things were NOT the answer and cost the most time.** Blockwise storage at 64 granularity: `lookup` already returns the longest prefix at any length — probed with a 100-token query against entries stored at 16..128 it returns `length=96`, so matching never required the whole entry to be a prefix and the granularity was already **16, four times finer than the 64 that was specified**. Per-boundary state snapshots: a GDN snapshot is **144 MiB** at 27B, an 11K prompt has 172 boundaries at stride 64 = **24.2 GiB against 1.43 GiB of KV for the same span, 16.9x independent of length**, on a card with 5.7 GiB free after weights. Boundary-state export from the kernel was written and verified (four boundaries against fresh prefix runs, `max|delta|` **0.000e+00**) and then **reverted** — splitting the chunk reaches the same aligned publish point with no kernel, backend, or 48-layer shadow-pool change. **Measured on the card**, one arm per server restart with `prefix_published == 0` asserted at entry: cold 20.63 s (6.32 ms/tok) vs warm 14.19 s (4.35 ms/tok) on the identical 3265-token prompt, and the consistency check is what makes it credible — 6.44 s saved is **31% against a 33% shareable fraction**, so the implied cost on the shared span is 5.90 ms/token against 6.32 overall. Cumulative over two 4-turn runs: **13 hits / 3 misses** where the same server previously reported 0 / 2. **A measurement was thrown away:** the first cold/warm script ran both arms in one process and reported **10.312x** — the cold arm publishes entries covering its whole prompt, so the warm arm matched its own earlier self and measured re-sending an identical prompt; 10.3x on a 33%-shareable workload is the tell, and the script now takes `--arm` and asserts an empty store. Gate `test_a_ragged_prompt_publishes_and_its_state_matches_no_store` compares the restored GDN state against a `NoPrefixStore` engine's, **not just the output** — an earlier attempt at this was byte-identical while the snapshot's norm was 74.0 against a correct 39.75, because argmax over a vocabulary absorbs a 1.86x state error. Both arms must be read at the same `prefill_from`; reading after one tick puts them at 164 and 128 and reports 66.5 vs 43.75, which is a measurement error and not a defect. Negative control on `origin/main` in a detached worktree with `__pycache__` cleared: `published 0`, fails at the first assert. [wins/2026-09-05-a-ragged-prompt-length-published-nothing.md](docs/experience/wins/2026-09-05-a-ragged-prompt-length-published-nothing.md) · [errors/2026-09-05-the-benchmark-measured-its-own-earlier-self.md](docs/experience/errors/2026-09-05-the-benchmark-measured-its-own-earlier-self.md)

- **accept, off by default** — **a DRAM snapshot tier that pays only above 9 concurrent sessions, and the verdict I published first was wrong.** Under `state_bytes` pressure the store now demotes the LRU GDN snapshot to pinned host memory and **keeps the entry** — same tokens, same blocks, still in the index — so a hit promotes it back instead of re-prefilling; entries are still evicted outright when the **block pool** is the pressure, the one case a snapshot tier cannot help. Why the snapshot and not the KV: measured on the live V100, **43 of 54 published entries were evicted with 64% of the block pool still free**, so every eviction was state bytes, discarding a reusable prefix to reclaim a byte the host could hold (144 MiB per snapshot at 27B against 2.125 MiB per block). **The condition is `concurrent sessions > HBM snapshot budget`, both operands**: rotating N conversations against a 9-snapshot budget gives 2 → 0 promotions, 4 → 0, **9 → 17, 12 → 24, 20 → 40**, and at 12 sessions the tier is the whole difference — **0 hits / 63 evictions without it, 24 hits / 0 evictions with it**. Below the threshold it is pure overhead: one conversation gave 43 demotions, **0 promotions and 1.51x worse wall clock**; two interleaved gave 16 and 0. So `dram_bytes` defaults to **0** — correct for the single-session endpoint this pod runs, wrong above 9 sessions, and the flag is the only thing that separates them. **I first called it structurally dead** — "demotion picks the LRU end and a lookup always wants the MRU one" — which holds *within* one conversation and fails *across* them, where the LRU end **is** another session's newest entry; the sweep I argued from held sessions at 2, the same two-variable relation with the wrong operand pinned, and a one-axis sweep of a two-axis condition reads exactly like a law. The gate asserts **both halves** for that reason. **Two instruments erred toward my conclusion:** an idle-card probe put a 144 MiB pinned D2H copy at 11.55 ms against **161.9 ms in situ (14.0x)**, because a demotion lands mid-prefill where it contends for the link and forces a sync — and even that attributes only **7.0 s of a 68.7 s** regression, the rest being contention in the prefill it interrupts that a per-call timer cannot see. Neither number was load-bearing; the promotion count was. An empty promotion **drops the entry rather than serving it** (blocks without a snapshot run the GDN layers from a zero state over non-zero KV — wrong, and silent in the way the truncated-entry bug was). `clear()` needs no new path: it drives `_drop`, which calls the tier's `forget`, so an optimizer step invalidates DRAM with HBM. `/health` gains `dram_*` counters including the in-situ millisecond totals. [errors/2026-09-05-a-two-variable-condition-read-as-a-dead-end.md](docs/experience/errors/2026-09-05-a-two-variable-condition-read-as-a-dead-end.md) · [docs/design-kv-tier.md](docs/design-kv-tier.md)

- **accept** — **a `--data` file that exists but holds no rows started a 100-step GRPO run on random prompts.** `_jsonl` returned `[]` and `cmd_train`'s `prompts = [...] or [torch.randint(...)]` (`cli.py:263`) substituted 8 random token sequences, so the run trained, scored, wrote a manifest and reported a reward — against noise. Caught while staging the P1 launch command, not by a test. `_jsonl` now exits naming the file. This is the case #99's flag check cannot reach: there the flag is missing, here it is present and the path resolves. Negative control: with the guard reverted the new test does not fail fast — it runs a full 97-second training run to completion, which is the defect.
- **fix** — **the eval cap was documented on 09-04 and still shipped, so the live P1 scored itself at its own rollout cap.** `cmd_train` built ONE `SamplingParams` from `--max-new-tokens` and handed it to both the rollouts and `gsm8k_accuracy` (cli.py:234, :297), so a run trained at 256 was evaluated at 256: **38.4% with mean completion 238.7 against ~82.5% uncapped**, a mechanism [already written up](docs/experience/errors/2026-09-04-the-eval-cap-measured-itself.md) a day earlier with the headline withdrawn and **the line unchanged**. `eval.py`'s own docstring asserted the opposite — "the only place the cap is absent" — which is how it survived a day of reading. Fixed with a separate `eval_params` from a new `--eval-max-new-tokens` (default 2048, the protocol the published before/after numbers were scored under), set explicitly in both 27B recipes so it cannot silently follow the rollout cap. Gate captures the `max_new_tokens` `gsm8k_accuracy` is **handed** — asserting the flag parsed would pass without the wiring, and asserting on completion length would need a model long-winded enough to reach the cap, which the tiny model is not; negative control restoring the shared `params` fails. Also lands **`--load-adapter`**, declined earlier as speculative and no longer so: the running P1's after-number is only recoverable by re-scoring its saved adapter uncapped, and nothing in the tree loaded one back. It `copy_`s into the tensors `add_lora` attached rather than rebinding — a rebind loads an adapter the forward never reads and re-scores the base under a trained run's name — and refuses unknown or missing keys, which an adapter saved before #98 has. Its gate decodes the same prompt with and without the adapter and requires the tokens to differ; the rebind control fails. **The gate's own first version was vacuous**: trained with exact-match on a tiny random model, every rollout scores 0, every group ties, `lora_b` never leaves its zero init, and the adapter is an exact no-op — it now trains on the dense reward and asserts `lora_b` specifically is nonzero, since `lora_a`'s random init makes an any-tensor check pass on a no-op. [errors/2026-09-05-the-eval-cap-was-documented-then-shipped.md](docs/experience/errors/2026-09-05-the-eval-cap-was-documented-then-shipped.md)

- **fix** — **half the LoRA adapters received no gradient, and rode in the optimizer and the checkpoint anyway.** `add_lora` identified a linear weight by shape — any 2-D parameter owning no `.wq`/`.w8` (`model.py:415-421`) — and two kinds of tensor pass that test without being a linear weight: a quantized weight's **scale sidecar** (an fp4 `.scale` is `[N, K/32]`, and `q_proj.scale.wq` does not exist, so it read as a dense base) and **`conv1d`** `[qkv, 4]`, which goes to the GDN kernel. Neither is ever looked up: `_linear` resolves `lora_a` from the **base** key (`model.py:161`). Measured on a tiny model, rank 4, one forward and one `tape.backward`: **fp4 64 adapters / 32 with a gradient; bf16 34 / 32**. The dead ones were returned as `trainable`, carried two AdamW moments each, and were written into `adapter.safetensors`. The two causes are independent — the sidecars exist only under fp4, **`conv1d` is dead in both precisions** — so the gate runs both and the negative controls fail separately: reverting the sidecar guard leaves 30 dead, reverting only the `.conv1d` exclusion leaves 2. Fixed by testing the **parent** for `.wq`/`.w8` and excluding `.conv1d` by name; fp4 goes 64 → 32 adapters with all 32 receiving a gradient, and the count of *live* adapters is unchanged at 32 in both precisions. Gate `test_every_adapter_receives_a_gradient` counts gradients rather than matching names, so a dead target nobody anticipated fails it too. **The 27B share is an estimate and stays unreconciled:** applying the pre-fix rule to `param_specs` at rank 16 predicts 200.8 M adapter params (68.1 M of them dead) against the **170.8 M / 341 MB** [the thinking cap](docs/experience/wins/2026-09-04-the-thinking-cap.md) records for the real run — a 30.0 M gap, and since the formula reproduces `add_lora` exactly on a tiny build (15028 == 15028) the gap is in the 27B parameter set, not the arithmetic. No checkpoint is reachable from this machine, so every published adapter-params number stays suspect until a real run's keys are counted and bucketed. [errors/2026-09-05-half-the-lora-adapters-trained-nothing.md](docs/experience/errors/2026-09-05-half-the-lora-adapters-trained-nothing.md)
- **default flip** — **the prefix store evicted the one entry every real workload re-reads: the policy was FIFO, not LRU.** `_fifo: deque` with `popleft`, and `lookup` recorded no recency at all. Both workloads this server has grow a prefix and re-read it — a chat client resends the whole conversation each turn, an RL rollout group shares one prompt across 8 samples — so **the hottest entry is the oldest**, which is exactly what FIFO takes. Fixed by merging `_fifo` into `_by_id` as an `OrderedDict`, so recency **is** the iteration order: `move_to_end` on a hit, `popitem(last=False)` to evict, and one field deleted rather than a structure added. Measured on the re-read shape (capacity 4, one conversation re-read for 6 turns, unrelated traffic each turn): **FIFO 0/6 hits, LRU 6/6**; negative control deleting the `move_to_end` line **FAILED** with `__pycache__` cleared. The gate asserts the hit **count**, not the policy's implementation, so it also catches `_evict_one` drifting off the LRU end. **The more useful finding is a pre-existing inert test**, and it was inert before this change too: `test_prefix_hit_survives_evicting_its_own_entry` guards the hazard that `submit`'s own `evict_until_free` can evict the entry it just matched, but probed directly, the victim is the **decoy** — the matched entry's blocks are already retained by that request, so `free_block` drops refcount 2→1 and frees nothing, while the decoy's 2 blocks satisfy `needed=2` and the loop stops (entries 2→1, free 1→3, and a re-match after eviction still returns the snapshot). **Two mutations moving the snapshot read after eviction both survived.** Its assertion is `evictions >= 1` — that eviction *happened*, not that it reached this entry, and only the second is the hazard. Left exactly as it was with a `# ponytail:` recording what it cannot catch, because making it bite needs the matched entry to be the only source of free blocks and that raises "insufficient KV blocks" first. Untouched by FIFO→LRU: nothing in it calls `lookup`, and with no hits recorded LRU order **is** insertion order. `state_bytes` accounting is byte-identical — LRU reorders which entry leaves, not what one costs. **Hit rate under real traffic is pending-remote**; the two shapes to measure are named in the entry, at `--max-ctx 32768` so today's 8x flag is not credited to the cache. [wins/2026-09-05-the-prefix-store-evicted-the-hottest-entry.md](docs/experience/wins/2026-09-05-the-prefix-store-evicted-the-hottest-entry.md) · [docs/design-kv-tier.md](docs/design-kv-tier.md)

- **test** — **every gate over `gdn_backward` passes at every value of `_GDN_CHUNK`, including the one the codebase deliberately rejected.** `51e965e` set the chunk to 16 rather than upstream's 64 on a measured precision tradeoff, priced with a three-tier probe (16/32/64 vs autograd, 3 shapes x 3 seeds) that **never entered the suite**. Measured now, worst relative error against the serial scan over seeds 0/1/2: **16 -> 3.26e-06, 32 -> 5.46e-06, 64 -> 1.13e-05, 128 -> 1.25e-05**. Against the enforced tolerances, chunk 64 sits **18x inside** the tightest gate (`test_gdn_bwd_spans_chunks`, 5e-5) and **3704x inside** `_autograd_gradcheck`'s 1e-2 — confirmed by running them: at `_GDN_CHUNK = 64` the GDN suite is **8 passed, 2 skipped**. A 4x coarsening, a real regression, is invisible. New gate `test_gdn_backward_precision_tracks_the_chunk_size` asserts the **ordering** (16 beats 64) rather than an absolute bound, since the magnitude is the machine's f32 reduction order while "coarser rounds worse" is what the constant was chosen on; three seeds because at one seed 64 measured below 32 and read like a reversal — noise, and the noise is what started this. Negative control both ways: 64 **FAILED**, 32 **FAILED**, 16 passes, pre-existing gates green at 64. **The lever this began as is closed:** chunk 64 is 2.171x the wall clock (t=1024, CPU) but the tradeoff still stands, and stands harder — the tape backward was 62% of the step at `51e965e` and is **33.74%** now, so the prize shrank while the precision cost did not. Two explanations were wrong on the way: mine, that the comment was stale (its "5-10x" is against the **serial scan**, not against chunk 16 — the ratio between them is 2.7x, exactly what I had measured, so my numbers agreed with the comment I was doubting), and a peer's, that fewer chunks accumulate less error (the data is monotone the other way). [errors/2026-09-05-the-gate-cannot-see-the-number-it-guards.md](docs/experience/errors/2026-09-05-the-gate-cannot-see-the-number-it-guards.md)
