- **default flip** — **`serve_v100.sh --max-ctx` goes 4096 → 32768, an 8x raise that cost nothing but reading the `min()`.** ckl reported the served context as too short with output truncating. **The truncation half was wrong and it changes the fix:** a 4000-`max_tokens` request returns `finish_reason: stop` at 1141 completion tokens — the model chose to stop — while an 11017-token *prompt* gets `400 request (11017 tokens) exceeds max_total_tokens (4096)`. So the limit was `--max-ctx 4096` at `scripts/serve_v100.sh:51`, in git, not the launch-only parameter I first told the peer it was (I had read `/proc/<pid>/cmdline` and not the value's origin). Why 4096 survived: `_fit_blocks` returns `min(fit, cap)`, `cap` is `ctx × max_batch / 16` = **256**, and `/health` reported `blocks_total: 256` — **the two operands are numerically identical, so the reported number cannot say which one binds**, and a cap reads exactly like a measured memory ceiling. It was the cap. `fit` at `--slots 8` with a draft was already measured at **2649 blocks = 42384 tokens** (#65) in the *costlier* `--depth 3` configuration; 32768 tokens is 2048 blocks, so the cap still binds with the fit above it. Also corrected in `serve-v100.md`: a block is **2.125 MiB**, not the 2.00 MiB the doc's table carried — 2.000 of trunk K+V plus the draft's mirrored 1/16 at `--depth 1`, which `_fit_blocks` charges for. **A derivation I published and withdrew is recorded in the entry rather than dropped:** I first put the ceiling at 2453 blocks from `25492 / 32768 MiB` read off the live server, but `nvidia-smi` later read **22136 MiB**, and the read happens after the pool is allocated while `_fit_blocks` runs after `torch.cuda.empty_cache()` (`engine.py:1211`) — a call that same file records as worth 1024 vs 62832 tokens. Post-hoc `nvidia-smi` cannot see the memory state the fit saw; the measurement supersedes the derivation. **Measured on the card** (`boot 0 at 09:39:13 sha 6a4e737`): `blocks_total` 256 → **2048**, the 11019-token prompt that returned 400 now returns **200**, idle VRAM 22136 → 25980 MiB and **peak 27036 of 32768** during the long prefill. The KV arithmetic checks out by a second route: the idle delta is **3844 MiB against a predicted 3808** (1.009x), which also confirms the 2.125 MiB block — omitting the draft's plane would have predicted 3616 and missed by 1.062x. **First-request cost is 19.4 s, not 182 s:** first request 182.5 s, identical second 163.1 s, so only the difference is the one-time fp4 JIT and the 163 s is what an 11019-token prompt costs every time (**14.68 ms/prompt token** vs 8.92 at 4096). Calling 182.5 s the JIT cost would have overstated it 9.4x. **Deployment carried its own defect, recorded separately:** the first restart logged `=== boot 3 … sha 6a4e737 dirty 0 ===` while the child's `/proc/<pid>/cmdline` still read `--max-ctx 4096` — bash parses the supervisor's `for` loop once, so an 8-hour-old supervisor relaunches the old flag string while `git rev-parse` stamps the new sha each boot, and `blocks_total: 256` looked like confirmation because it is exactly what the old config produces. **Two claims withdrawn:** the 2453-block ceiling derived from a post-hoc `nvidia-smi`, and "the server is mid-session so this is unmeasured" — `/health` showed `finished: 0` against 31.5 minutes of uptime, so nothing was in session and the excuse simply needed no defending. **`kv_tier`'s before-arm must be 32768**: 8x of the headroom is a flag, and re-prefilling this prompt costs 163 s against the 19 s of JIT an offload tier would avoid. [wins/2026-09-05-max-ctx-4096-was-a-flag-not-a-ceiling.md](docs/experience/wins/2026-09-05-max-ctx-4096-was-a-flag-not-a-ceiling.md) · [errors/2026-09-05-the-boot-line-sha-outran-the-flags.md](docs/experience/errors/2026-09-05-the-boot-line-sha-outran-the-flags.md)

- **fix** — **`opd_loop` exempted the one teacher that goes stale fastest.** The on-policy guard ran only `if trainable is not None`, on the reasoning that "a frozen teacher with no adapters cannot go stale". That teacher is not frozen: with no `trainable`, `train_step` updates `model.params` (`train.py:81`) and the engine holds that same object (`engine.py:320`), so every step the teacher samples from weights the student just moved. Measured on tiny with the guard's other conditions satisfied, so the exemption was isolated: **27 of the teacher's parameters changed within two steps**, `max|delta|` 2.03e-01 on `embed_tokens`, `final_norm` and each `down_proj` — the exact off-policy sampling the guard exists to refuse, and silent. Not a live defect: no caller passes `trainable=None` (`cli.py:287` calls `add_lora` unconditionally, and `--lora-rank 0` raises `ZeroDivisionError` at `model.py:424` rather than reaching the exemption; all three test callers pass it). Fixed by deleting the condition rather than the comment, because a comment recording a known-wrong exemption is a record, and a record produces no action. Gated by `test_opd_refuses_a_cached_engine_with_no_adapters_too`, which asserts the no-adapter call **is refused** rather than asserting no caller passes None — the latter is a fact a future caller reverses silently; negative control restoring the condition **CAUGHT**. Found by a peer challenging the comment's premise, not by a failure. [roadmap P1](docs/roadmap.md)

- **fix** — **two checks were pointing at moving targets: a negative control aimed at `HEAD~1`, and an injection keyed on a call count that raced the engine loop.** (1) `probe_ui_gate_negative_control.py` proved the JS reference gate catches the real `addThinking` bug by reading `HEAD~1`'s source — a relative ref, so it named the broken commit on the day it was written and something else after, giving the probe a useful life of **one commit**. Run on main it fails with "the gate does NOT catch the bug it was written for", a message about the gate produced by the probe looking elsewhere; nobody noticed because it lives in `scripts/` and CI never runs it. #90's asset split fixed *where* it looks (both `ui_assets.py` and `server.py`) and left `REV = "HEAD~1"` — the path was the symptom, the ref is the defect. Pinned to `69398d4`, verified before pinning: of `{69398d4, e954f8c, HEAD~1, origin/main}` it is the **only** revision whose `_CHAT_UI` leaves `addThinking` unresolved, and it is the parent of the fix. `scripts/baseline.py:74` also uses `HEAD~1` and is **not** the same defect — it checks that any two adjacent commits order correctly, where a relative ref is the right semantics; checked rather than assumed from the shape. (2) `test_v1_messages_never_answers_200_for_an_engine_failure`'s `_Dies` let one `take` through and raised on the next, so when the request finished before the route's first poll the raising call never happened: non-stream **200**, stream **400** (its allowance already spent), tripping the agreement assertion. Measured rather than dismissed, because "passes alone, fails in the suite" is the shape of a flake and not proof of innocence: **1 of 12 runs**, exactly that signature. Keyed on the result instead (`take` returns None → still running, pass through; the first with a result raises): **0 of 12**. **The identical construction beside it does not flake, and the assertion is why:** `_DiesAfterOnePeek` uses the same `_left = 1` and measured 0 of 12, because it asserts a *contract* (`[DONE]` or an explanation) that an unfired injection still satisfies — a count-based injection is only a race when the assertion can distinguish "failure injected" from "request succeeded". Left alone. [errors/2026-09-05-a-probe-aimed-at-a-moving-target.md](docs/experience/errors/2026-09-05-a-probe-aimed-at-a-moving-target.md)

- **test** — **`README:89` claimed every number above it sits in a dated entry; one did not, and it was a number with no measurement record at all.** `43.2 tok/s wall over the network` (README:22, added by `cb1b59e`/#74) appears in **no** dated entry: its only source was that commit's body, and the commit touched README.md alone — so it satisfied the every-runtime-change-gets-an-entry rule by not being a runtime change. Searching all history, the one prior `43.2` as a tok/s figure is a DFlash depth-2 verify column that `fa8634a` **deleted**; unrelated, same digits. Measured across the claim's whole range: **23 distinct decimals, 285 dated entries, exactly 1 absent, 0 false positives** — #87 replaced it with 50.0 decode-only / 46.3 wall (`wins/2026-09-04-the-pages-rate-is-50-…`, 4 runs plus median, and 46.3 reconciled against a recorded 46.1 at 1.004x) and 19 GB of weights off disk (`errors/2026-09-02-serve-never-sized-its-kv-pool.md:90`). **Deliberately not written: the weights' resident VRAM**, which nobody has measured on its own — it lies between the 19 GB on disk and the 23.2 GiB of whole-process residency this session read off `nvidia-smi` all night, and picking either would be inventing. That is the shape 43.2 had: its direction was right (a network path is slower than 46.3), it had simply never been measured. Two-arm negative control: 43.2 restored **FAILED**, a sourced number repeated stayed green. Integers are excluded — `8`, `27`, `4096` are configuration, and scanning them returned mostly version numbers and tensor shapes. [errors/2026-09-05-a-readme-number-with-no-record.md](docs/experience/errors/2026-09-05-a-readme-number-with-no-record.md)

- **method** — **the gate ladder has three rungs, not two: enumerable range with a decidable test, enumerable range with an approximate test, and no enumerable range at all.** Yesterday's note recorded the first and third. The README-numbers gate is the middle rung and the first with an inherent direction: **it falsifies but cannot verify.** Its positive match is a string comparison while a real confirmation needs semantic association — `README:57`'s "1.6 points apart" (MMLU significance) matches "~1.6% of f32 peak" in `errors/2026-08-29-chunkwise-wy-wrongly-ruled-out.md:74`, two unrelated quantities sharing a digit string. So "the number appears somewhere" proves nothing about provenance, while "the number appears in no dated entry" is a fact about the README. The three earlier gates (#83 exception types, #85 CLI flags, #86 wrapper interface) are two-directional because membership in their sets is decidable. Recorded here beside yesterday's boundary because it decides how much a green gate is worth: on the middle rung, green means "no proven-absent number", never "every number is sourced", and a docstring that implies otherwise is the defect. [errors/2026-09-05-a-readme-number-with-no-record.md](docs/experience/errors/2026-09-05-a-readme-number-with-no-record.md)

- **fix** — **one of the on-policy guard's two arms was never tested, and it is the one CUDA takes.** `_require_on_policy` refuses a captured decode graph **or** a live prefix store; the single test covering it built an engine with the prefix cache on and `decode_graph` at its default. On the CPU target `_graph_on` returns False, so that engine trips only the prefix half. Measured by deleting each operand and re-running that test: **`_decode_graph_on` half deleted → 1 passed** (untested), prefix half deleted → 1 failed (caught). **On CUDA the default is True**, so a `grpo_loop` that forgot `decode_graph=False` lands on exactly the uncovered clause — and the failure is not a crash but rollouts drawn from a captured graph of an earlier policy, which is what the guard exists to prevent. Test now builds both engines; `decode_graph=True` is honoured on cpu (asserted), so the graph arm is testable here rather than CUDA-only. Both arms CAUGHT after. **The probe before the mutation was load-bearing:** it established `decode_graph=True` survives on cpu — had `_graph_on` forced False there, the widened test would have built a second engine identical to the first and the mutation would still have passed, reading as "the guard is fine" rather than "the test is inert". [errors/2026-09-05-the-guard-arm-cuda-uses-was-untested.md](docs/experience/errors/2026-09-05-the-guard-arm-cuda-uses-was-untested.md)

- **method** — **where the "write down the range" gate stops working, and it is not a matter of effort.** Three gates landed today (#83 handler set over exception types, #85 doc invocations over the CLI's flag set, #86 one wrapper's answers against another's) and all three work the same way: name a range some set has to exhaust, then assert it does. That is why a test can carry them — the range is enumerable. **The fourth range in the same family, "which attributes must this object have", is not enumerable, because a read can be a string literal** (`getattr(engine, "limits", 0)` is invisible to every scan keyed on attribute syntax, as #86 measured). So there is no static form of that question and no gate to write; the class stays a reading problem, and the reviewable signal is the shape `getattr(x, "y", <non-None default>)` itself. Recorded here rather than only in #86's entry because it decides where the next instance of "the forwarding layer is missing something" should go — a gate or a review — and that is a method boundary, not a detail of one bug.

- **fix** — **`serve --devices` answered 400 to every Claude Code turn, because the clamp in front of the engine read a `getattr` default.** `/v1/messages` has to accept `max_tokens: 32000` — Claude Code sends it every turn — so `mount_messages` clamps against the engine's budget via `getattr(getattr(engine, "limits", None), "max_total_tokens", 0)`. `DataParallelEngine` forwards 11 methods explicitly, has no `__getattr__`, and had no `limits`: the default **0** fired, `if engine_limit` read 0 as *no limit known*, the clamp became a no-op and 32000 went to `submit` unchanged. Measured, one engine and one request, only the wrapper differing: **plain `Engine` → 200; `DataParallelEngine([e])` → 400 `request (32060 tokens) exceeds max_total_tokens (512)`.** The 400 is `engine.py:459` doing its job; the defect is the clamp going silent in front of it. **Third instance of one shape** — code doing an existence check against a duck type: `peek` raising past the SSE 200 header (#80), the `_thread` liveness check blocked before it landed (#83), and this one, the quiet variant, where nothing raises because a number that reads as valid comes back instead. **A numeric `getattr` default cannot distinguish "missing" from "0", and for a limit 0 is the most permissive reading, not the conservative one** — `None` would have raised on the next line. Fixed with a `limits` property on the wrapper (`min` over replicas by `max_total_tokens`, since `submit` routes to the shortest queue) rather than a third default at the call site: `StepLimits` is half of the `submit`/`poll` + `StepLimits` seam contract, so it belongs on every implementation of the seam. Gated by `test_the_clamp_survives_the_data_parallel_wrapper`, which asserts the two wrappers **agree** and then that they agree on 200 — a fix that broke the clamp for everyone would pass a one-arm test by making both 400; negative control with the property removed and `__pycache__` cleared **CAUGHT** (`plain 200, wrapped 400`). Surface closed: the server layer reads six engine attributes (`submit take peek logprobs stats limits`), `_thread` appears only in #83's comment with no live read, and all six are now present on `DataParallelEngine` — **but no grep produced that list, which is the transferable part.** #80's probe (set difference between `engine.<name>(` call sites and `dir()`) still misses this gap today, and so does a widened `engine\.[a-z_]+`, because `limits` is never written as `engine.limits`: the attribute name lives inside a string literal in `getattr(getattr(engine, "limits", None), ...)`. **A `getattr` with a default is both the quietest failure shape and the one a syntactic probe cannot see**, so the two properties compound; this was found by reading twenty lines and finding a doubled `getattr` suspicious. It also narrows #80: "`peek` was the only missing method" holds as "the only missing member called as `engine.<name>(`". **Not claimed:** that wrapper-shaped gaps elsewhere are enumerated, by this or any scan — `train.py:213` reads `engine._decode_graph_on` and `engine._prefix` and would raise on a wrapped engine, unreachable today and not fixed here. [errors/2026-09-05-the-clamp-read-a-getattr-default.md](docs/experience/errors/2026-09-05-the-clamp-read-a-getattr-default.md)

- **fix** — **the SSE handler covered 2 exception types out of 6, so a defect in a stream returned success with no content.** `_stream` caught only `(TimeoutError, RuntimeError)`, and the 200 header goes out before the generator body runs, so anything else escaped after the response was already committed as a success. Six types injected at the engine boundary: **non-stream classifies all six (504/500); stream returns 200 for all six**, and `ValueError`/`AttributeError`/`KeyError`/`IndexError` deliver **0 frames, no error frame, no `[DONE]`** — indistinguishable from a model that said nothing. `DataParallelEngine`'s missing `peek` was one instance of this, and removing only the catch-all from a tree that already carries that fix reproduces the table exactly, so the two are independent: the method was the instance, the handler set is the container. Fixed with a catch-all that frames **and logs** — the log was checked, not asserted (1 ERROR record, `exc_info`, `AttributeError` named), because a tidy error frame is easier to ignore than silence. `/v1/messages` does not share the gap, measured rather than read: identical stream and non-stream columns, no row a 200, since its `sse()` yields nothing until `_run` has built the whole body. Gated on the **contract** — `[DONE]` or an explanation, never a bare 200 — so a root-cause fix satisfies it too; negative controls in both files, including that hoisting `sse()` ahead of `_run` (the per-token rewrite its ponytail marker warns about) fails the `/v1/messages` gate. [errors/2026-09-05-the-sse-handler-covered-two-of-six.md](docs/experience/errors/2026-09-05-the-sse-handler-covered-two-of-six.md)

- **fix** — **`/health` reported `status: "ok"` for an engine whose `stats()` raises, because "ok" was a literal.** A health endpoint that cannot report ill health. Found by reading, not by an outage — and it had no live consumer: all three script readers take `stats`, `bench_workloads.py` fails its own `finished != 2` guard on the `{}` fallback and `probe_served_rate.py` raises `TypeError` subscripting `None`, so neither can produce a fabricated number from it. Now returns `degraded` plus the exception type. **Loop liveness is deliberately still unchecked:** it needs `engine._thread`, which `DataParallelEngine` lacks, so the check would pass vacuously under `--devices` — the shape of the `peek` gap — and it wants a liveness method on the engine instead. Gated by `test_health_says_degraded_when_the_engine_raises`, which asserts the two states differ *and* that a healthy engine still says `ok`, so a fix marking everything degraded fails. [errors/2026-09-05-the-sse-handler-covered-two-of-six.md](docs/experience/errors/2026-09-05-the-sse-handler-covered-two-of-six.md) (same entry — same shape, one endpoint over)

- **correction** — **the SSD tier `#75` removed from the landing page was real, and never reached `main`.** `#75`'s message said "no such tier exists", citing a grep of `src/` and `packages/` — a search of one tree, supporting a claim about all of history. `KvTier` was written and iterated on for four days on the sm70 branch (`e9d5852` 08-29 tiered cache, `9a685d6` 08-30 six review blockers, `29b58a3` 08-31 size-based LRU, `3e53893` 08-31 HBM-derived capacity). My first correction, and the peer reading I had accepted, were **also wrong**: we said it reached `main` and was deleted by `a317f61` — `a317f61` still contains it. Checked exhaustively instead: **zero of `main`'s 567 first-parent commits contain `KvTier`**, and `e9d5852`/`29b58a3`/`a317f61` are all `--is-ancestor = NO`. The real defect is a partial port — `e4aaf8c` (`#60`) brought the sm70 branch's server work over with the landing page's two `SSD` strings and **zero** `KvTier`, so the advertisement shipped and the feature did not. `#75` removed the right end; only its stated reason was wrong, and the wrong reason reads as "never built" when a four-commit implementation with a review pass exists one branch away. **The landing page was not the only surface:** `main` carries **eight mentions of `KvTier` and zero lines of it** — and `docs/serve-v100.md:163` listed `--kv-tier <dir>` as the third mitigation for a KV pool that does not fit 32K, saying "the engine path is tested", next to two real levers, while neither the flag nor the class exists in `src/`. Rewritten to say the code was branch-only. The CHANGELOG and entry mentions stay — a record of what was done on a branch is true; an instruction to use something is a claim about `main`. [errors/2026-09-05-the-ssd-tier-was-real-and-never-reached-main.md](docs/experience/errors/2026-09-05-the-ssd-tier-was-real-and-never-reached-main.md)

- **fix** — **`alloc_slot` popped the slot before it zeroed it, so a raise inside `zero_` lost that slot for the life of the process.** `LinearStatePool.alloc_slot` checked `_free`, popped, then zeroed two tensors — and both zeroing steps can raise, by which point the slot has left `_free` and has not reached the caller, so **nothing can ever `free_slot` it**. `Engine.submit`'s unwind cannot compensate: it guards on `slot is not None`, and on this path `slot` is still `None` precisely because `alloc_slot` never returned. Measured with a probe driving the pool directly (`states[slot]` replaced by an object whose `zero_()` raises): **free before=3, after=2 — the pool now offers 2 of 3 slots**, unreachable forever. Slot capacity is the concurrency ceiling (`Engine.__init__` warns when `usable_slots < max_batch`, since a slot is held from submit to finish) and `serve` defaults to `--slots 8` (`cli.py:615`), so eight such failures take concurrency to zero — and the error that follows is `LinearStatePool exhausted` on a pool that should have room, which reads as a config problem rather than a leak. **Reachable on this card:** `errors/2026-09-04-the-recompiles-reproduce-and-are-not-a-shape-set.md:168-172` logs `torch.OutOfMemoryError` with **186.38 MiB free of 31.74 GiB** on this V100; at that margin any allocation raises, `zero_()` included. Fix returns the slot on any post-pop failure. Gated by `test_a_failed_alloc_slot_does_not_consume_the_slot`, which substitutes a raising `zero_`, asserts `len(_free) == 3`, then allocates all three — the last part because a slot can be back in `_free` while the pool is otherwise inconsistent; negative control with the pre-fix body **CAUGHT**. **Found by reviewing a peer's premise, not by a failure:** they asked me to challenge "`alloc_slot` is atomic because it checks `_free` before popping" — checking a precondition before mutating is not being atomic, and the two statements after the pop are the whole defect. `PagedKvPool.alloc_block` was checked for the same shape and does not have it. **Not claimed:** that this leak has occurred in a live run, or that the recorded OOM was inside `alloc_slot` (it was in `silu_mul`). [errors/2026-09-05-alloc-slot-popped-before-it-zeroed.md](docs/experience/errors/2026-09-05-alloc-slot-popped-before-it-zeroed.md)

- **fix** — **`Engine.submit`'s rollback released blocks the `PrefixStore` owns, and `serve --devices` could not stream at all.** (1) `submit` seeded its unwind list with the prefix-cache hit blocks *before* retaining them, and allocated the state slot inside the same guarded region — so an `alloc_slot` failure (the pool being full at `max_batch` concurrency, an ordinary condition) called `free_block` once per hit block. Those blocks are the store's; `free_block` decrements without recording an owner, so a spurious decrement is indistinguishable from a release, and the store keeps serving pages the pool believes are free. Fixed by reordering, not by adding state: slot first, `blocks` empty, retain-then-append. (2) `DataParallelEngine` forwards 10 methods with no `__getattr__` and had no `peek`, while `server.py:265` calls it unconditionally as `_stream`'s first line — and that handler catches only `(TimeoutError, RuntimeError)`, so the `AttributeError` escaped *after* the SSE 200 header was sent. Measured: **HTTP 200, `r.text == ''`, 0 frames, no `[DONE]`** — a client reads that as an empty reply, not an error, and the chat page always sends `stream=true`. Gates `test_a_rejected_submit_does_not_release_the_prefix_stores_blocks` (needs a *longer* prompt sharing the prefix — `_match_prefix` treats `matched >= len(tokens)` as a miss, which is why two earlier attempts passed against unfixed code — and asserts `prefix_hits >= 1` so it cannot go inert) and `test_a_data_parallel_engine_can_stream` (asserts frames, not status: the status passes against the bug); negative control on the second **CAUGHT**. `peek` was the only missing method, by set difference between every `engine.<name>(` call site and `dir(DataParallelEngine)`, not by grep. [errors/2026-09-05-a-rejected-submit-released-the-prefix-stores-blocks.md](docs/experience/errors/2026-09-05-a-rejected-submit-released-the-prefix-stores-blocks.md)

- **measurement** — **the ~600 s that justified the 1800 s cap was a B=8 whole-tick cost quoted as a single request, and the 4.3x fix for it had already landed four commits earlier.** `5cdbf7e` raised the completion cap 600 s → 1800 s on the strength of one commit-body line — "4K prefill on sm70 takes ~600s" — with **no `docs/experience/` entry anywhere**; `grep` for it across `docs/` and `CHANGELOG.md` returns nothing. Measured on the live V100 (`550740a`, `--max-batch 1`, `--max-ctx 4096`, 27B NVFP4 + depth-1 draft), one request with the client budget set to **900 s** deliberately so that whichever side gave up first was identifiable: **3478 input tokens COMPLETED in 39.1 s**, 11.25 ms/input token. Adding output up to the context limit — `--max-ctx 4096` minus a 3478-token prompt leaves **618** tokens, and decode at 4K ctx is 41.9 tok/s (50.3 measured at short context, scaled by the **spec** column's 48.4 → 40.3 falloff, since the service runs `--depth 1`) — gives **53.9 s worst case**, **11.1x** under the cap, so on this service the 600 s limit is unreachable and ~600 s is **15x** the real cost. (**I got that worst case wrong three times, every time too high:** 80 s from the short-context rate plus an assumed 2048 output tokens the context cannot hold; then 52.5 s from scaling by the *dense* falloff for a service running spec; then 53.9 s. Each error strengthened the conclusion, so nothing looked wrong — and the third was made *while writing the entry about this failure mode*, minutes after recording the rule. Writing a rule down does not install it; re-deriving the number does.) **It reconciles against a bench taken a different way:** `serve-v100.md:106-112`'s in-process `step()` loop gives 8.92 ms/token at 4096 ctx, which over 3478 tokens is 31.0 s, plus 0.16 s of decode = 31.2 s accounted, leaving 7.9 s (20%) for HTTP, tokenize, template render and scheduling — two methods with nothing in common agreeing within **1.26x**, with the sign right (mine carries the overhead the bench does not). That is the check ~600 s never had. Two causes, and the ordering is the finding: the only prefill measurement from that day is `wins/2026-08-31-sm70-gdn-chunk-fused.md:60-65`, **B=8, warm prefill ~65 s → 15.1 s**, breakdown labelled "tick 1, 8x64 tokens" — a *batch tick* figure, extrapolated to 4096 tokens per row, lands in the hundreds of seconds; and `1ecf8ee`, the **4.3x prefill win**, landed FOUR commits before the cap was raised, so the cap accommodated a cost the tree had already made 4.3x cheaper. **Not claimed:** that 4.3x accounts for a specific share of the 15x — 15.35/4.3 leaves 3.57x unexplained, and multiplying figures from different harnesses into a factorization is the move this entry warns about; the 4.3x is here for *when* it landed, not as a term in an equation. **My own instrument nearly reported this wrong in the safe direction:** a 4-point fit extrapolated to 31.5 s (close to the true 39.1) but had to be discarded because **465 tokens came back faster than 124** — a longer prompt finishing sooner means the slope describes noise, so the right answer would have been luck. **One comparison NOT made:** the B=8 entry works out to 29.49 ms/token against my 11.24, i.e. 2.62x more per token batched — not reported as a batching finding, because **79 runtime commits** sit between the two measurements, the row lengths differ 54-fold (64 vs 3478 tokens), and the harnesses differ; that ratio mixes five days of code change with a row-length effect and a batch effect. **Stands unchanged:** #78's defect — one policy in two constants, only one updated. **Withdrawn:** "a 4K prefill takes ~600 s on its own" as a fact about a single request. **Not claimed:** that ~600 s was wrong for the B=8 configuration it was taken in; that was never measured and this does not measure it. Resolves the `pending-remote` stub in the entry below. [errors/2026-09-05-the-600s-that-justified-1800s-was-a-batch-tick.md](docs/experience/errors/2026-09-05-the-600s-that-justified-1800s-was-a-batch-tick.md)

- **fix** — **the 600s→1800s completion timeout was raised on one of two API paths, and `/v1/messages` is the one Claude Code speaks.** `5cdbf7e` raised the cap citing "a 4K prefill on sm70 takes ~600 s on its own" — **that figure is withdrawn by the entry above (measured 39.1 s); the defect below is independent of it** — and it changed only `server.py`. `messages.py` has its own wait loop over the same engine and kept waiting 600 s, so a 4K+ request through `/v1/messages` raised `TimeoutError` where the identical request through `/v1/chat/completions` completed. Hoisted to `_COMPLETION_TIMEOUT_S = 1800.0` because the 600 appeared **twice** in that function, as the deadline and in the error text, so changing one alone would have made the other lie. Gated by `test_both_api_paths_wait_the_same_wall_clock_for_one_completion`, which reads both caps out of the source and asserts equality — reading rather than running, since a test that waits 600 s to observe a timeout would never run in CI. Mutation control both directions, both CAUGHT: `messages.py` back to 600 (the bug exactly as found), and `server.py`'s stream cap drifting to 900 — the second arm matters because a gate that checked only `messages.py` against a hardcoded 1800 would pass while the other side drifted. **The `pending-remote` stub is now resolved:** a 3478-token `/v1/messages` request completes in 39.1 s on the live V100. [errors/2026-09-05-the-timeout-fix-landed-on-one-of-two-api-paths.md](docs/experience/errors/2026-09-05-the-timeout-fix-landed-on-one-of-two-api-paths.md)

- **process** — **`gh pr checks` carries no sha, so two sessions read one stale green and drew opposite wrong conclusions off identical output.** I pushed three commits past PR 67's tested head `550740a`; `gh pr checks` still showed both gates passing. Queried by sha the picture is unambiguous: `550740a` has 3 successful check-runs, **`0aa930d` has `total_count = 0`** — never triggered, because `ci.yml` fires only on `push: branches: [main]` and `pull_request`, and GitHub's PR index was lagging so the PR never updated (git ref API returned `0aa930d` while the PR API still reported `550740a` and its commit list held none of the three). **A state existed that the command cannot express:** commits on a pushed branch that no CI run has ever seen, reported green. The peer read it as "the head is green" and merged; I read it as "CI re-ran and passed" and told them so. **Neither of us corrected the other, because we were reading the same instrument** — every other mutual check that day worked, and each of those used different methods, so the redundancy went to zero while feeling unchanged. Nothing untested reached main, but only because the stuck index pinned the head to the one tested sha: the process was wrong and the result was right, and those are separate records. Fix: `gh api repos/.../commits/<sha>/check-runs`, or `gh run list --json headSha` with the sha string **compared** (the newest run is not necessarily the head's — here it belonged to `550740a`). A watch script keys on `$(gh pr view N --json headRefOid)`. [errors/2026-09-05-two-agents-one-broken-instrument.md](docs/experience/errors/2026-09-05-two-agents-one-broken-instrument.md)

- **verdict** — **Task #21 closed as already-answered, no run: all three of its numbers had been superseded and one of them is 2.1-2.3x off two agreeing in-graph measurements.** #21 asked to profile `attn o` at N=1024 (5% of peak), `gdn out`/`gdn z` (32%), and 144 GEMV launches/token, measuring in the captured graph first because the microbench has a ~60 µs eager launch floor. Before running anything I looked for where the figures came from. **Both levers were retired three days earlier** by `errors/2026-09-02-per-shape-gap-was-a-wrong-shape-table.md:62-69`: `n_partition` was aimed at N=1024, which **this engine never launches** (the smallest real shape is `gdn ab` at N=96, 0.1% of the stream), and `gdn z` was **already fused into `qkvz`** — the table listed the pre-fusion members. Those per-row percentages came from a shape table whose `hq*d` used head_dim 64 instead of 256 and whose count of 32 double-counted a projection that exists 16 times; it passed its own `abs(nib_tot/1e9 - 12.81) < 0.02` assert at 12.799 GB because `attn o` being 168 MB short cancels `gdn out` being 252 MB long, and a total-only check cannot see the distribution every conclusion was about. **The 144 is the part that crosses the 2x instrument line:** the graph profile measures **305** GEMV launches/token (`linear_fp4_gemv_sm70_m_kernel`) and `prof_decode_budget.py` measures **313.4** calls/forward at depth 1 — independent, different days, agreeing to 2.8% — against which 144 is 2.1-2.3x low. 144 is in the tree three times and **never as a live launch count**: `r4 x144` is the tick count on rung 4 in a depth-3 row, `ceil(2176/16)·1 + 8 = 144` is the KV block count `NB`, and its one use as a launch count sits under *What this withdraws* in `errors/2026-09-03-batching-is-non-monotone-padding-rows-cost-3x.md:130`. The live descendant is real and has a number: **305 f32→f16 casts of X, 1.64 ms/token, 5.9% of the token**, one per GEMV launch from `backend.py:554`, at **102x the byte cost** — a launch-count problem, filed separately. [errors/2026-09-05-task-21-numbers-do-not-survive-a-grep.md](docs/experience/errors/2026-09-05-task-21-numbers-do-not-survive-a-grep.md)

- **verdict** — **the loose-cap control moved GSM8K 6.8 points off 8 gradient steps, and refutes the mechanism the thinking cap was written around.** `8ca073e54686` retrained at a 2048-token cap, where the policy solves 96.6% of training prompts and **92 of 100 steps carry no gradient at all**. It still scored **482/500 = 96.4% uncapped against a 448/500 = 89.6% base** — z=4.25, p=2.1e-05, and **not distinguishable from the 256-cap arm's 94.8%** (+1.6, z=1.23, p=0.22). So the cap's difficulty pressure is real (tie rate 72% → 92% without it) but was **not necessary for the accuracy**: both arms land in the same place, and what the cap bought is sample efficiency — 28% of steps carrying gradient against 8% — plus the 22.8% token reduction the loose arm has no reason to produce. **`tied_group_fraction` measures efficiency, not efficacy:** a tied group contributes a zero and costs wall clock, but does not dilute the steps that are not tied. **I published the opposite conclusion first.** MMLU (`max_new_tokens=1`) printed 56 minutes before GSM8K (500 uncapped questions), and in that window I wrote that an arm this starved "should not be expected to move a downstream metric at all, and it did not" — a conclusion drawn from the out-of-domain check while the metric the reward optimizes had not landed. The early number agreed with my model, and I had correctly hedged its precision (2.2 points, n=1, p=0.26, not a regression) — the hedge was about whether that number is real, not about whether it was the number to read. [wins/2026-09-04-the-cap-was-the-gradient.md](docs/experience/wins/2026-09-04-the-cap-was-the-gradient.md) · [errors/2026-09-05-i-concluded-from-the-metric-that-had-reported.md](docs/experience/errors/2026-09-05-i-concluded-from-the-metric-that-had-reported.md)

- **fix** — **the supervisor treated a killed server as a deliberate stop, so restarting it to pick up new code left nothing listening.** `scripts/serve_v100.sh` read "stopped on purpose" off the child's exit code (`case $rc in 0 | 130 | 143`), but 143 is `128 + SIGTERM` and the child exits 143 whether the supervisor forwarded the signal or something else killed the server — a plain `kill -TERM <server pid>`, or the OOM killer. Two of the four cases are exactly why a supervisor exists and both were read as deliberate: measured on the pod, TERM to the server pid left `ss -ltnp` empty on 8000 and `/proc/1987997` gone, with no log line saying anything was wrong. Intent now lives where intent exists — the trap sets `stopping=1` before forwarding — and the loop reads that. **The gate was the defect's alibi:** `test_a_deliberate_stop_is_not_restarted` asserted the broken behaviour and passed throughout, so deleting it is part of the fix; `test_killing_only_the_server_restarts_it` replaces it by signalling the stub server's own pid and asserting a second boot **and** a live supervisor. Negative control on the pod with the old `case` line restored: 1 failed / 5 passed, failing with the exact symptom seen live; fixed script 6 passed. [errors/2026-09-05-the-supervisor-read-stopped-on-purpose-off-the-wrong-process.md](docs/experience/errors/2026-09-05-the-supervisor-read-stopped-on-purpose-off-the-wrong-process.md)

- **fix** — **the chat page's live tok/s read 1.8x low because it counted SSE frames, not tokens.** `_stream` coalesces whatever the engine decoded since the last 0.02 s poll into one frame — measured **109 frames for 200 tokens** on the 27B — so the page's gauge showed ~27 tok/s for the whole reply and only jumped to the real rate when the final usage chunk arrived. The server already had the count, so every content frame now carries cumulative usage under `include_usage` (vLLM's `continuous_usage_stats` shape); the usage-ONLY chunk is still the one with an empty `choices` list, which is what the page keys on. Live on the V100 after the change: content frames carry `completion_tokens` 1 → 3 → … and three runs at `max_tokens=200` give **decode 50.3 / wall 46.3 tok/s, ttft 324 ms**, unchanged from before the restart. **The change broke its own instrument:** `probe_page_rate.py`'s `if obj.get("usage"): continue` then skipped every content frame and asserted "no content frames arrived" against a server that had just sent 109 — the same defect being fixed in the page, in the tool measuring it. `test_the_chat_page_reads_the_stream_this_server_sends` now asserts one count per content frame plus the final chunk and that counts never decrease; verified by negative control (usage dropped from content frames → FAILED).

- **fix** — **the markdown renderer let a quote break out of an HTML attribute, and my own gate then invented a second one.** `mdEscape` escaped `& < >` and no quotes while two rules interpolate its result into an attribute, so `[x](https://a"onmouseover="alert(1))` rendered a **live event handler** into a page whose sink is `innerHTML` on every assistant reply. Found in review, reproduced by execution, fixed at the source rather than by tightening the URL character class — a class guards only the rule it sits in. **Then the correction that matters:** I reported the single-quote variant as live too and told the reviewer their fix was half a fix. It was not. Every attribute this template writes is double-quoted, so `'` inside the value is an ordinary character; measured on the exact string with no renderer involved, my regex `[\s"']([a-zA-Z-]+)\s*=` reports `href, onmouseover, target, rel` while `html.parser` reports `href, target, rel` — the regex treats `'` as an attribute separator and matched **inside** the quoted value. So the harness had reported CAUGHT for a mutation that reintroduces no bug. The gate now parses with `html.parser`, and the harness asserts an **expected verdict per case** (`"` CAUGHT, `<` CAUGHT, both-quotes CAUGHT, `'` passed — four for four) because a wrong kill is worse than a missing one: it marks inert code as load-bearing. The `'` escape stays as defence in depth, and the docstring says so instead of implying it is load-bearing. **Three checks failed the same way in one afternoon, in three directions** — the reviewer's substring assertion could not tell an attribute from text, my regex invented one, and my attack set was all quote payloads so dropping the `<` escape read MISSED until a raw `<img>` went in. Each covered only the failure its author had already imagined. Also: I had MORE evidence than the reviewer (execution against the live server vs reading the template) and was still wrong, because the extra evidence went through a broken instrument. [errors/2026-09-05-a-regex-standing-in-for-a-parser-invented-a-bug.md](docs/experience/errors/2026-09-05-a-regex-standing-in-for-a-parser-invented-a-bug.md)

## 2026-09-04

- **accept-or-reject verdict** — **the thinking cap is ACCEPTED: training under a 256-token budget buys +5.2 points and −22.8% tokens, and it transfers.** Adapter `52b8251e79bc` (grpo-gsm8k-27b, 100 steps, group 8, LoRA r16, lr 1e-4, `max_new_tokens=256`, thinking off) scored against its base under the **uncapped** protocol — 2048 cap, thinking on, greedy, the same 500 questions, one engine, `add_lora` starting B at zero so the first pass IS the base: **448/500 = 89.6% [86.6-92.0] → 474/500 = 94.8% [92.5-96.4]**, intervals disjoint, **z = 3.07, p = 0.0022** unpaired (the arms are paired on the same questions, so the real test is sharper). Tokens **157,601 → 121,642 (−22.8%)**, cost per correct answer **351.8 → 256.6 (−27.1%)**. Both directions moved, so this is not a speed/quality trade. **The 4.79-point threshold was pre-registered** when the baseline was first measured and before the trained arm ran, so the bar was set in advance. **It transfers out of domain** — the adapter saw GSM8K and nothing else, and free-form n=100 each gives MMLU 493→385 tokens (−22.0%), ARC-Easy 251→204 (−18.9%), PIQA 210→162 (−22.9%), with accuracy moving −4/+4/+4 against a ±12.7-point interval, i.e. **not distinguishable from no change in either direction**; MMLU had to be re-run free-form because `mmlu_score` runs at `max_new_tokens=1` and structurally cannot see length. **Mechanism, n=1 by eye and labelled as such:** on the same question the base spends 256 tokens and 2 of 4 steps on `$$V_{bed} = \text{Width} \times \text{Length} \times \text{Height}$$` before the cap cuts it (scored on the intermediate 320 against gold 1920), while the trained arm writes all four steps and reaches $1,920 in 109 tokens — every intermediate survives, the LaTeX ceremony does not. **Cost of getting it:** 72% of steps carried zero gradient (44 of 61, 42 because all eight rollouts were right), `groups_untied` failed at 0.76, and the run is marked FAIL for it — the result came from about a quarter of the intended signal, which is the argument for `--judge`. Reproduction exact: `52b8251e79bc` matches `a4332cbca4fa` on every metric, differing only in `peak_gib` by 0.01. [wins/2026-09-04-the-thinking-cap.md](docs/experience/wins/2026-09-04-the-thinking-cap.md)

- **measurement** — **the RL run's 39.0% GSM8K baseline measured the 256-token cap, not the model, and the +55 points it anchors is mostly a broken control.** Run `a4332cbca4fa` reported `gsm8k_before 195/500 = 39.0%` → `gsm8k_after 471/500 = 94.2%` with MMLU flat (75.2 → 75.0). Both arms are scored by one `SamplingParams` built at `cli.py:209` from `args.max_new_tokens` and handed to `gsm8k_accuracy` twice by the `evals()` closure, so both ran at the recipe's 256-token default with thinking off. Measured on the 27B at that setting, n=60: **39/60 = 65% of completions hit the cap**, greedy 22/60 = 36.7% correct (untruncated 44/60 = 73%, 21/60 = 35.0%). `answer_match` takes the last number in the completion, so on a truncated derivation it scores whichever intermediate the model was mid-way through — the 39.0% is how often an intermediate coincides with the gold answer, not the model's accuracy. Uncapped with thinking on (n=40, 8192 cap): mean 320.8, **median 283**, p90 527, max 926, **0/40 = 0% hit the cap**, **33/40 = 82.5% correct** (untruncated 32/40 = 80.0%). So the honest baseline is ~82.5% (sd ~6 at n=40) and the after-arm carries the same cap in the same direction, making `94.2 − 39.0` a conflation of getting more right with learning to finish inside 256 tokens. Two further reasons the run does not support its headline: `groups_untied` **failed at 0.76**, so three quarters of GRPO groups had identical rewards and `group_advantages` returned zeros — the number came from a quarter of the intended gradient; and `manifest["artifacts"]` was empty, so the adapter behind 94.2% does not exist and no protocol can be re-run against it. Checked and cleared as a cause: train (512 rows) and test (500 rows) share **0 prompts**; 382/500 test answers also appear as a train answer, which is GSM8K's small integer range, not leakage. **My own earlier claim is retracted**: I called 94.2% impossible for exceeding an "82.5% ceiling" — that 82.5% is the *base* model at n=40, which training may exceed, so the contradiction was a small-n artifact and the real defect is the control. Fixed in #64 (pool follows `--max-new-tokens`, since a flat `num_blocks=512` is 1024 tokens/row over 8 slots and made the uncapped protocol unreachable via `PagedKvPool exhausted` at `kv_cache.py:80`; adapter saved before the after-eval). Re-running before/after in one uncapped 8192/thinking-on configuration. [errors/2026-09-04-the-eval-cap-measured-itself.md](docs/experience/errors/2026-09-04-the-eval-cap-measured-itself.md)

- **phase exit** — **the V100 chat deploy is live at the root URL, and the page's rate is 50.0 tok/s decode-only against 46.3 wall.** Four page changes ckl asked for: a ~60-line markdown renderer (fences split first, escaping before any rule), a live decode-only rate windowed from the FIRST token, TTFT, and the reasoning fold. **The fold was dead for a whole deploy** and the cause is the template: it ends the prompt with `<think>\n`, so generation begins INSIDE the block and the stream carries only `</think>` — measured, a 300-token reply contains the close tag and no open tag — while the page keyed on the open one. One `splitThink(raw, inside)` now serves the live fold and the history strip and one `THINK` constant feeds both the request body and the split, so the page cannot ask for thinking and parse as though it had not. `/` serves the chat UI, the landing page moves to `/about`. **The rate needed attribution, not reporting:** 50.3 was 1.257x the 39.3-40.2 I had published hours earlier off the same server, and acceptance had moved only 1.010x (1.835 tok/forward at 0.829 vs 1.795 at 0.821), the decode-vs-wall口径 1.081x, and length 1.8% from 200 to 390 tokens — so the gap was **my earlier instrument**, `wall_ms / tokens`, which the entry directly above this one already lists as having "read as a 15% serve regression that does not exist"; this run's wall figure 46.3 matches its recorded 46.1 to **1.004x**. **Deploy survives a crash**: 59 lines of bash with restarts capped at 10 (a crash loop is a finding, not a hiccup), flock for one supervisor per pod, a 32 MiB log cap, and TERM forwarded to the child — without that last one the supervisor exited in 1s and left python holding **23466 MiB** of the card with nothing watching it. Not a systemd unit: `loginctl show-user` reports Linger=no here. **Three gate defects fixed in the same tranche**, all found by review rather than by failure: `_script()` sliced with `rsplit("</script>")` and `server.py` now holds two pages, so a two-block page returned **27299 characters of markup** to the bare-call resolver as JavaScript; the supervisor check lived in `scripts/` so pytest collected it **zero** times; and the markdown gate's fence-isolation assertion passed under mutation until it asserted absence rather than presence. 11 passed + 5 skipped here, 5 passed on the pod. [wins/2026-09-04-the-pages-rate-is-50-and-my-39-was-a-rejected-instrument.md](docs/experience/wins/2026-09-04-the-pages-rate-is-50-and-my-39-was-a-rejected-instrument.md)

- **fix** — **the draft skipped two shape buckets the trunk has, and a served first visit paid 15.7x for it.** `engine.py:740` rounds the trunk's prefill width to `_PREFILL_BUCKET = 64` and `:666` sizes its block table to the whole pool, with the comment "the kernels compile it in, so a per-tick width recompiles". `spec.py` did neither — `w = max(hi - lo + 1)` and `nb = max(len(r.blocks))`, both raw — and the draft runs the same two shape-baking kernels (`write_tokens_f32`, `paged_attention_split`, the only two taking `seq_q_lens`), so **every distinct prompt length compiled them inline**, with the block table adding a second axis that grows one column per 16 tokens of context. Measured on the live V100 server, first visit to each length: **37 tok → 14 compiles → 17320 ms → 4.4 tok/s** and 24 tok → 14 → 14766 ms, against **0 compiles → 1104 ms → 36.2 tok/s** after both fixes, with the wall now **flat** across 21/44/48-token prompts (1109/1104/1104 ms) where each unseen length used to be a 14-compile cliff — **15.7x on wall, 8.2x on rate**. Bucketing the width alone left 4-8 compiles, all at S=64 with block tables `[1,1] [1,3] [1,4] [1,5] [1,6]`, which is how the second axis surfaced. **Located by reading `params.pkl` out of `~/.tilelang/cache`** — `[1, 37, 4, 256]` for a 37-token prompt and `[1, 24, 4, 256]` for a 24-token one, exactly `prompt_tokens`, while the trunk's own tensor for the same tick was 64 wide — after **four wrong guesses**: `_PREFILL_BUCKET` (13/15/37 all round to 64, so prefill shape was identical across arms differing 0 vs 14 compiles), block count (`13+76` and `37+76` both add 5), a per-call cache key (an identical repeat compiles zero times), and "each new request opens a new shape" (14 pairs arrived across byte-identical prompts). Safe by construction: `kernels_mma.py:71` already gates the pool write on `if t < SeqQLens[b]` and `sq` stays exact; `_PREFILL_BUCKET` moved to `spec.py` because engine imports spec and both paths must round identically; the verify path shares the table via `bt[live]`. Gated by `tests/test_e2e.py::test_the_draft_prefill_width_is_bucketed_like_the_trunks`, which asserts on the widths `draft.forward` **sees** rather than mirroring the arithmetic — a formula mirror passes even when `spec.py` stops calling it, which is the failure mode this bug had for its whole life. Both mutations run and both fail it. 256 passed / 8 skipped. [wins/2026-09-04-the-draft-skipped-two-shape-buckets.md](docs/experience/wins/2026-09-04-the-draft-skipped-two-shape-buckets.md)

- **measurement** — **a served request pays 10.1x on its first visit to a prompt length, and the entry that called serve immune was wrong.** Live V100 server (27B NVFP4 + draft, `--depth 1 --max-batch 1`), two-arm test with the request window read from `date` on both sides rather than inferred from command order: prompt **13 tok → 0 compiles → 1650 ms → 46.1 tok/s**, **15 tok → 0 → 1811 ms → 42.0**, **37 tok first visit → 14 compiles → 17320 ms → 4.4**, then the **same 37 tok → 0 compiles → 1714 ms → 44.3** and again **1687 ms → 45.0**. The arithmetic closes: `17320 − 1811 = 15509 ms` over 14 compiles is **1108 ms each** against the 1-4 s the log's own begin/complete stamps show, and the server compiled **81 times over 40 minutes**. This **refutes two things published earlier the same day**: the recompile entry's "Bench-only, eager-path … a served tick replays a graph whose compiles were paid once at capture" — `engine.py:223` does force the JIT before capture, but the graph covers the shapes *capture visited* and a request can introduce one it did not — and that entry's open candidate (b), "their cache key includes something that changes per call", which an identical repeat compiling **zero** times kills. Three data points now say first-visit shape specialization (6-compile B=1 bench, 14-compile served first visit, 0-compile repeat); only the 269-compile B=8 run does not, and it stays the outlier. **What keys on 37 is NOT identified** and two candidates are eliminated: not `_PREFILL_BUCKET = 64` (13/15/37 all round to 64, so prefill shape is identical across all three arms) and not block count (`13+76` grows 1→6 blocks, `37+76` grows 3→8, both +5). The pair is always `write_tokens_f32` + `paged_attention_split`, the only two taking `seq_q_lens`. **Cost shape:** not a throughput ceiling but a latency spike on cold shapes, worst exactly when someone first tries the server; a warmup pre-visiting the reachable shape set would hide it, which is the same sizing question the B=8 run left open. **Three instruments failed first**, two of them producing numbers I stated before catching: `wall_ms / tokens` charges prefill to decode (#24's 31 ms/prompt token is ~310 ms of a 1650 ms request, against a bench figure that is decode-only by construction) and read as a **15% serve regression that does not exist**; `curl -N` buffers the SSE body so time-to-first-chunk came back **0 ms**; and counting SSE frames measures `server.py`'s 0.02 s poll sleep, giving **22.6 tok/s** for a server doing 46 — 76 tokens over 39 frames is 1.95, close enough to the real 1.88 tok/forward to look like a tick count. The working instrument is `scripts/probe_served_rate.py`, which brackets `/health`'s cumulative `decode_forwards` around one HTTP request so tok/forward and acceptance are the engine's counters, and `prefill_forwards == 1` proves the prefill sits outside the window. [errors/2026-09-04-a-served-first-visit-pays-10x-and-serve-was-not-immune.md](docs/experience/errors/2026-09-04-a-served-first-visit-pays-10x-and-serve-was-not-immune.md)

- **accept-or-reject verdict** — **#22 closes REJECTED on the clean rung pair, the depth-4 stalls are TileLang compiles, and my own "not a shape set" claim is retracted.** Six groups × four depths at B=1 ctx=1024 (`t74`, `1f58293`) — the first B=1 sweep to reach p3 at all, since every earlier row was `--prompts 3` = p0/p1/p2, so "all groups within 1.075x, therefore B=1 is immune" was a run that never entered the region in question. **Depths 1-3 are flat** (tick spread 1.0879x / 1.0107x / 1.0123x, the first being p1 alone); **depth 4 blows at p3 and p5** — 405.55 and 204.55 ms against a 98.43-100.79 clean floor, **4.12x and 2.08x** — which reproduces the sm90 peer's "nothing before p3" boundary at depth 4 while **inverting it at depth 1**, where the only excursion is p1 and p3/p4/p5 are the flattest three in the set. **Both proposed mechanisms are dead.** The prefill rectangle cannot be it: `measure()` opens its window only after every row is `_PHASE_DECODE` (`ab_draft_depth.py:195-199`) and submits nothing inside it, and `:240-241` already raises on any nonzero `mixed_forwards` delta — which every printed row passed — so capping `max_num_batched_tokens` would cap an absent term and **both readings of that experiment predict no change**, making it null rather than weak; it was not run. Allocator fragmentation, **my** candidate, is refuted by the discriminator registered before the run: **retries 0 in all 24 group-windows** and reserved **19.98G flat to the digit** in all six depth-4 groups, through a 4.12x stall. **What it is: 6 TileLang compiles**, 3 matched `write_tokens_f32`/`paged_attention_split` pairs, all inside 50 s at the depth-3 → depth-4 transition, **12 s of compile wall time** — and depth 4 is the only depth reaching **rung 8** (`r8x244` of 287 ticks; depths 1-3 live on r2/r4), so the two `seq_q_lens` kernels compile for a new shape. The two blown groups carry **16.09 s of draft excess** (tick-side route 19.77 s), agreeing to 18.6% and both within 1.6x of the logged compile time. **`tick − draft` is flat through every excursion** — 0.20% spread across all six groups at depth 1 while the draft alone moved +3.10 ms to explain a tick moving +3.06 — which is a real attribution and *not* the circularity flagged in the derived-verify figure, because there verify was defined as tick minus its own rung's draft and could not fail to absorb, whereas here the draft is CUDA events and the tick is wall clock. **#22's arithmetic, now on the clean pair** (d2 and d3 both 98%+ rung 4, so verify is held constant and one draft forward is the only difference — the subtraction the script exists for): draft share at the shipped depth 3 is **26.5%**, tick ceiling if the draft were free **1.2115x**, collapsed tick 50.56 ms, which at depth 3's measured 2.55 tok/fwd reads **50.4 tok/s against depth 1's measured 50.1 — 1.0067x, inside the script's own 1.16% noise floor**. So a *perfect, zero-cost* block-parallel head is indistinguishable from `--spec-depth 1`, a config flip. At depth 2 a free head **loses outright** (45.43 + 5.72 = 51.15 ms at 2.27 tok/fwd = 44.4 tok/s, **0.8858x**). Break-even needs 2.552 tok/fwd at depth 2 against 2.27 (FAIL) and 2.533 at depth 3 against 2.55 (1.007x). **The reason is the rung, not the draft**: verify r2 29.40 → r4 45.41 is a **16.03 ms step**, 2.89 draft forwards' worth and **1.50x everything a parallel head could remove at depth 3** — and a head emitting W tokens in one forward reads a W-row output, so it pays a smaller version of that same ladder rather than zero. The differenced draft forward (4.83 ms) and the timed one (5.54) disagree **12.8%**; the timed figure is used because a difference amplifies its operands' noise, and the verdict holds either way. **The depth default: depth 1 beats the shipped depth 3 by 1.204x** (50.1 vs 41.6 tok/s), with p1's excursion **kept** — it costs the mean 1.4% and dropping it would be selection on the dependent variable, the error correctly identified in the peer's own 1.078x. **Retracted:** `errors/…-the-recompiles-reproduce-and-are-not-a-shape-set.md` argued from a flat rate that "a finite shape set exhausts; this does not" and declared the shape hypothesis dead — this run shows **6 compiles, 3 pairs, then zero across ~9 minutes and 287 further ticks**, which *is* exhaustion, so the claim was a distribution read off one B=8 log and generalized; the 269-compile run is now the outlier needing explanation. Also corrected: the corpus spread is **2.05-2.59 at depth 2 (26.3%)** and 2.15-3.02 at depth 3 (40.5%) against the 15.8% the docstring records, so that figure is a floor on the spread and any single-group verdict sits inside the band. [wins/2026-09-04-depth-4-stalls-are-compiles-and-block-parallel-closes.md](docs/experience/wins/2026-09-04-depth-4-stalls-are-compiles-and-block-parallel-closes.md)

- **measurement** — **#60's 51 recompiles reproduce on demand; the "not a shape set" half is RETRACTED (see the 09-04 verdict above: 6 compiles then exhaustion at B=1).** The B=8 arm of #72 hit **169 compiles in 7m50s with zero depth rows emitted**, on an invocation that (unlike the ctx=2048 one, where "0 compiles" measured the absent INFO logger) actually logs them. Parsed over the first 273 s: `paged_attention_split` 20 compiles at 3.5 s and `write_tokens_f32` 20 at 2.8 s carry **127 of 132 s — 96% of all compile time — and 48% of the run's wall clock**, while `rmsnorm_apply`/`rmsnorm_partial`/`rope`/`embedding_f16`/`silu_mul`/`gdn_chunk_fused` compile 1-9 times each and stop. Three facts end the shape hypothesis I had left standing: the **rate is flat** at ~1 pair per 12 s across every 30 s bucket (a finite set exhausts; this does not), the count **exceeds the enumerable space** — for this invocation the reachable `(prefill_rows, S_padded)` set is **1 pair**, since `budget = max_num_batched_tokens − len(decodes)` = 512−n and `_PREFILL_BUCKET = 64` round every chunk of a 1152-token prompt to 512, so 20 compiles against one shape — and it is the **same two kernels, matched 20/20**, the only two taking `seq_q_lens`. **No mechanism is named here**: either the disk cache is not consulted for these two or their key changes per call, and the difference from the four earlier wrong attributions (`NB` const, full disk, `_kernel` factory args, `(B,S)` specialization) is that this time a bound I had *published* — "~7 reachable shapes against 51 measured" — was watched climbing past itself. Consequence: **no B=8 row from `ab_draft_depth.py` is usable on either arch** (the sm90 peer's 1-group run buried a 15693 ms compile in a 70-tick mean and read 190.9 tok/s where the clean answer was 320), so #72's batch-dependence needs a harness that pre-visits every shape or the graph path, where `engine.py:223` forces the JIT to finish before capture and a served tick replays. The B=1 verdict is unaffected. Separately, **the peer's ctx=2048 B=8 config cannot run on sm70 at all**: `num_slots = max(batches)+1 = 9` is hardcoded (`ab_draft_depth.py:267`) and `step_states` costs **0.141 GiB per slot per step** on the 27B geometry, so slots=9 depth=4 is 7.59 GiB of GDN state + 4.28 GiB KV + 24.4 GiB weights = **36.3 GiB on a 32 GiB card** — it runs on H20 only because H20 has the memory, which is a constraint on #72 rather than a launch detail.  The run then **OOMed at depth 1** — `Tried to allocate 272.00 MiB, 186.38 MiB free` in `silu_mul` (`backend.py:1202`) — so the memory estimate was wrong in the direction already flagged and **the sm70 B=8 arm does not run at any depth**, which is the reportable row for #72. The **8x is now resolved and my reading of the budget was the error**: `engine.py:741-742` builds one **rectangle** from the WIDEST row — `input_ids = np.zeros((len(rows), width))` — and `max_num_batched_tokens` bounds `sum(chunks)` only, so one 512-token prefill chunk beside 7 decode rows gives `8 x 512 = 4096` rows into `silu_mul`, exactly the observed allocation. The ceiling is therefore not "B=8 needs more memory than this card has" but **"a mixed tick pays the widest row's width on every row"** — a decode-only tick at B=8 is 8 rows x width 5. Distinct from #41's verify-ladder padding, which rounds `rows x W` up the M-ladder; this one pads *decode* rows out to a prefill chunk and its cost is memory rather than launches. Gated in `tests/test_e2e.py`, which also gives `mixed_forwards` its first coverage — three attempts, each killed by its own negative control: asserting on row count passed with the padding removed, reconstructing the tick from outside `step()` read pre-promotion state and measured 0.00x waste, and every `seq_q` first chosen (64, 512) was already a bucket multiple so the rounding was a no-op. 96/65/63 exercise it, and both mutations now fail — dropping the rounding, and dropping the `chunk > 1` gate. [errors/2026-09-04-the-recompiles-reproduce-and-are-not-a-shape-set.md](docs/experience/errors/2026-09-04-the-recompiles-reproduce-and-are-not-a-shape-set.md)

- **accept-or-reject verdict** — **block-parallel drafting is REJECTED, and the shipped `--depth 3` is 1.2522x slower than depth 1 at ctx=1024 / 1.1186x at ctx=2048.** Eight rows, two contexts, all monotone in tick: `d1 48.4 / d2 38.4 / d3 38.7 / d4 24.6` at ctx=1024 and `47.5 / 40.0 / 42.5 / 30.3` at ctx=2048 — depth 1 wins at both, and the margin is **larger at the shorter context**. What makes them trustworthy is a redundancy the harness provides rather than the test I registered: the draft is **timed, not differenced**, verify is derived per-rung, so **one rung measured at three or four independent depths** cross-checks for free — rung 2 spreads **0.17%** across four depths at ctx=1024 (29.38/29.34/29.33/29.36) and **0.16%** across three at ctx=2048, rung 4 **0.26%** and 1.23%. `verify r2 = 29.38` is byte-identical to #60's independent 29.38 on another day. The rung step, measured **rung-locally** at five depth×context points, reads **16.09-17.29 ms** (r4−r2) and **34.67/35.02** (r8−r4) — #60's cross-depth-subtracted 16.11 and 34.82 land inside both, so its staircase figures were right and only its draft cost was not. **Why depth 1 wins, in one number:** entering rung 4 costs the step plus one more draft forward = **60% of the depth-1 tick at both contexts**, against the **23-30%** more acceptance it buys. `cli.py:496`'s help text argues depth 3 fills the rung-4 tile while 4 spills to rung 8; both halves check out and the effect is **1.008-1.062x**, while *not entering* rung 4 is worth **1.119-1.252x** — it optimizes inside the wrong branch of the staircase. **#22's arithmetic, off the depth-1 row** (215 of 215 ticks on one rung): draft share **19.0%**, ceiling if the draft were free **1.2340x**, parallel head at 1.00x our draft cost **0.9982x**, at DSpark's actual **4.08x** parameter count (1.86B vs 456M) **0.6305x**; break-even tok/forward **1.783/2.121/2.823** against **1.78** measured. A parallel head costing exactly what our serial draft costs breaks even at *best*, and block-parallel changes how drafts are *issued*, not how often they are accepted. The four depths also **separate the draft into two terms** — 7.18 ms/forward at d1 against 5.91/5.79/5.75 is a fixed per-call cost over 0.99 forwards instead of 3.80; fitting gives **1.60 ms fixed + 5.28 ms per forward**, worst residual 3.3% — and the 1.60 is `_time_draft`'s own sync (`engine.py:970-977`) which the captured graph does not pay, so the production ceiling is the tighter **1.1620x**. **#71's premise reversed:** depth 4 does improve with context and it is irrelevant — at ctx=2048 it reads 30.3 against depth 1's 47.5, so a depth that improves with context is still 0.64x the best depth *at* that context; measured at depth 1 where both contexts sit on one rung, doubling context costs 3.7% of the tick for 1.7% acceptance, a **2.0% net loss**. **The pre-registered clean-row test turned out to have a hole**, found when the sm90 peer's B=8 row scored **1.0026x** while carrying a 15693 ms rung-2 verify: `verify + fpt·draft` reassembles what the harness had just subtracted, so it is near-tautological once the draft is per-rung — it caught #60 only because those rows pooled the draft across rungs. Replacement is the rung cross-check at identical `(rows, W, ctx)`; **and one flag of mine was wrong** — rung 4/8 differing 2-3x between my B=1 rows and the peer's B=8 is not contamination, since the ladder rounds `rows × W` and attention splits history by `KVSPLIT = f(s)` on query width (`registry.py:23`), so rung identity does not make verify comparable across B. **Not flipped here** (#72): every number is eager-path, and at B=8 depth 1 already sits on rung 32 with depths 2-4, so the staircase argument that wins at B=1 does not exist there. **Not answered**: the 51-recompile cause — this run logs 0 compiles *and* 0 TileLang lines, so that measures the logging; a code read bounds distinct shapes at **~7 against 51 measured**. [wins/2026-09-04-depth-1-wins-and-block-parallel-is-rejected.md](docs/experience/wins/2026-09-04-depth-1-wins-and-block-parallel-is-rejected.md)

- **method** — a **full `/tmp` reads as a run that produced nothing**, and I gave the empty log four mechanisms before checking the disk. The V100's `/` is at **100%, 0 bytes available**, and `/tmp` is on `/`, so every log write in a launch sequence failed silently: the process starts, runs, and its output is discarded, which is indistinguishable from Python block-buffering, from a second arm truncating the first, and from a launch that never detached — **all three are real failure modes on this box**, which is why each had a ready explanation. Two of the four diagnoses were checked against real evidence and still wrong: `O_TRUNC` in `/proc/PID/fdinfo/1` records the flags the file was *opened* with, which every `>` sets, so it is not evidence of a re-truncation, and I **published** "run 1 finished and run 2 truncated it" with those flags quoted as proof — the flags read correctly, the inference invented, same class as tonight's `NB` mechanism and the stale-branch byte count. The discriminator was a **three-line script that only echoes**: `write error: no space left on device`. Relaunched with the log on `/data00`, 1840 bytes in 25 s. The runner also gained `python -u` (a run whose rows only exist if it exits cleanly cannot report where it died), `>>` over `>`, and `set -x` — which would have shown the log empty *from line 1*, before any Python ran and before buffering could be the explanation. This disk was already at 100% earlier the same day and I had used it to explain `nvcc fatal: Failed to preprocess host compiler properties`, then did not check it when the next silent failure arrived. Rule: prove the instrument can read non-empty before explaining the emptiness, and check the boring resource first — `df` costs nothing and a full filesystem fits whatever hypothesis you brought. [errors/2026-09-04-a-full-disk-reads-as-a-run-that-produced-nothing.md](docs/experience/errors/2026-09-04-a-full-disk-reads-as-a-run-that-produced-nothing.md)

- **fix** — the draft-parity test's `argmax` assertion is **a lottery on the drift, not a gate on it**, and my first fix for it was worse than the bug. It compares `int(full.argmax()) == int(got.argmax())`, which carries information only where the top-2 gap exceeds the drift — and on sm90 **two of four positions measured narrower**: `[chunked]` margin **0.2863** against drift **0.3562** FAILED, while `multirow` row 2 at margin **0.2667** / drift **0.3536** — a *worse* ratio — passed. Two near-ties, drift over margin in both, one flipped. **The margins are per-arch**: the same position on cpu reads margin **4.6552** / drift **0.2775**, 16.8x the other way, which is why I first reported the near-tie reading refuted — it is refuted on cpu, and `tiny()` runs different kernels per target (sm90's top-2 there are tokens 226/262, cpu's are 145/146, a different distribution rather than a reordering). Two instrument errors surfaced getting here: the peer's first logit dump (~0.01–0.21 deltas) was **pytest's truncated repr** of a 320-wide tensor, head and tail with the middle elided — caught because 0.21 cannot flip a 4.66 margin, so the arithmetic did not close; and **my own fix, gating the assert on `margin > drift`, was killed by its negative control** — corrupting one logit by +50 gives margin 2.4555 against drift 50.0787, so **the gate closes precisely when the draft is most broken**, the condition being backwards for exactly the case it existed to catch. Fixed by **deleting the argmax compare**: `rel = ||full-got||/||full||` already sat three lines below and measures the difference instead of betting on it — sm90's real range is **1.617e-02 to 5.822e-02** against the 0.1 bound, and the +50 control reads **3.235e-01**, caught with 3.2x margin over the bound and 5.6x over the worst real drift. No coverage that was ever real is lost; the argmax token is kept in the failure message as diagnosis. 251 passed / 8 skipped. [errors/2026-09-04-the-argmax-gate-was-a-lottery-on-the-drift.md](docs/experience/errors/2026-09-04-the-argmax-gate-was-a-lottery-on-the-drift.md)

- **phase exit** — **sm70 is green: 245 passed, 0 failed, 14 skipped in 51.9 s at ff75987**, on `Tesla V100-SXM2-32GB` from a clean checkout. That closes the compatibility matrix, and the answer to the isolation question is that **all 14 sm70 failures were test defects and 0 were product defects** — pytest was never installed on that pod, so the sm70 column had never existed. First run at b20ca9b was 13 failed / 230 passed. Four classes, every one a test encoding a target assumption rather than a product symbol differing per target: **8** the test helper's own `PagedKvPool` taking bf16 by default against sm70's f32 kernel ABI; **3** `torch.equal` on `Float8_e4m3fn`, which torch 2.5.1 has no `equal_cpu` for; **2** fp4 parity using `target.startswith("cuda")` as a stand-in for "has a w4a8 kernel" — true on sm90, false on sm70, so the test charged the kernel for e4m3's ~2% error on both operands and read `max abs diff 6.695e-01`; **1** the graph's pad row. One run in between reported 7 failed / 7 errors and was **not a code result**: the V100's root filesystem was **100% full, 0 bytes available**, so everything writing under `/tmp` died in `safetensors` with `No space left on device`. Nothing was deleted to fix it (`/tmp/hf_cache` alone is 37 GB — a `Qwen3.5-9B` snapshot under **this** account, not someone else's as first written here) — the runner now sets `TMPDIR` to the 46 GB-free `/data00` volume. Worth recording because 14 simultaneous failures across two unrelated files reads exactly like a regression and the discriminator was one `df -h`. Final matrix: `cpu 251+0 / metal 244+4 (same 4 on main) / sm90 248+1 (main: 200+1, SAME test) / sm70 245+0`, plus four sm90 throughput cells whose **worst ratio is 1.002x against a 0.97x gate** with acceptance byte-identical to main at B=1 (54329/73983). **Still unexercised on H20**: `_fit_blocks`' CUDA arithmetic — `test_serve_sizes_...` turned out to be a test defect rather than the coverage it looked like, and the sm90 throughput cells pass `num_blocks=512` explicitly, so `serve --blocks 0` is unmeasured *there*. It was measured on the V100 earlier the same day (#62: 3927 blocks = 62832 tokens at slots=3, matching prediction exactly), so "no coverage on any card" — which both of us wrote tonight — is wrong and is corrected here. [wins/2026-09-04-the-sm70-test-row-existed-for-the-first-time.md](docs/experience/wins/2026-09-04-the-sm70-test-row-existed-for-the-first-time.md)

- **fix** — the mma8 branches re-target `wq`/`scale` from the plan's `Np` to `Np32`, and **the two rungs do not order**, so the re-target asks `_pad2d` to shrink: `Np = _round_up(N, bN)` with `bN` from the plan's N tile (64 on the fp4 decode arm) overshoots `Np32 = _round_up(N, 32)` whenever bN's rounding jumps past 32 — N=24 gives Np=64 against Np32=32, which is the sm90 suite's `_pad2d: (64, 256) exceeds the target [32, 256]`. It is a **class of N** (24, 40, 96, 130 …), not one shape, and `pack_fp4(torch.randn(24, 32))` in the parity test is exactly N=24. **My published claim was wrong**: I wrote that the fp4 twin "does not pre-pad, which is why only fp8 breaks" — it does not pre-pad on the **M** axis, it pre-pads on **N**, and I had compared the two branches only on the axis the fp8 bug occupied. `main` passes both fp4 parity tests **while feeding the kernel a `wq` cropped from 64 rows to 32** — the rows are zero pad, so its numbers were right by luck, second instance of the same class as the fp8 site. Fixed with one helper rather than three patches, which is the peer's sweep: `_fit_rows` drops rows off a plane padded to a wider rung and pads otherwise, applied at all three re-target sites (fp4 `wq`/`scale`, fp8 `w8`/`wscale`, fp8 `x2`, replacing a hand-rolled slice); the other 10 `_pad2d` sites were checked individually — the two residual ones reshape to `(M, N)` with `M <= _MX`, so their pads stay non-negative. **The gate is the equality, not the shape**: rows between the real N and either rung are zero, so `_fit_rows(_pad2d(p, Np), Np32)` must equal `_pad2d(p, Np32)` elementwise, and it also asserts `Np > Np32` really occurs or `_fit_rows` is dead code — on the CPU target, where neither mma8 branch can execute. Negative control, no card: `_pad2d: (64, 8) exceeds the target [32, 8]`. Separately, `test_serve_sizes_its_pools_from_the_flags_not_the_context` read `65 == 64` on **both** CUDA arches while passing on cpu and metal: `_graph_on` is True by default on CUDA and the captured tick's padding row owns a block reserved up front, so the test read an intentional reservation as an off-by-one in `_fit_blocks`. 251 passed / 8 skipped. [errors/2026-09-04-the-mma8-N-rung-does-not-order-with-the-plans.md](docs/experience/errors/2026-09-04-the-mma8-N-rung-does-not-order-with-the-plans.md)

- **fix** — the draft's vocab readout was **reduced on two call sites of three**, and the one it missed is the path every tick goes through. `DraftHead.step` (`spec.py:404`) called `forward` with no `last_only`, so a 512-position prefill chunk built a **[512, 248320] f32 readout — 485.9 MiB — and read one row of it**; the decode-graph capture (`engine.py:239`) and the trunk verify tick (`757`) both pass it. That is the allocation that killed lc17's ctx=8192 warmup (task #57) with 466 MiB free, three frames in at `backend.py:534`. Task #43 built the reduction machinery and measured -1.53 GiB from it, but off the graph path, and its entry names a saving without naming how many callers were changed. **No correctness gate could see this**: `step` sliced `logits[rng, last]` itself, so the token is byte-identical either way — the only observable is peak memory, and every spec test runs a tiny model whose readout is 6 x 300 floats. The existing parity spy even read the wide tensor and its comment called the index "the last valid row either way", documenting the wide shape as normal. Fixed by passing the per-row width `step` already builds (`last_only=sq`, the same argument `engine.py:757` passes for the trunk): **485.9 MiB → 0.95 MiB per chunk, 512x**, decode ticks unaffected. Gate: the parity spy now asserts the shape instead of reading around it — `out.shape[1] == 1` for any multi-position tick — placed one frame in from the bug so it covers all three callers; negative control run with `__pycache__` cleared, reverting the one line fails **all six** arms at `7 positions wide for a 7-position tick` and nothing else. 248 passed. It survived #42, #43, #44 and #45 — four consecutive memory investigations on this code — because each measured a configuration whose readout happened to fit; it surfaced when the pool grew, not when the code changed. [errors/2026-09-04-the-draft-readout-was-never-reduced-on-prefill.md](docs/experience/errors/2026-09-04-the-draft-readout-was-never-reduced-on-prefill.md)

- **default flip** — `serve --slots` goes **16 → 8**, worth **3.50x the served context** (12112 → 42384 tokens on a 32 GB V100) for **zero lost concurrency**. Answered by reading the admission path rather than by a throughput A/B: a slot is allocated in `submit` (`engine.py:462`) and freed at finish, while `max_batch` caps concurrent rows in `_build_plan` (`571`) — so **`slots` bounds submitted-but-unfinished requests (queue depth) and `max_batch` bounds concurrency**, `slots >= max_batch` is *required* (every admitted row holds one), and above `max_batch` each extra slot only buys a request that waits instead of getting a 503 (already handled: `LinearStatePool exhausted` → `server.py:178` → 503, and `submit`'s except frees both blocks and slot). Measured, 27B + draft + depth 3, one `build_engine(num_blocks=0)` per process: slots **3 → 3927 blocks = 62832 tok** (but `max_batch 8` unreachable), **8 → 2649 = 42384**, **16 → 757 = 12112**. All three answer with byte-identical output, so this is capacity, not quality. 8 is the smallest value keeping the shipped `--max-batch 8` reachable; what it gives up is 8 queue positions, and on a card whose measured B=8 ceiling is already ctx=32 those were buying admission capacity the batch ceiling cannot use. **New guard**: the engine warns when `usable_slots < max_batch`, naming both numbers and the graph's pad row — warn rather than clamp, since `num_slots=2` with the default `max_batch=8` is a legitimate test config (4 in the suite now emit it).
