That was the failure the old default hid in the other direction: nothing said `slots`, not `max_batch`, is the real concurrency ceiling. **Rate across 12112–62832 tokens stays unmeasured (#57)**, so the claim is capacity only. [wins/2026-09-04-slots-default-8-not-16.md](docs/experience/wins/2026-09-04-slots-default-8-not-16.md)

- **fix** — `serve --blocks 0 --draft` **OOMed at its own default `--slots 16`**, and `--blocks 0` is the default, so this was the shipped path. It dies in `reference.py:245`'s twiddle with **104 MiB free** and never prints a pool. Cause is the allocation order in `build_engine`: the state pool, then `_fit_blocks` taking **2/3 of what is free**, then `PagedKvPool`, then `PrefixStore` taking a quarter of the rest — and only THEN does `Engine.__init__` quantize the draft head's own weights, which are charged to nothing. `_fit_blocks` already takes `draft_layers`, but that reserves the draft's KV *mirror*, a different quantity from its *weights*, and the name made it easy to believe both were covered. **Why it never showed**: the recorded 4046-block measurement ran `probe_kv_ceiling.py` at `--slots 3`, which leaves 3.04 GiB free by accident; serve defaults to 16, where `step_states` (scaling `slots x width`, not `max_batch`) leaves nothing for 2/3-of-free to spend. Fixed by serving the draft's weights before anything reads free memory — the same ordering the state pool already uses, allocate then *measure*. After: slots=16 reads **757 blocks = 12112 tokens and answers**, slots=3 unchanged at 3927 = 62832, both with byte-identical output. **Task #62 closed on the way**: `_fit_blocks`'s CUDA branch had never run end to end since `b36e45a`, and at slots=3 it returns **3927 blocks = 62832 tokens against a prediction of exactly 3927/62832** from the corrected 1/16-of-a-block draft term — so the 4046/64736 in `cli.py`'s help and the 64912 in `engine.py`'s comment were both pre-fix and now carry the measured figure with the flags it was measured at. Gate: the ordering test gains a drafted arm asserting `["draft", "state", "fit"]`; negative control run — reverting the move fails it at `index 0 diff: 'state' != 'draft'` and nothing else. The gate also caught a second dict copy in my own fix (`Engine.__init__` re-serves, idempotently) and is written to allow exactly one rather than hide it. **Still open (#65)**: 16 slots costs 5.19x the KV pool; the throughput side of 12112 tokens against 62832 is unpriced. [errors/2026-09-04-the-fit-spent-the-drafts-own-weight-bytes.md](docs/experience/errors/2026-09-04-the-fit-spent-the-drafts-own-weight-bytes.md)

- **measurement** — the verify rung step is **92.9% fp4 GEMV at a flat launch count**, which closes the last open piece of the block-parallel reject. The step matters because #22 now rests on rung 8's verify (80.31 ms) exceeding our whole k=3 tick (62.74), and nothing had attributed it; it is also the wrong shape for bandwidth, since a rung-W verify streams the *same* weights as W=1 and X's own traffic at M=8 is 27.03 MB = **0.030 ms** against a measured **8.71 ms/row marginal — 2320x the byte cost**. Profiled by class at ctx=1024 over depths 1-4 in one process: at the 4→8 crossing the GPU forward grows 32.92 ms and the GEMV carries **30.58 of it (92.9%)**; at 2→4 it is 84.1%; every other class moves under 0.8 ms, attention included at 2.0%. **The launch count is the control that makes this an M cost**: 313.4 → 338.0 calls/forward, **+2.6%** across four depths, against a GEMV that grows 3.06x. Second control, and the one a rung claim needs: **d2 and d3 are both rung 4**, and their GEMV differs +2.8% against a launch count differing +2.6% — so inside one rung the GEMV tracks launches, not depth, i.e. that growth is the extra draft forward and not a dearer verify. Two instruments agree on the per-row cost: profiler GEMV **7.52 / 7.64 ms/row** against the end-to-end tick's **8.06 / 8.71** (1.07x, 1.14x), the residual being the non-GEMV classes the same table prices at 7.1% and 4.4%. **REJECT stands on a measured kernel property**: flipping it needs rung 8 under 62.74 ms, i.e. the GEMV **1.34x faster at M=8**, where SMEM staging measured 0.72x, PIPELINE 0.99x, occupancy 1.00x, NO_SCALE 0.98x, NO_DECODE 0.98x — none a speedup. The kernel written for exactly this range, `make_linear_fp4_mma8` ("Marlin-style decode GEMM for M <= 8"), **cannot run here**: it issues `mma.m16n8k16`, Ampere+. What reopens #22 is a new M≤8 sm70 GEMV, not a re-derivation. One dead end recorded: `record_shapes=True` does not separate a TileLang kernel's shapes (it groups by the launching ATen op's shapes, and there is no ATen op above a raw CUDA kernel — all 330 launches came back as one empty row, caught by the guard rather than published). [wins/2026-09-04-the-rung-step-is-93-percent-gemv.md](docs/experience/wins/2026-09-04-the-rung-step-is-93-percent-gemv.md)

- **correction** — the draft forward is **5.75 ms, not 3.93**, and the missing 1.8 ms was sitting in `VERIFY_MS` — so the whole sm70 cost staircase moves and the block-parallel reject now rests on a different fact. Measured directly with CUDA events on `_draft.step`, twice (ds16, ds17): **6.67 / 5.97 / 5.75 / 6.03 ms/forward** at depths 1-4, the two runs agreeing to 1.2% at every depth. **The two instruments agree** — within one run at the same rung, the subtraction reads a marginal **5.21 ms** against the direct **5.29 ms**, 1.6% apart — so `3.93` was one draw of a 12.9x-amplified difference, not a different quantity. What the amplification cost was in the *other* term: verify per rung reads **29.38 / 45.49 / 80.31** against the shipped **32.79 / 49.52 / 86.24** (0.896 / 0.919 / 0.931x), and per-rung verify now agrees **across depths to 0.15-0.31%** (rung 2 at four depths, rung 4 at three) where the old method asserted that agreement by construction. **The "systematic 5-7% offset, left alone deliberately" paragraph is withdrawn** — an offset uniform across three rungs is what a misattributed additive term looks like, and the entry picked the reading that required no action. Two downstream errors surfaced: the **9.28 ms break-even was a unit error** (= 2.36 tok/fwd × 3.93 ms; the correct solve is 5.78 ms = 1.47x, so the budget was overstated 1.61x), and the gate's **1.06 margin bound is breached at 1.104x** and is therefore **removed**, as its own comment required. Two replacement asserts were caught by the negative control as **structurally true** — `((V+3D)-V)/D` is algebraically 3 whatever the measurement — and replaced. **REJECT stands on a new, falsifiable fact**: rung 8's verify alone (**80.31 ms**) exceeds our *entire* k=3 tick (**62.74 ms**), so the k=7 arm a block head exists to buy reads **0.862x even with a free forward and zero accuracy loss**; it needs neither the decay model nor DSpark's 4.08x parameters, and it flips if rung 8 gets 1.28x cheaper (task #21). Both asserts fire under a control pricing rung 8 at 50.00 ms, checked separately. Harness fix that made it measurable: bucket draft timings by the tick's own rung, and drop the prefill drain's 6 eager ticks (they read 165.97 ms/forward alone and had pulled depth 3 to 7.68). [wins/2026-09-04-a-difference-amplifies-its-operands-noise.md](docs/experience/wins/2026-09-04-a-difference-amplifies-its-operands-noise.md)

- **measurement** — Task #40 answered: **a verify tick costs its RUNG, not its useful rows.** Solved at B=2 from depths 2 and 3 both landing on rung 8, so two tick times give two equations: draft **6.40 ms**, verify **83.40 ms**. The same rung with 3 of 8 rows idle (B=1 depth 4) costs **82.15 ms** — 60% more useful rows for **1.5%** more time. Confirmed independently at rung 32 from two different batch sizes: **190.8 ms with 12 useful rows (B=4 depth 2) against 191.1 ms with 10 (B=2 depth 4), 0.16% apart.** This replaces the "a padding row costs 3.3x a useful one" figure recorded on 09-03, which was inferred from two different launches; `engine.py`'s between-rungs warning is repriced onto the measured pair. Two consequences: **a batch-size inversion is a width-selection bug, not a kernel bug** (B·W landing one row past a boundary buys the whole next rung — which is the mechanism behind a peer's B=1 spec 1.728x vs B=8 0.928x), and **ncols=2's microbench win is spent on the padding its own gate implies** (at B=2 depth 4, M=10 rounds to rung 32, crosses onto the *faster* kernel, and still costs 1.86x the rung-8 tick for 1.04x the tokens — net 0.559x). On wikitext depth 1 wins at every batch measured and its margin GROWS with B: **1.259x at B=1, 1.371x at B=2**, always because it is the last width on the cheap rung. **B=4 is now measured and does not reverse it**: depth 1 reads **1.513x** the shipped depth 3 (73.4 vs 48.5 tok/s), so the margin grows monotonically with batch — 1.259 / 1.371 / 1.513 at B=1/2/4. Rung-32 verify derives to **170.03 ms at depth 2 (52 ticks) and depth 3 (46), 169.47 at depth 4 (47)** — three depths with 12/16/20 useful rows agreeing to **0.33%**, the rung thesis with no cross-batch subtraction in it. **Depth 3 is the within-rung optimum and one prediction was refuted getting there**: the entry's first draft said a chain position is free inside a rung, so depth 4 should win; measured, acceptance saturates (8.17 → 8.45 → 8.45 tok/fwd) and depth 4 costs 7.7% more tick for zero tokens — the verify launch is free inside a rung, the draft forward that produces the position is not. **This also reprices #22 at the serving batch, where it gets harder**: drafting is 15% of a rung-32 tick against 19-24% at B=1, so a block head replacing three forwards with one has a ceiling of **1.115x** (against 1.16-1.21x) and must retain **89.7%** of tok/forward (against 82.7%); asserted in `spec.py`, negative control run — pricing rung 32 at rung 4's cost reads 1.346x and fails. **No default flipped** — every row is wikitext and GSM8K acceptance puts depth 3 ahead at B=1, so the default needs the serving mix, not another batch size. [wins/2026-09-04-rung-cost-not-useful-rows.md](docs/experience/wins/2026-09-04-rung-cost-not-useful-rows.md)

- **correction** — the draft-forward cost, which every block-parallel number descends from, is derived by **subtracting two rung-sharing tick means, and that amplifies their noise by operand/difference — measured 12.9x.** Two runs of the identical command agree to **0.81%** on the depth-3 tick (62.2 vs 61.7 ms) and disagree **10.42%** on the draft taken from them (5.30 vs 4.80); 62/(62−57) = 12.9 predicts it exactly. Propagated at a fixed rung-4 verify, the block-parallel ceiling is **1.16x–1.21x**, so the three-figure values printed for it at various times (1.147x, 1.200x, 1.209x) were quoting the run. **REJECT stands and is unaffected** — it rests on DSpark's 4.08x parameter count against a 2.36x budget, not on a margin this instrument can resolve; what retires is the *margin* argument. `Engine._draft_ms` (off by default) now brackets `_draft.step` with CUDA events, dividing by forwards the head **counts** rather than by depth, because the chain loop breaks when a row runs out of blocks and dividing by depth under-prices the draft in the direction that flatters speculation. `ab_draft_depth.py --time-draft` arms it. Gate `test_the_draft_forward_counter_counts_forwards_not_ticks`; negative control run — moving the increment to once per `step()` reads 11 against the real count and fails. The `VERIFY_MS` constants are left alone deliberately: they are measured directly, and their 5.0–7.4% offset is **uniform across all three rungs**, i.e. a prompt difference rather than instrument error, and conservative in the direction the gate needs. [wins/2026-09-04-a-difference-amplifies-its-operands-noise.md](docs/experience/wins/2026-09-04-a-difference-amplifies-its-operands-noise.md)

- **fix** — `_fit_blocks`, which sizes `tilerl serve`'s KV pool from free memory, priced a block wrong two ways and **neither raises**: over-ask and the OOM lands in whatever allocates next, under-ask and serve quietly loses context. `per_block` already carries the K+V factor, so one draft layer is `per_block / len(full_attn_layers)`; dividing by `2 * len` charged **half a layer** and over-asked 3.03% on the 27B (148 blocks too many at 15.17 GiB free). And the draft term was **unconditional**, so every dense engine — all of training — paid for a pool it never builds. Found while attributing a real OOM: the depth sweep's hardcoded `num_blocks=2048` cost 3.0 GiB across the trunk pool *and the draft's mirror of it* to hold 288 blocks of live context, and the traceback named `linear_fp4` three frames from the cause. Gate `test_the_block_fit_prices_a_block_at_what_the_pools_actually_allocate` **inverts** the function rather than restating it — blocks returned x the bytes `PagedKvPool` really takes, against the 2/3-of-free it set out to spend, at draft_layers 0 and 1; negative control run, the old expression reads 0.6667x and fails. **Still open (#62)**: the CUDA branch itself has zero coverage on any hardware — it returns early for non-CUDA and every bench passes `num_blocks` explicitly, so `serve --blocks 0` has never been exercised on sm70 or sm90.

- **fix** — the depth sweep's "per-passage" rows were **one passage repeated**. Adding `--batch` replaced `for p in prompts` with `prompts[:B]`, so at B=1 every repetition measured `prompts[0]` and printed four identical `tok/fwd` values — which reads as a clean instrument and is a stuck one. It hid the only error bar these numbers have: **acceptance varies 15.8% between wikitext passages** (2.49/2.44/2.15 tok/fwd at W=4, p = 0.689/0.676/0.592), while run-to-run variation is nil (2.49 twice, three digits). The earlier report of a "5.5% cross-run spread" is withdrawn — it compared a 3-passage mean against one of those passages. The script now cuts prompts into disjoint groups of B, refuses a `--prompts` that is not a multiple of `--batch` instead of dropping the remainder, and prints the group count so a single-group run says on its own header that it shows no spread. The depth win it has to clear is 1.19x on the best passage and 1.30x on the worst, so the spread does not reach the winner; the corpus does. [wins/2026-09-04-depth-default-is-wrong-on-text.md](docs/experience/wins/2026-09-04-depth-default-is-wrong-on-text.md)

- **fix** — a test I added yesterday was **flaky, 2 failures in 6 runs**, and it failed for the reason its own comment three lines above warned about: the live-engine SSE gate asserted `deltas[-1] != expected` ("nothing streamed while it generated"), but the delta count off a live engine is a race between generation speed and the loop's 20 ms poll, and the tiny model can finish a 24-token reply inside one window. It passed every time it ran alone, which is what made it look like an ordering bug. The assertion is **removed, not weakened**: `test_a_reply_that_arrives_over_many_polls_streams_over_many_deltas` already asserts the same invariant against a stepped engine that reveals one token per poll by construction. Coverage verified by mutation — with `rstrip("�")` changed back to `split("�")[0]` the deterministic test fails on its own, so removing the racy copy loses nothing. 8/8 clean runs after, against 2-in-6 before. [errors/2026-09-04-draft-wrote-an-unallocated-block.md](docs/experience/errors/2026-09-04-draft-wrote-an-unallocated-block.md)

- **fix** — the draft wrote a KV position **the trunk had no block for**, and the filed description of the bug was wrong in two ways. `engine.py:707` grows `r.blocks` for `decodes` only, but `_draft.step(rows)` runs on every row including one that just finished prefill, and the draft writes position `seq_len - 1`: a 15-token prompt owns exactly one 16-token block and the draft immediately asks for block index 1 of a 1-column table — `IndexError: index 1 is out of bounds` from `kv_cache.py:149`, three frames away inside the trunk's own writer. Task #58 called this "multi-row chunked prefill"; **it needs neither** — it reproduces with one block, no chunking, on the first tick after prefill, and the 32-token case had hidden the cause behind two irrelevant variables. Found by extending `ab_draft_depth.py` along the batch axis, which crashed on its first B=2 submit. **The first fix was wrong and the gate caught it**: clamping the draft's span to the blocks a row owns (mirroring the hidden-span clamp above it) stops the crash and leaves a **hole in the draft's KV**, so the next position attends over it — the engine then drafted token 79 at position 22 where full context drafts 61, i.e. a crash traded for silent divergence. The shipped fix allocates what the draft will write, in the layer that owns allocation. Gate `test_engine_draft_matches_full_context_draft[blockedge]` — `plen=15`, batched, unchunked, the minimal form; the existing comparison against a full-context draft catches both the crash and the divergence. Negative control run: with the growth loop disabled both `blockedge` and `manychunks` fail. `manychunks`'s `xfail(strict)` is removed rather than flipped, 241 passed / 7 skipped. Also guarded `ab_draft_depth.py`'s `torch.cuda.synchronize()` so its batching logic can be exercised on the CPU cell at all. [errors/2026-09-04-draft-wrote-an-unallocated-block.md](docs/experience/errors/2026-09-04-draft-wrote-an-unallocated-block.md)

- **measurement** — the spec **depth winner flips with the corpus**, and the entry's original "wrong on text" headline is corrected in place: at ctx=1024 B=1 on wikitext-103, depth 1 reads **48.5 tok/s against the shipped depth 3's 38.3 — 1.266x**, repeatable to 0.3% across two independent runs at 1530 MHz with throttle reasons 0x0. The mechanism is the rung, not acceptance: depth 3 buys 1.356x the tokens (2.36 vs 1.74) for 1.719x the tick (61.70 vs 35.92, rung 4 against rung 2), netting 0.789x, and W=2 is the last width on the cheap rung. **Cost is prompt-independent, acceptance is not, and that is the finding** — same code and same rungs, random ids vs wikitext agree on ms/tick within 3.6% at every depth (34.98/54.43/61.63 vs 35.92/56.41/61.70) while acceptance does not: **text accepts worse than uniform-random ids** (2.36 vs 2.99 at W=4), which inverts the choice — depth 3 wins on random ids at 48.4 and loses to depth 1 on text. So the "random vocabulary is the pessimistic end" label in two earlier entries is **wrong**, and both are annotated. **Not flipped**: every number is B=1 ncols=1 while serving runs B=4 ncols=2, where W=2 launches 8 rows against W=4's 32 — task #40 is that measurement (now taken: a padding row costs what a USEFUL one costs, see below), and flipping now would trade a measured B=1 win for an unmeasured B=4 regression. Method: `scripts/corpus.py` loads the wikitext test split with a `spans()` self-check that refuses a short prompt; `ab_draft_depth.py` takes `--prompt wikitext|random`. **One run was contaminated and caught by its own arithmetic** — a first wikitext pass read 97.28 ms and 24.3 tok/s at depth 3, a 1.58x outlier against the two runs after it, with *identical* tick counts, and it produced a **negative verify cost (−24.33 ms)** which is what exposed it; at 20% instead of 58% it would have published a plausible curve. The script now samples SM clock, throttle reasons and load per depth and prints per-passage rows. Also **corrects yesterday's claim that the block-parallel verdict is prompt-independent**: it is so in form but not in value — the decay factor rises as p falls (0.847 → 0.885 → 0.902), so *lower* acceptance favours the parallel head, and the wikitext p gives the arm its best reading yet at **1.035x**. Reject stands, carried by the 4.08x parameter gap against a 2.36x budget, not by the margin. `spec.py`'s gate now derives our own rate from the same staircase and p instead of a 42.7 tok/s hardcoded from a different prompt, and its bound is the decay model's 1.06 rather than a noise floor it never gated. [wins/2026-09-04-depth-default-is-wrong-on-text.md](docs/experience/wins/2026-09-04-depth-default-is-wrong-on-text.md)

- **accept** — **speculative decode is accepted at B=1 and stays rejected at B=8.** 200 GSM8K, greedy, `max_new_tokens=512`, decode graph on, both arms one process on an H20: B=1 base **78.4** vs spec-w8 **135.5 tok/s (1.728x)**, B=8 base 242.9 vs spec-w8 225.4 (0.928x). B=1 is the shape a rollout has. At B=1 the trunk runs **6.11x fewer forwards** (64565 -> 10569) for a 1.728x wall clock, so a width-8 spec tick costs 3.54x a width-1 tick, of which the verify tick is the recorded 2.41x and the drafter is the rest; at B=8 the base tick already amortises across 8 rows and the same forward reduction buys less than the spec tick costs. The B=8 arm reproduces the recorded pair (base 232.3 to +4.6%) and shows what the batched selector walk was worth: spec **139.4 -> 225.4, 1.617x**, against the 3.64x ceiling this repo had projected, with **acceptance unchanged at 6.17 vs 6.18 of 8** — the drafter got faster without getting different. Two measurement facts recorded with it, each after invalidating a run: the **`max_new_tokens` cap is part of the result** (at 256 the base arm's GSM8K reads 38.5% against 85.0%, because completions average 236 tokens and lose their final answer to the cap), and **every spec number in this repo before today is a B=8 number** — `scripts/acc_spec_arms.py` had `num_slots` and both eval concurrencies as the literal `8`. `--concurrency` now exists and the B=1 rows carry their own control, `tok/decode-fwd` reading 1.00 against 7.79 at B=8. [wins/2026-09-03-batched-selector-walk.md](docs/experience/wins/2026-09-03-batched-selector-walk.md)

## 2026-09-03

- **correction** — the depth sweep's knob was **dead in the tree**, and repairing it moved every number the block-parallel verdict rests on. `scripts/ab_draft_depth.py` set `e._spec_depth`, live at `engine.py:893` until `7069a1f` moved the chain loop onto the head; both of the script's commits are *after* that, so the committed script never varied depth — it would have measured one config four times and reported drafting as nearly free. (The 09-02 measurement itself predates the refactor: its own table reads 60.93 vs 66.46 for depths 2 and 3, so the knob was live in the uncommitted version that produced it.) Gate `test_the_engines_verify_width_is_reachable_from_outside` moves both holders of the width and asserts the move reaches `graph_keys`; negative control run — stubbing `set_depth` to a no-op fails it and nothing else in the suite. **The number moved for a second reason, which is the substantive one**: `verify_lens` trims each tick's chain, so a configured depth is an upper bound and each depth runs a *mixture* of rungs — depth 2 ran 15 rung-2 ticks and 56 rung-4, depth 3 ran 14 and 55, so subtracting the two depths' mean ticks drags 16% of all ticks across the 16.73 ms rung step and charges it to the draft. Bucketing ticks by their own rung: one draft forward **5.53 → 3.93 ms**, share **25.0% → 19.2%**, block-parallel ceiling **1.200x → 1.147x**. Internal check the mean-tick version could not make: rung-4 verify derives to **49.52 ms independently at both depths** (56 and 55 ticks). **REJECT stands, and its margin is a few percent, not 32%** — the previous pass compared a yield re-derived at p=0.554 against a break-even taken from tpf=3.34, two prompts in one comparison; break-even scales *with* tok/forward, so the only prompt-independent form is `verdict = (yield/tok_fwd) × ceiling`, which reads **0.972x / 1.016x / 1.062x** at tpf 3.34 / 2.62 / 2.03. Note the direction: the tighter draft measurement made the ceiling **worse**, because a cheaper draft is less to remove. The 0.675x recorded earlier today is corrected to 1.111x. And the ceiling still assumes the parallel head's one forward costs what ours does — DSpark's head is **4.08x the parameters** (1.86B vs 456M) against a 2.36x budget. `spec.py`'s gate reprices onto the new staircase {2: 32.79, 4: 49.52, 8: 86.24}; its wider-is-free control trips two asserts, checked **separately** because an assert that only fires after an earlier failure has not been shown to do anything. [errors/2026-09-03-block-parallel-drafting-is-1.016x-on-sm70.md](docs/experience/errors/2026-09-03-block-parallel-drafting-is-1.016x-on-sm70.md)

- **correction** — the "tick cost is not linear in context" open question is **answered and the claim withdrawn**: it was a verify-rung crossing read as a context cost. Profiling ctx 1024 vs 2048 by kernel, `fp4 GEMV` carried **+7.09 of the +10.79 ms/forward at an identical call count** (330.2 → 330.0 calls/forward) — which no shape change explains until the chain width is printed: the mean drafted chain rises **2.28 → 3.70** and crosses `LADDER_WIDTHS` rung 2 → 4. Same staircase error as `errors/2026-09-01-spec-depth-is-a-staircase-not-a-line.md`, invisible this time because the GEMV launch count is per-layer and identical on both rungs. The clean control is the **dense** path, pinned to rung 1 so the shapes cannot move: GPU **24.92 / 25.55 / 26.73 ms/forward** at ctx 1024/2048/4096, with GEMV flat to 0.05% and **attention carrying 99.4% of the delta at a dead-linear 0.600 ms per 1K** (1.15/1.75/2.95, fitting `0.55 + 0.600 × ctx/1K`). Order was controlled both ways: the spec arm read 29.43 GEMV running first and 29.50 running second (0.24%), and GEMV's flatness across 4x context is the dense arm's own control. Two numbers this leaves — attention runs **4.0x its byte floor** (0.149 ms/1K for 128 KiB/token at 900 GB/s; the withdrawn paragraph compared a *4-row* floor against a 1-row slope, which is what made it look 15x off), and **the wider chain at long context is a loss**: +0.16 tok/forward for +10.0 ms/forward, because `verify_lens` trims on draft confidences that rise with context and spends a rung on tokens the verifier rejects. Capping verify width by context is a candidate lever, **not sized here** — the profiler's 82.0 ms/forward carries overhead the 61.6 ms bench tick does not, and dividing one by the other would fabricate the ratio. `prof_decode_budget.py` now takes several contexts in one process, normalizes by **forward** rather than by token (per-token folds acceptance into a cost), prints the mean chain, and gates the comparison in `comparable()` with a `--self-check`; negative control run — dropping the rounding makes 2.28 and 3.70 share a rung and the check stops flagging the crossing it was written for. [wins/2026-09-03-long-context-decode-is-all-tick-cost.md](docs/experience/wins/2026-09-03-long-context-decode-is-all-tick-cost.md)

- **measurement** — the long-context sweep changed the prompt, and re-measured cleanly **decode's context curve is all tick cost, with acceptance flat**. The old sweep drew `range(10 + i*ctx, ...)`, so prompt *content* was a function of length and `tok/forward` mixed two causes; those rows (including the `38.0 tok/s at ctx=8192` below) are withdrawn. On a prompt drawn from one fixed distribution over the whole vocabulary, ctx 1024/2048/4096 read **38.8 / 34.1 / 31.8 tok/s** at **2.03 / 2.10 / 2.10 tok/fwd**: acceptance rises 1.034x over 4x the context while the tick rises 1.262x, and `1.262 × (2.03/2.10) = 1.220` closes the rate drop exactly. At fixed ctx=1024 the prompt change *alone* moves acceptance **2.86 → 2.03** and the tick **62.3 → 52.3 ms**, which is where the old 45.9 came from — a correct measurement of a prompt the draft finds unusually easy. This **re-prices the block-parallel reject**: p inverted from 2.03 is **0.554** (not 0.881), the arm yields **1.880 against a 2.784 break-even = 0.675x**, so the verdict's margin widens from 1.7% to 32% and leaves the noise question behind; `spec.py`'s gate deliberately keeps p=0.881, the optimistic arm, so an assertion holding there holds for any prompt. Neither prompt is the serving distribution, so **the depth default stays unsettled** — that needs real text. Open and load-bearing: tick cost is **not linear in context** (9.08 ms per 1K over 1024→2048, then 2.15 over 2048→4096, against a 0.597 ms/1K f32 KV floor), so something steps rather than scales; three points cannot say what and `prof_decode_budget.py` can. [wins/2026-09-03-long-context-decode-is-all-tick-cost.md](docs/experience/wins/2026-09-03-long-context-decode-is-all-tick-cost.md)

- **fix** — `graph_keys` read a `self._spec_depth` the Engine never assigns, so `precapture()` died with `AttributeError` on the **first drafted run** — the dense path was fine, which is why nothing caught it: `test_graph_keys_covers_what_a_decode_tick_keys_on` built only dense engines, so the `_draft is not None` branch was never executed by any gate. `self._width` is the same value and the better source (it is what the drafter settled on after `set_depth`, which may clamp, and `__init__` already range-checks it). The gate now builds a drafted engine and asserts both directions: every width a trimmed chain can present is present, and nothing past the settled width is — those captures are ~14 s each and dead. Negative control: the pre-existing off-by-one now fails it. First long-context number off the fitted pool: **38.0 tok/s at ctx=8192**, 2.74 tok/fwd, single-stream spec d3, against 45.9 at ctx=1024 — 8x the context for 1.21x. 16K+ still running. Also recorded a method error that cost ~10 calls: a negative control's one-character mutation, reverted inside the same mtime tick, kept serving **mutated bytecode** — `.pyc` invalidation keys on (mtime, size) and neither changed, while `grep` and `inspect.getsource` both read the file the interpreter was ignoring. [errors/2026-09-03-a-restored-file-is-not-a-restored-import.md](docs/experience/errors/2026-09-03-a-restored-file-is-not-a-restored-import.md)

- **default flip** — `serve --blocks` defaults to fitting the pool to the card, and the context ceiling on the 32 GB V100 goes **8192 -> 64736 tokens at B=1**, a 7.9x, with the tick untouched. The 8 GiB of non-KV residency I had flagged instrument-suspect was neither fragmentation nor the pools: loading and quantizing the 27B leaves the caching allocator holding **29.02 GiB reserved against 15.96 allocated**, and `mem_get_info` counts all 13.06 GiB of that as used — which is the number `build_engine` sizes `PrefixStore` from and any block fit reads. One `empty_cache()` after `materialize`, A/B'd one arm per process: free before sizing **2.36 -> 15.17 GiB**, KV pool **0.13 -> 8.42 GiB**, context **1024 -> 64912 tokens**. Two ordering constraints, both found by getting them wrong: the fit must run after `materialize` and the reclaim (sized one call earlier in `cli._build_engine` it asked for 10.21 GiB with 4.96 free and OOMed in `PagedKvPool`), and after the GDN state pool, which is 2.94 GiB at slots=3 depth=3 — **79% of it `step_states`, which scales `slots x width` and not `max_batch`**, so `--slots 8 --max-batch 2` was paying for six slots that can never be admitted. Allocating it first turns the estimate into a measurement. Gate `test_fitting_the_kv_pool_happens_after_the_state_pool` asserts the sequence, since the fit is card-specific and no CPU gate can check it numerically. This **demotes the f16 KV pool** (136 -> 68 KiB/token): 2x for a kernel change, against 7.9x for none. Rate across the newly reachable contexts is **not measured**. [wins/2026-09-03-allocator-reserve-was-the-context-ceiling.md](docs/experience/wins/2026-09-03-allocator-reserve-was-the-context-ceiling.md)
- **fix** — two bugs the capacity probe surfaced, both shipped broken. `spec.py` could not load the **only draft head in production**: the NVFP4 shard's readout is `lm_head.wq/scale/oscale`, `_param_key_for` names none of the three, and a skip keyed on `mapped == "lm_head"` sent all of them to `unknown` and raised — every test used an f16 head with a single `lm_head.weight` and passed. Now keyed on `_is_lm_head`, negative control that an unrelated unmapped tensor still raises. And three sites in `backend.py` read `io = bf16 if target.startswith("cuda")`: **sm70 is CUDA and has no bf16 load**, so `gdn_prep` got f16 against an f32 signature and died mid-run with "input Q dtype mismatch, expected float32", pointing at the kernel rather than the marshalling. `Backend.io` is keyed on arch and already documents the hazard; all three now read it. Gate `test_kernel_io_is_keyed_on_arch_not_on_being_cuda` parses the source, because the arch that breaks cannot run in CI.
- **fix** — the GRPO training rectangle is a **JIT cache key**, and it was sized from the rollout's own output. `grpo_loop` padded to `max(len(c) for c in comps)`, so a step whose completions ended at a new length compiled a fresh TileLang kernel set: measured on tiny at group 6, **239.2 s across 8 distinct widths against 0.6 s fixed (398x)**, and in isolation **37.7 s for a novel width against 71 ms for a repeat (530x)**. It now pads to `max(sampling.max_new_tokens, max(len(c)))`, a width the configuration fixes before the rollout starts; `seq_lens` already carried the true lengths and the advantage mask already zeroed padding, so the update is unchanged and only the compiled shape moves. The smoke recipe could not show this — tiny never emits EOS, so every completion is exactly `max_new_tokens` and both arms pick the same width; **the first A/B I ran was a null experiment** and only varying the width directly separated them. Two fixes rode along: `clip_grad_norm` went to one `torch._foreach_norm` plus one f64 reduction, **141 -> 17.5 ms** at 27B-LoRA scale (1092 f32 adapters) agreeing to **7.7e-07** relative with the one-sync property intact, widening anything narrower than f32 first because `_foreach_norm` squares in the input dtype (raw bf16 loses 1.3 decimal digits, raw f16 0.9 — my first cut named bf16 only and missed f16); and `cli.py`'s `reward_first`/`reward_last` became windowed means, since `hist[0]` vs `hist[-1]` compares two draws of a per-step reward rather than two policies. [wins/2026-09-03-grpo-step-width-is-a-jit-key.md](docs/experience/wins/2026-09-03-grpo-step-width-is-a-jit-key.md)

- **fix** — `grpo-tiny-smoke`, the only recipe that runs on CI hardware, **failed its own gates on every run**, and `tests/test_recipes.py` wrapped it in `contextlib.suppress(SystemExit)` — so the one gate reachable on CPU was switched off by the test that owned it. Two causes, both in the recipe rather than the gate: at `max_new_tokens=4` every group ties whatever the reward's shape (12/12, already measured in [tied-groups-are-the-rewards-shape](docs/experience/errors/2026-09-03-tied-groups-are-the-rewards-shape.md)), and 2 steps cannot measure `reward_rises`. At **steps 12, group 6, max_new_tokens 8, lr 0.05** — the shape `tests/test_rl.py`'s passing loop already used — it passes on **4 of 4 seeds with `tied_group_fraction` 0**; at group 2 it was 2 of 3, which is a coin flip and not a gate. The test now asserts `gates_pass(m)` and the recipe's own step count instead of a hardcoded 2, with a negative control: zeroing `group_advantages` turns it red (`SystemExit: 1`) and restoring it green. Also `tilerl train --recipe X --json` was **unparseable** — the recipe banner printed to stdout ahead of the manifest; it goes to stderr now, and stdout stays the JSON stream apart from TileLang's own C++ cache warnings, which is why the test still slices from the first `{`. And the 27B recipes died with a 20-line HuggingFace 401 traceback because `cmd_train` built the tokenizer before `_load_model`'s guarded path: one `_qwen38_tokenizer()` now carries the same hint for both, trimmed to the first line of the cause. [wins/2026-09-03-grpo-step-width-is-a-jit-key.md](docs/experience/wins/2026-09-03-grpo-step-width-is-a-jit-key.md)

- **fix** — the q/k norm casts **once**, not twice. `q_norm`/`k_norm` feed rope and then the bf16 KV pool, and the sm90 norm kernel stored bf16, so the value rounded there and again at the pool. Measured on card 6 through the shipped call path: on the **3163 of 65536** elements the change moves, mean error against a dense f64 norm+rope falls **2.049e-03 -> 1.009e-03**, ratio **2.0314**, closer on **3163 of 3163** — matching the 2.0007 that separated the discrete prelude from `attn_prep`, which is what identifies this store as the extra rounding rather than something correlated with it. `make_rmsnorm_fused` takes `out_dtype` (one launch either way, so dtype is the only variable); the two q/k sites take a new `Backend.rmsnorm_f32`. Scoped to those two: `input_norm`/`post_attn_norm` feed GEMMs whose own cast rounds harder, `final_norm` rests on this checkpoint being untied with a quantized head and is left as a **config dependency**, and `dflash2.py:236` has the same shape but no pool so there is nothing to remove. The load-bearing line is `_BWD["rmsnorm_f32"]`: an op absent from it is returned raw and records **no tape entry, silently** — 1 entry for `rmsnorm` vs 0 for an unregistered twin, with forward parity, the CPU twin and the sm90 oracle all green while q_norm/k_norm stop learning. Gate `test_rmsnorm_f32_records_a_tape_entry_and_its_gradient_is_right`. **Prefill cost unmeasured**: a kernel-level 226x is recorded as invalid (one fused launch vs `rmsnorm_partial` + a freshly JIT-built `rmsnorm_apply` conflates dtype, launch count and compile time), and the engine-level A/B is the open number. [wins/2026-09-03-qk-norm-casts-once.md](docs/experience/wins/2026-09-03-qk-norm-casts-once.md)

- **phase gate** — P1 at the pinned `lr=1e-4` runs **100/100 steps with no collapse** and the ledger still returns **FAIL**. The learning-rate fix worked: step 9, where the 1e-3 arm went flat for 92 consecutive steps, scores 0.5000 with a live gradient. What is left is a different blocker with the same observable — **86 of 100 steps have every rollout tied, and 80 of those tie at reward 1.0000**, not at 0. `train.py` runs one prompt per step, so a group is 8 attempts at one GSM8K question, almost always solved 8/8 or missed 8/8; only 14 steps produced a gradient (mean reward 0.6071). `reward_first=1, reward_last=1, ce_last=2.507, secs_per_step_median=60.45, peak_gib=38.51`. Not fixed: more prompts per step and a reward with more than two outcomes are both design changes needing their own gate. [errors/2026-09-03-p1-ties-at-the-ceiling.md](docs/experience/errors/2026-09-03-p1-ties-at-the-ceiling.md)
- **error** — the 53 MMLU answers that move with `fuse_projections` are the **unfused** arm being wrong, and it is `cli.py`'s default. `fuse_projections` is not a fusion toggle: the concat is weight-preserving bit-for-bit (5/5 groups, fp4 and fp8) and the fused vs unfused GEMMs are **bitwise identical** at M=1/8/64, but `model.py:202`'s `if self._has(qkv_key)` gates a different *branch* — `backend.attn_prep` (norm+rope+kv-write in one launch) instead of `rmsnorm`/`rope`/`write_tokens`. `attn_prep` is closer to a dense f32 norm+rope on **580 of 580** differing K-plane elements by exactly **2.0007x**, one extra bf16 rounding step: the discrete path rounds at `rmsnorm`, at `rope`, and again at `write_tokens`. The seed appears at layer 3 (the first full-attn layer; 0-2 bitwise identical) and amplifies **x561** to a 2.136 logit move, noise floor exactly 0.000. So 746/1000 with fusion is the accurate score and `cli.py`'s 742 is the degraded one. Uncaught through **two independent coverage holes**: `src/tilerl/testing.py:53` stubs `RefBackend.attn_prep` to `None` so the CPU cell always runs the discrete path and both arms of every CPU parity test share one prelude; and the extra rounding itself (`kernels.py:110`, `Y = T.empty(..., "bfloat16")`) is registered **only in `_SM90_KERNELS`** — `_CPU_KERNELS` emits f32 and does not double-round, so a CPU parity gate cannot catch this even after the twin lands. The gate has to run on sm90. **Fix decided, not landed**: cast once in the discrete path, do not flip which branch runs (`attn_prep` is sm90-only with no CPU twin, so flipping the default would leave the CPU cell double-rounding; and the discrete path is the arithmetic the tape differentiates). Wants its own tranche with a CPU twin beside it. Gate `test_attn_prep_has_no_cpu_twin_so_the_parity_gate_misses_the_fused_prelude` asserts the hole and fails when a CPU twin lands. Also **corrects the concurrency entry below**: its `M = B*W` mechanism is measured false (concurrency 8 vs 32 differ on **0 of 1000** answers; prefill `M` comes from `_PREFILL_BUCKET`, and `M = B*W` is a decode-tick mechanism MMLU never reaches). [errors/2026-09-03-unfused-prelude-double-rounds.md](docs/experience/errors/2026-09-03-unfused-prelude-double-rounds.md)

- **reject** — **block-parallel drafting (DFlash/DSpark) on sm70 is 1.016x, and the one thing the mechanism buys is negative here.** The gating number is measured, not fitted: depths 2 and 3 share rung 4, so their difference is exactly one draft forward — **5.53 ms, 25.0% of a 66.46 ms depth-3 tick**. Three ceilings, and only one is the fair one: draft cost → **0** gives 1.333x (achievable by nothing), **one** forward — what a block head actually does — gives **1.200x**, break-even **2.784 tok/forward**. Our per-position acceptance is inverted from the measurement rather than probed (`1 + p + p² + p³ = 3.34` ⇒ **p = 0.881**, reproducing 3.340). Applying DSpark's own measured decay *shape* (its `[72,57,45]` ⇒ ratios 0.79) gives **2.831 against a 2.784 break-even — a 1.7% margin against a 1.16% noise floor**, i.e. the win is the size of the instrument. **The structural gift is worth less than zero**: block-parallel makes wider blocks cheap (draft is O(1) in k), but the sm70 ladder prices verify in steps, and going k=3 → k=7 buys **+0.213 tok/forward for +18.59 ms** of rung-8 verify — 1.016x falls to **0.818x**. The optimum is k=3, the depth already shipped. A 6-agent source study confirms the mechanism independently (`fc.weight [5120, 25600] = 5 × 5120` is a per-**request** projector; **zero per-position parameters** in all 63 tensors; K/V is `cat(context, noise)` bidirectional; `dspark.rs:919-921` fills `mask_token_id` and overwrites row 0 with the anchor) and prices the port as worse still — DSpark's head is **5 layers / 1.86B** against our 1 layer / 456M, and agent-infer measured that head at **13% acceptance on Qwen3.8, cause unexplained**. Not measured and not needed: the 1.7% margin fails before the depth penalty is added. **Correction in the same pass**: `docs/roadmap.md` claimed "on sm70 the draft is in-graph" — it is not. `_run_decode_graph` captures only `model.forward` (`engine.py:231,237`); the draft runs at `engine.py:908` after `g.run()` returns, and `DraftHead.step` allocates arrays whose shapes are data-dependent per tick, which is why it cannot be captured. Neither source entry ever claimed otherwise. Gate in `spec.py`'s `__main__` (any target): k=3 optimum, k=7 below it, margin inside the noise floor — negative control **run**, pricing rung 8 at rung 4's 49.87 ms moves the optimum to k=7 at 54.9 tok/s and trips two of the three. 227 passed, 6 skipped, ruff clean, no GPU. [errors/2026-09-03-block-parallel-drafting-is-1.016x-on-sm70.md](docs/experience/errors/2026-09-03-block-parallel-drafting-is-1.016x-on-sm70.md)

- **deferred** — **`KvTier` (HBM→SSD prefix spill) is out of the branch, and the premise its design rested on is false.** Rebasing onto current `main` found nine conflict hunks in `kv_cache.py` that were two independent designs on one class: upstream reworked `PrefixStore` for GDN state snapshots plus a byte budget (`state_bytes`), which occupies the same eviction seam the tier hooks. Taking upstream's and re-landing the tier on top is cheaper than hand-merging, so the class, its `--kv-tier` flag, its 7 unit tests and its e2e cold-hit gate are deferred together rather than left half-merged. Kept from this side: `state_bytes` derived from free HBM (`mem_get_info()[0] // 4`) instead of upstream's 8 GiB default, which is most of a 32 GB V100's post-weights headroom. Separately, the tier's docstring justified having no DRAM level with "on a 32 GB V100 with a **full host**" — `free -g` on the pod reads **available 25 of 31 GB**, so the host is not full and that rationale is retired. **Not** established: that a DRAM tier is worth building. The other number that decision needs, SSD reload latency on a prefix hit, is unmeasured. [errors/2026-09-03-the-full-host-premise-is-false.md](docs/experience/errors/2026-09-03-the-full-host-premise-is-false.md)
- **error** — **the incremental SSE loop shipped with two defects that masked each other, and the gate that was supposed to catch them was unsatisfiable.** Found while rebasing, by reading the committed code rather than trusting the entry that described it. (1) `if len(live) > sent` compared a **token count** against a **character count**, so `sent` never advanced past a longer prompt's token index. (2) The U+FFFD guard cut at the **first** replacement char instead of the trailing run, so any prefix beginning with an unmappable byte decoded to `""` — and `tiny()` has random weights, so that is most prefixes. Each hid the other: with the cut returning `""`, `sent` staying 0 looked harmless. **Measured, both arms, four model seeds**: the broken loop put the entire reply in the final tail chunk on **3 of 4** (seeds 7/21/99; seed 42 was the exception), the fixed loop **0 of 4**, and `joined == expected` held in **all 8 runs** — which is why the shipped assertions could not separate them. Worse, the module fixture's engine is **seed 42**, the one seed measured where the defect does not bite, so the test would have stayed green for either version; it now builds its own engine at seed 7 with that reason written down. The "no U+FFFD in a mid-stream delta" assertion is **deleted as unsatisfiable**: the non-streamed reply carries interior replacement chars on 4 of 4 seeds, so no streaming split can avoid emitting one, and the old loop passed only by emitting nothing until the end. What replaced it is the invariant that actually belongs to the server: **no incremental delta may END on U+FFFD** (that is a character cut in half), plus **the last delta may not be the whole reply**. Negative control run: the broken loop fails the new gate with `only 1 content delta(s)`. **A gate that passes on the arm it was written to reject is not a gate** — and the sequence here was: entry written, number reported (32.4 vs 32.4 tok/s, which is real), code never re-read. 227 passed, 6 skipped, ruff clean. [errors/2026-09-03-the-sse-gate-could-not-fail.md](docs/experience/errors/2026-09-03-the-sse-gate-could-not-fail.md)
- **fix** — the captured decode tick's padding row is engine overhead and is sized for, instead of being taken from the pools the caller sized. `num_slots=N` served **N-1**: a replay's padding rows write to both pools, the engine took a slot and a block on the first tick that padded and never returned them, and the N-th `submit` came back with the `RuntimeError` from `alloc_slot`, which has no fallback. Lazy, so a run served N until the first tick that happened to pad and N-1 after. `build_engine` now allocates `num_slots + 1` / `num_blocks + 1` when the captured tick is on, `Engine` reserves the row up front, and `_run_decode_graph` checks the reservation rather than allocating. `decode_graph=None` meaning "on for CUDA" now has one definition (`_graph_on`) that the sizing and the reservation share. `submit`'s KV guard compared against the gross pool in the first cut, so the one request sized to the whole pool got past it and died in the allocator with `RuntimeError: insufficient KV blocks` instead of the `ValueError` naming the cause — usable capacity now has one definition (`usable_blocks`/`usable_slots`) that the guard and `stats()` share. Cost on the 27B, computed from the config: **156.2 MiB** (144.0 state slot + 11.2 conv window + 1.0 KV block), 0.395% of the 38.62 GiB serving peak, for one concurrent request back. Gates `test_the_graphs_padding_row_is_not_taken_from_the_callers_capacity` and `test_the_kv_guard_measures_usable_capacity_not_the_pool` run on any target, with the graph-off arm as the negative control; both mutations were run red then green, not asserted. [wins/2026-09-03-graph-pad-row-is-engine-overhead.md](docs/experience/wins/2026-09-03-graph-pad-row-is-engine-overhead.md)
- **accept** — batching the DFlash2 drafter is worth building: the ceiling with the drafter's cost set to zero is **3.64x**, and **3.64x is `6.20 / 1.70`** — the measured forward reduction over the measured tick cost, not a third figure beside them. What the probe adds is that the schedule is measured rather than inferred: 1719 verified row-ticks all at W=8, 10651 committed tokens, the trace reconciled against the engine's own `spec_accepted` (8995 = 8995), and **no draft head in the timed process** (asserted). A py-spy extrapolation of the same configuration gave ~3.4x, agreeing to 7% but resting on the same tick ratio, so it is a second route to the tick's composition and not to the ceiling. **It bounds the prize, not the profit**: a batched drafter is not free, and 74.75% acceptance was measured with the per-row drafter that batching would restructure. Separately, the W=8-vs-W=1 tick cost is **1.70–1.81x across three runs** (two cards, two harnesses, a moved `main`), so the recorded 1.79x is a spread and not a constant — the ceiling is ~3.5x either way, but nothing should fit a constant to it. [wins/2026-09-03-drafter-batching-ceiling.md](docs/experience/wins/2026-09-03-drafter-batching-ceiling.md)
- **error** — an MMLU score depends on `concurrency` and nothing recorded which one was used: the 746/1000 and 742/1000 on clean main are the **same 1000 questions** (`seed` pins the draw; this is not the moved-slice bug from this morning) scored at concurrency 32 and 8. Concurrency sets B, B sets `M = B*W`, and `M` picks the fp4 linear arm across `_MGEMV=3`/`_MX=8` — so two concurrencies run two kernels with two reduction orders on the same question, and the width ladder measures that as a **median 0.153** top-1 logit move against **exactly 0.000** when the arm is held. 4 flips in 1000 four-way questions is that effect's size. `max_new_tokens=1` is no protection: the prefill runs `M`-dependent arms too. `mmlu_accuracy` now returns `(correct, total, concurrency)`, `cli.py` records and prints it, and `scripts/mmlu.py` pins 8 so the two callers stop disagreeing. **Mechanism corrected later the same day**: concurrency moves **0 of 1000** answers, so there are no 4 flips and `M = B*W` is a decode-tick mechanism MMLU never reaches; the real variable was `fuse_projections`, and 74.6% is the accurate arm, not one number measured two ways. Gate `test_mmlu_score_reports_the_concurrency_it_used`, red when `cli.py` unpacks two values. [errors/2026-09-03-mmlu-score-depends-on-concurrency.md](docs/experience/errors/2026-09-03-mmlu-score-depends-on-concurrency.md)
- **audit** — `verify_lens` is not inert and was one commit from being deleted as such. On the 27B with a real confidence head at depth 7 it trims **235 of 1334** rows (0 of 157 at offered 1-5, 8 of 58 at offered 6, **227 of 1119** at offered 7). The 08-30 entry's "always returns its maximum" was measured at depths 1, 2 and 4 — exactly the region where it cannot bind. Worse than an untuned constant: `BIAS + ROW_MS·n` is affine and monotone while the measured cost is a staircase (+9.52 ms at row 3, **-0.11 ms** at row 8, because `linear_*_mma8` pads M to 8), so no scalar `ROW_MS` fits and **the policy can prefer a shorter chain that costs the same or more** — every trim inside the padded region gives up a draft and saves nothing. Not changed: carrying the staircase or restricting trims to arm boundaries are both design changes needing goodput. Safe on the shipped path only by accident — `--depth` defaults to 2, inside the never-trims region. [errors/2026-09-03-verify-lens-binds-where-nobody-measured-it.md](docs/experience/errors/2026-09-03-verify-lens-binds-where-nobody-measured-it.md)
- **default flip** — `grpo-gsm8k-27b` pins `lr=1e-4`; it inherited the CLI's **1e-3**, which destroys the policy by step 9. The 100-step P1 run scored 0.75-1.00 for seven steps, then **exactly 0.0000 for all 92 remaining steps**, `tied_group_fraction=0.97`. Not truncation (that starves the reward from step 1, and this one scores 1.0 for seven) and not prompt noise: `prompts[step % n]` and seeds `seed + step*group + g` are both step-indexed and identical across arms, so at a fixed step the weights are the only difference. Step-aligned, from step 9 the 1e-3 arm is flat zero for twelve consecutive steps while 1e-4/1e-5/1e-6 score; at **step 13** — same question, same noise — 1e-4 and 1e-6 both take 1.0000 and 1e-3 takes 0.0000. Reproduced the same day on another card: identical shape, and from step 9 **every group is tied on every step** — zero advantage, so no gradient, so the policy is frozen at the broken state rather than learning slowly — the loop `zero reward -> tied group -> zero advantage -> no gradient -> zero reward` is self-sustaining, which is why 92 of 100 steps are exactly 0.0 rather than noisy-small. Tying itself is expected on GSM8K ([tied-groups-are-the-rewards-shape](docs/experience/errors/2026-09-03-tied-groups-are-the-rewards-shape.md)); what separates the two causes is the **value** the group is tied at, and the log already carries it — one group per step, so `reward 1.0000 tied 1.00` is the reward's shape and `reward 0.0000 tied 1.00` twelve times over is collapse. The earlier sweep could not find this because it compared 1e-4/1e-5/1e-6, which are one cluster, and omitted the value that ships. 1e-4 is inside the surviving cluster, **not established as the best in it**; the CLI default is untouched, since SFT and pretrain have no evidence here. [errors/2026-09-03-grpo-default-lr-flattens-the-reward.md](docs/experience/errors/2026-09-03-grpo-default-lr-flattens-the-reward.md)
- **superseded instruction** — a standing session instruction asks for *"tilerl-48 的 option C"* to be merged. **Option C is the rejected change two lines below** — recomputing the truncation mask at training time — and it is not in the tree: `git grep -E 'kept_set|keep_mask|mask_transport'` over `src/` and `tests/` returns nothing, and the reject line records why (61.0–98.3% of steps lost at the recipe's `max_new_tokens=256`, because a dropped token gives `log q = -inf` ⇒ `inf` loss ⇒ `_step` returns early). The instruction predates the measurement and re-fires verbatim on every loop tick, so it names a change the evidence withdrew. Anyone acting on it literally should read the reject line first; what replaced option C is untruncated rollouts (the default flip below), already shipped. [errors/2026-09-03-recomputed-mask-loses-the-step.md](docs/experience/errors/2026-09-03-recomputed-mask-loses-the-step.md)
- **reject** — DFlash2 reaches the engine tick and is **not** enabled: 27B, H20, B=8, graph on, 200 GSM8K, the block drafter accepts **6.18 of 8** and cuts trunk forwards 5.5x (42.99 vs 7.80 tokens per decode forward), and the wall clock is **1.67x worse** (466.5s vs 278.6s, 139.4 vs 232.3 tok/s). A spec tick costs ~9.2x a base tick because `_draft_block` walks the rows one at a time in Python outside the decode graph — `py-spy` puts the drafter at **68.4%** of the arm (`block_hidden` 52.9%), the trunk's own graph replay at 13.2%. Accuracy 167/200 vs 170/200, three questions against a 2.5-point binomial sd, which supports nothing either way. The largest single self-time frame was the benchmark's own NaN tracer at **13.8%**, a GPU sync per row per tick charged to the spec arm only — the instrument was bigger than the trunk it was measuring, and is fixed. Engine-side, one drafter contract replaces `isinstance(draft, DFlash2Head)`: `engine.py` −171 lines, and two checks that the isinstance had narrowed to block drafters (the verify-tile width bound, the prefix-cache refusal) now key on `draft.width` and `draft.aux_layers` and cover both heads. Batching the drafter is the open work; the share above prices it at ~3.4x faster than base, estimated, not measured. [wins/2026-09-03-dflash2-on-the-engine-tick.md](docs/experience/wins/2026-09-03-dflash2-on-the-engine-tick.md)
- **accept** — `read_head_params` raises on a checkpoint tensor its map does not name, instead of dropping it. The classification had three cases and two visible branches: loaded, warned-and-skipped, and a fall-through with no `else`. A real 5-layer DFlash2 checkpoint read through the NextN map loses **23 of 81** tensors this way (all its conv and selector weights); read through its own map it is 81/81, so the raise cannot fire on a correct path. It is not the all-NaN verify source — every one of `DFlash2Head`'s 19 weight reads is a direct subscript with no `.get()`, so a dropped tensor is a `KeyError` on the first block, not garbage. Had one been tolerant, every acceptance number on record would have been suspect. Gate `test_unmapped_tensors_raise_instead_of_vanishing`, with the correct reader on the same file as its in-body negative control. [errors/2026-09-03-draft-loader-drops-tensors-silently.md](docs/experience/errors/2026-09-03-draft-loader-drops-tensors-silently.md)
- **accept** — the split-KV combine's all-empty row is guarded rather than left unreachable-by-luck: `exp2(-inf − -inf)` and `0/0` both sit behind `T.if_then_else`, and the invariant that makes the row unreachable (split 0 always spans tile 0, which every chain row may attend) is now written in the docstring instead of nowhere. It cannot be a host assert — `seq_lens` is a device tensor and reading it would break the graph-capturable path. Verified on sm90 card 7 with the arm that matters: the same all-empty partials give **0 NaN of 256** guarded and **256 of 256** through the pre-guard arithmetic, so a clean live run — which cannot reach the row — is not the evidence. Live geometries unchanged, 1.7e-03–2.9e-03 against the dense kernel at W=1/2/4/8 × n=64/100/4096. [wins/2026-09-03-combine-split0-invariant.md](docs/experience/wins/2026-09-03-combine-split0-invariant.md)
- **reject** — that the 100-step GRPO collapse is explained by the default `lr=1e-3`: not confirmed, and the sweep run to test it could not have confirmed it. 20 steps each at `1e-6`/`1e-5`/`1e-4` all keep nonzero reward through step 20, and the three arms agree column-by-column — step 6 is 0.00 in all three, step 7 is 1.00 in all three — because `grpo_loop` draws `prompts[step % len(prompts)]` and **question difficulty dominates the per-step reward**. Three curves against a metric that moves with the input cannot separate three policies. The 92 consecutive zeros at `lr=1e-3` stay unexplained. A held-out eval scored every N steps is the instrument this needed. [errors/2026-09-03-per-step-reward-cannot-compare-two-policies.md](docs/experience/errors/2026-09-03-per-step-reward-cannot-compare-two-policies.md)
- **retraction** — the DFlash2 line below was merged as a **phase exit** for a component with no path through the product, and its 5.80-of-8 acceptance has been quoted next to the width-W tick's 2.4x cost as though the two composed. They do not: the acceptance is DFlash2's, measured by `scripts/probe_dflash2_acceptance.py`, and every engine-measured speculation number on record (48.5% acceptance, 22.78 tokens per decode forward, 70 of 70 wide ticks) is the NextN `model_mtp.safetensors` head. Two different components. Separately, **W=8 is not a shipped width**: `--depth` defaults to 2 (W=3), `build_engine`'s own default gives W=5, and `_MAX_VERIFY_W = 8` is the kernel ceiling reachable only with `--depth 7`. A capability is recorded as exited when the product runs it, not when a script does. [wins/2026-09-03-dflash2-block-drafter.md](docs/experience/wins/2026-09-03-dflash2-block-drafter.md)
- **error** — `decode_graph=True` permanently consumes one state slot and one KV block: the first decode tick that does not fill a graph bucket allocates `_pad_slot`/`_pad_block` (`engine.py:678`) and never frees them, so an engine with `num_slots=N` serves `N-1` and the N-th `submit` raises `LinearStatePool exhausted` instead of queueing. `serve` has slack (16 slots against `max_batch` 8) and the training paths keep the graph off for unrelated reasons, so it has gone unobserved; a harness at `num_slots == max_batch` hits it on the second arm. Found, not fixed. Also recorded: `TILERL_GDN_CHUNKWISE` is a **chunk length, not a switch** — `=1` reads as "chunkwise on" and is a serial scan; `=64` is the setting anyone testing it means. [errors/2026-09-03-decode-graph-leaks-a-slot-and-a-block.md](docs/experience/errors/2026-09-03-decode-graph-leaks-a-slot-and-a-block.md)
- **error** — the 27B GRPO recipe was called stalled three times and was never stalled: `py-spy dump --locals` read `step: 89` of 100 off the live frame, with `secs` of 70.0 and 73.9 already in the history — **~72 s per step, ~2 h for the run**. Nothing printed because `grpo_loop` returns the whole history and `cli.py:222` logs it only after the last step. The 60 s profile behind the same entry stands and is unaffected: rollout 61% / backward 34%, `torch.nn.functional.pad` the largest single self cost at 14.8% (`_pad2d` short-circuits, so that is real allocate-and-copy per linear per layer), tilelang jit argument binding 29.6%, the torch-eager cells 28.4%. [errors/2026-09-03-grpo-step-is-python-dispatch-bound.md](docs/experience/errors/2026-09-03-grpo-step-is-python-dispatch-bound.md)
- **error** — the MMLU eval slice moved under the number: `81e9789` replaced `shuffle(idx); idx[:n]` with `sample(range(len(ds)), n)`, a different draw from the same seed, so the recorded 763/1000 = 76.3% and today's 746/1000 = 74.6% (same runner, clean main) share **2 of 1000 questions**. Not a regression: the 08-28 run's saved `idx` re-read against today's cache reproduces its gold letters exactly. README and roadmap now carry 74.6% with the draw named. Record the slice with the score. [errors/2026-09-03-the-mmlu-slice-moved-under-the-number.md](docs/experience/errors/2026-09-03-the-mmlu-slice-moved-under-the-number.md)
- **verdict** — W=8 speculation on `e26af2d` does not reproduce the unspeculated greedy string, and that is arithmetic, not a defect: 4 of 8 GSM8K completions differ, and at every one of them the committed token **is** the verify tile's own argmax (0 of 1766 sampled entries say otherwise). The two arms differ because `Backend._plan` sends M=1 down the GEMV path and M=8 down an MMA tile — every projection in every layer takes a different reduction order — so top-1 logits land 0.153 apart at the median (n=920, p90 0.411), about 2 bf16 ulps at magnitude 24. Only 5 of those 920 positions have a top-2 gap below that; four are the four divergences (gaps 1.24e-2 … 7.43e-2). `_verify`'s "bit-identical to the unspeculated one" was never true off the CPU reference; the guarantee is that a committed token is this tick's own draw from the trunk, and `test_verify_commits_the_trunks_own_draw` gates that plus the step-plane pairing, each with a negative control. Standing lead closed with it: `alloc_slot` leaves `step_states`/`step_windows` unzeroed, but no read is stale — 0 violations pairing every write against every read across slot reuse, ragged widths and trimmed chains, and NaN-poisoning both planes changes nothing. Throughput reproduces: 22.78 tokens per decode forward against the base arm's 6.77. [errors/2026-09-03-w8-greedy-is-not-the-unspeculated-greedy.md](docs/experience/errors/2026-09-03-w8-greedy-is-not-the-unspeculated-greedy.md)
- **verdict** — A is not a waypoint to mask transport; it is the better estimator, and the earlier "waypoint / destination" framing in this file and `rl-sota-parity.md` §2 was wrong. Scored on the metric that grants the mask-transport case entirely — train each arm, then generate with the **deployed** sampler `T=0.7/p=0.8/k=20` — untruncated rollouts beat both the status quo and gradient-under-q. Two runs: A +0.456 / status +0.356 (this session, A regressed on 1 of 5 seeds) and A +0.556 / status +0.236 / grad-under-q +0.290 (independent, 0 of 5 regressing). **The ordering reproduced; the multipliers did not** — 1.28x vs 2.36x on the same comparison, so no multiplier is quoted. The structural half needs no seed: at a 1-token nucleus `q*A − E_q[A]*q ≡ 0`, so gradient-under-q is the exact zero vector at 8 of 12 tiny positions, and a good token just outside the nucleus (π=0.024, q=0) is frozen there permanently while the true gradient pushes it up. Mask transport stays on the roadmap for what it actually buys — making the card's sampler usable in RL — not for reward. `pending-remote`: tiny cfg, 12 steps, 5 seeds — a direction, not a size, so a disagreeing 27B number supersedes the reward rows and leaves the structural result standing. [docs/rl-sota-parity.md §2](docs/rl-sota-parity.md)
- **audit** — W>1 speculation is not token-identical on the 27B, and the split-KV NaN guard is confirmed. On `main` `e26af2d`, GSM8K greedy at W=8 scores 4/8 like the unspeculated arm and **disagrees with it on 4 of 8 completions** — coherent alternative wordings, not corruption, with W=8 fully engaged (70 of 70 wide ticks at width 8, 48.5% acceptance, 22.78 tokens per decode forward against 6.77). `Engine._verify` claims an accepted token is bit-identical to the unspeculated one; at n=8 that is not what it does, and whether the flips are near-tie float nondeterminism or a logic error is not separated here. The negative control: the same script on `perf/spec-graph`, one commit below the guard, gives 0/500 and 500/500 differing with NaN in 372 of 560 verify row-ticks; on `main` it is **0 NaN of 393 wide row-ticks with 39 at `n % 64` in `[1,7]`**, the geometry the guard targets. Standing lead: `alloc_slot` zeroes `states` and `conv_windows` but not `step_states`/`step_windows`, which `select_step` reads back across reused slots. MMLU 0-shot cannot gate any of this — at `max_new_tokens=1` both arms report `decode_forwards 0` and `spec_drafted 0`, so its 742/1000 agreement is a tautology. [errors/2026-09-03-w8-verify-tick-is-not-lossless-on-the-27b.md](docs/experience/errors/2026-09-03-w8-verify-tick-is-not-lossless-on-the-27b.md)
- **error** — four tests were red under `TILERL_TARGET=cuda` for four unrelated reasons and CI could not see any of them (`.github/workflows/ci.yml` is cpu-only on ubuntu and macos): `state_scatter` cast the payload's dtype and not its device; `test_opd_ema_self_teacher_shares_the_model` attached the adapters before `build_engine`, which is what materializes; `test_tape_gradcheck`'s 1e-3 step is 0.1 bf16 ulps because `Backend._rows` casts to bf16 on cuda; and `test_seedless_requests_decorrelate` asserted six completions differ when what it guards is that six seeds differ. Fixing the second exposed that serving mutates the frozen fp4 base — `_served_fp4` twiddles `.wq` in place on first use, 4075 of 4096 bytes after one 4-token generation, by design and undone by `save_hf`. Two fused-projection tests stay red on clean main and are out of scope. [errors/2026-09-03-three-cuda-training-tests-red-on-main.md](docs/experience/errors/2026-09-03-three-cuda-training-tests-red-on-main.md)
- **default flip** — GRPO rollouts draw untruncated (`temperature=1.0, top_p=1.0, top_k=0`), forced in `grpo_loop` rather than left to the caller. `rl_step` takes the gradient under the full softmax, so a rollout drawn at the card's `T=0.7, top_p=0.8, top_k=20` came from one distribution and was differentiated as another, with nothing reweighting them — every GRPO step before this was biased. Serving keeps the card's values; this is RL rollouts only. Flagged for ckl's verdict as a training-semantics change. [errors/2026-09-03-recomputed-mask-loses-the-step.md](docs/experience/errors/2026-09-03-recomputed-mask-loses-the-step.md)
- **reject** — importance weighting (AReaL's decoupled ratio) cannot correct a truncated sampler, measured rather than argued: 97.2% of π's mass sits outside q's support per position, ESS over a group of 8 falls to 1.74 at 256 tokens, and TRL's `clip_max=3.0` and AReaL's `upper=5.0` fire at 0.0% because every weight sits far *below* 1 (mean sequence log-ratio −921). The guardrail is on the wrong side of a collapsing weight. [docs/rl-sota-parity.md §2](docs/rl-sota-parity.md)
- **reject** — recomputing the truncation mask at training time instead of transporting it: the paged-KV rollout forward and the dense training forward disagree on the `top_p` nucleus at 0.046%–0.20% of positions across three probes (one to three events each; `top_k` misses at 0/1920 — rank order is robust), and a dropped token makes `log q = -inf`, so `_step` skips the whole update. - **default flip** — sm90 prefill takes the chunkwise-WY path; `TILERL_GDN_WY` is deleted. The layer around the core is now two kernels, `gdn_prep` (conv1d + SiLU, q/k L2-norm, log gate, beta, next conv window) and `gdn_post` (gated RMSNorm times SiLU(z)), split out of `gdn_chunk_fused` rather than rewritten: torch glue per GDN layer 48 → 2 aten dispatches. 27B prefill on a quiet H20 with the shipped-serial arm run from clean `origin/main` in the same window, twice, bracketing the WY arm to 0.5%: GPU-busy 233.8 → 176.4 ms, kernels 3827 → 3925, harness 2238.1 → 2887.6 / 2215.4 → 2852.5 / 2135.3 → 2729.0 tok/s at 512 / 2048 / 8192 — **1.29 / 1.28 / 1.27x against a 1.15x gate**, where the same core behind torch glue managed 1.036x because its 6755 kernels were 1.77x the shipped count. Predicted 3875, measured 3925 (+1.3%, one launch a layer unaccounted). Beats fla's chunked route too (193.4 ms, 6899 kernels). The 8192 row's 27-50% sampled spread was first blamed on a neighbouring tenant and is not that: eight consecutive runs read 0.16%, the steadiest series in the set, and the harness's median-of-three is reproducible to 0.14% across sessions. CPU cells first, sm90 prep cell overrides; parity out 4.0e-3 sm90 / 2.1e-6 cpu / 7.5e-7 metal against a 1e-2 gate, the sm90 row now measured at the same input amplitude as the CPU one and decomposed per `gdn_prep` output — every bf16 output within 1.5x of bf16's 1.95e-3 rounding unit, the f32 gate 8.3e-8, the window bit-exact. Block-forward parity vs a torch-eager transcription of vLLM's `qwen3_dflash2.py` 1.19e-07. **No engine path reaches it, and the width-W verify tick did not change that.** The engine calls `draft.forward()` and `draft.confidence()`; `DFlash2Head` has neither and exposes `draft()`, whose only callers are `tests/test_dflash2.py` and `scripts/probe_dflash2_acceptance.py`. The missing piece was never the verify tick — it is that `_draft_step` is autoregressive and this head emits a block. So 5.80 of 8 is a probe-script number on a path the product cannot take, and it must not be read next to the width-W tick's cost as if the two composed. This line was merged labelled **phase exit**; that was wrong, because the phase is not exited until the product can run it. Gate `tests/test_dflash2.py`. - **accept** — the prefix store owns the recurrent-state snapshot and evicts it by a byte budget (default 8 GiB) alongside entry count and block pressure; `NoPrefixStore` retains nothing. One snapshot is 149.6 MiB on the 27B (f32 state on sm90, not the 74.81 MiB bf16 figure the old comment carried), so the 4096-entry cap alone permitted 598 GiB. 27B on H20, 200 cached prefixes: 29.22 -> 7.89 GiB of snapshots served, 29.22 -> 0.00 GiB in training; peak 55.36 -> 34.17 GiB. On main a rollout-shaped arm OOMs inside `_publish_prefix` at the 144 MiB state clone and a dropped engine never releases its snapshots (the `on_evict` lambda closes over the engine); both are gone. Gates `test_prefix_state_budget_evicts`, `test_noprefix_store_retains_no_snapshot`. [wins/2026-09-03-prefix-snapshot-byte-budget.md](docs/experience/wins/2026-09-03-prefix-snapshot-byte-budget.md)
- **reject** — **silu_mul's strided copy is 0.057% of a single-stream tick, and the note that opened this was wrong by 952x.** The standing item said the fused `gate_up` layout costs "476 MiB of copies per MLP layer". Measured on the real 27B shape (`I = 17408`, f32): **that figure holds only at 3584 rows** — the 7x512 prefill — and the decode path runs **4 rows** (B=1 depth 3, `LADDER_WIDTHS` at `spec.py:47`), where the copy is **0.5 MiB per layer, 34 MiB per tick, 0.037 ms at 900 GB/s, 0.057% of the measured 65 ms tick**. The harness noise floor is 1.16%, so the win is **20x below what can be seen**. Second correction: **at one row there is no copy at all** — a `[1, 2I]` tensor's halves are each contiguous, so `_c()` is a no-op and dense B=1 decode never pays it. **A byte count without its shape is not a measurement**: "476 MiB per MLP layer" reads as a property of the layer and is a property of one row count that only appears in prefill, and it would have sent me to rewrite the GEMV's output contract — a real slicing-bug risk at `M` not dividing 32, in a branch with no CPU twin — for 0.037 ms. Method note: my first denominator was a roofline estimate of MLP weight traffic (8160 MiB/tick), giving 0.42%; that implies a **9.1 ms tick against a measured 62-70**, a 7x contradiction, so I did not report the share built on it and used the measured tick instead. **When a share disagrees with a measured end-to-end number, the denominator is the suspect.** The fix itself stays cheap and correct — write `[2, M, I]` so each half is its own contiguous plane, weight permutation at load time, zero runtime cost, MLP-local — and stays open as a **capacity** lever for wide ticks only: at 3584 rows its 476 MiB is one of the three allocations recorded OOMing at exactly that padded shape. 203 passed, 4 skipped, ruff clean, no GPU. [wins/2026-09-03-silu-copy-is-0.057-percent-at-b1.md](docs/experience/wins/2026-09-03-silu-copy-is-0.057-percent-at-b1.md)

- **verdict** — **upstream's "W=8 answers 0 of 500 GSM8K" does NOT apply to this branch, and checking that found a different latent bug in my own combine.** `origin/main` has moved 70 commits ahead carrying `e947ce2`, and since every speed number here is acceptance-driven (45.9 tok/s at ctx=1024 is `tok/forward` 2.86), a correctness verdict against wide speculation could reprice the whole line — so it was read before rebasing. **It does not apply**: the follow-up `dbf755c` records that the 0/500 run was measured one commit *below* the split-KV NaN guard and re-runs clean on main (0 NaN of 393 wide row-ticks, 39 at the `n % 64 in [1,7]` geometry the guard targets), i.e. the catastrophic reading was that branch's own negative control. What survives upstream is narrower and still open: at W=8 an accepted token is not bit-identical to the unspeculated one, cause not separated from near-tie float nondeterminism. **The same latent row exists here with a different signature.** `paged_attention_split_combine` ends in `o[d] / l[0]`; upstream's `m` init is `-inf`, so an all-empty row gave `exp(-inf − -inf)` → NaN, while **mine is a finite `-1.0e30`**, so `w = exp(0) = 1`, `l = 1·0 = 0`, and the row divides by **exactly 0** → inf. Same class of silent wrongness, different arithmetic — **which is why matching on the symptom missed it: I went looking for NaN, found none, and would have stopped.** The row is unreachable, and now says so: `n >= 1` for every row the dispatch can produce ⇒ `per = ceildiv(n, KVSPLIT) >= 1` ⇒ split 0 gets `p1 = min(n, per) >= 1` ⇒ it runs a tile holding key 0, which every query may attend. Verified for every `n` in [1, 4200) at both shipped split counts, padded query rows included. **One premise was not guaranteed by the kernel and is now asserted**: `backend.py:690` fills an absent `seq_q_lens` with the padded width `s`, so a caller passing `seq_len < s` would make `n = 0` at `tt = 0` — the constraint lives in the dispatch, not the kernel. The tightest real producer is the draft chain step (`engine.py:987`), which has margin at `n >= 2`. **The direct check cannot run here**: recomputing `l` from the kernel's own `PM`/`PL` needs the split kernel, which has no CPU twin (`tl.reduce` unimplemented for `target="c"`, a recorded gap) — that assertion is staged in `check_split_attn_parity.py` for the next card run, and the gate that runs everywhere is the arithmetic one, with a negative control showing the same math asked about split **1** returns 0 tiles at `n=1`. **A conclusion from another branch has to be re-derived before it is imported or dismissed** — and the dismissal was only safe because the upstream entry said which tree each arm ran on. 203 passed, 4 skipped, ruff clean, no GPU. [errors/2026-09-03-combine-divides-by-zero-on-an-unreachable-row.md](docs/experience/errors/2026-09-03-combine-divides-by-zero-on-an-unreachable-row.md)

- **accept** — **the page's JS has a gate now, and `node --check` is not it.** Closing the hole the `addThinking` bug went through: 197 Python tests could not see a call to an undefined function because `_CHAT_UI` is a JS string Python only measures the length of. **The obvious fix does not work, and I measured that before writing it**: `node --check` reports **SYNTAX_OK** on `function a(){ return undefinedFn(1); }`, because an undefined call is a runtime error — a `node --check` step would have gone green on the exact commit that shipped broken, while adding a runtime dependency to a gate that must run everywhere. What ships is pure Python, no JS runtime, so CI always executes it: blank out string literals and comments, collect identifiers called **without a leading dot**, subtract `function` names, `const`/`let`/`var` bindings, arrow params, **declared function parameters** and the browser globals, fail on the rest. **Two of my own errors died against the negative control, not against the unit test.** The parameter rule was missing, so it flagged `onFrame` (a parameter of `readSSE`) — and a gate that cries wolf on a healthy page gets deleted. Then, run against `HEAD~1`, it reported `['addThinking', 'files', 'tilerl']`: the last two are **UI copy** — `"read/write files  (Enter to send)"` — where an English parenthetical reads as a call, so **any future label of the form `word (note)` would have tripped it**. With literals stripped, `HEAD~1` reports **`['addThinking']` exactly**. **Reach for the check that runs everywhere over the check that catches more** — `node --check` felt like the professional answer and is strictly weaker than 40 lines of regex for this defect. And: **a negative control on synthetic input proves the pattern, not the gate**; both implementation errors passed the synthetic case and failed against the real broken commit, which is why `scripts/probe_ui_gate_negative_control.py` runs the shipped gate against `git show HEAD~1:src/tilerl/server.py` rather than a string I wrote. Scope is honest and marked: undefined calls only — a typo'd property, a wrong argument order, a DOM id that never matches are all still invisible, and stub-DOM execution is the next step when the page grows. 199 passed, 4 skipped, ruff clean, no GPU. [wins/2026-09-03-a-gate-for-the-page-js.md](docs/experience/wins/2026-09-03-a-gate-for-the-page-js.md)

- **fix** — **the reasoning path was dead on arrival: `addThinking` was called and never defined, so every reply died mid-stream.** The `<think>` splitting shipped one commit earlier calls `addThinking(bubble)` and **that function does not exist**. The checkpoint's template defaults thinking ON, so the branch is entered at the first delta of **every** reply, and the turn dies with a `ReferenceError` and a blinking cursor. Found by the subagent redesigning the page, not by me and not by any test. **Why nothing caught it**: the entire gate is Python. `tests/test_server.py` asserts on the SSE bytes — delta count, joined text, no U+FFFD mid-stream, the usage contract — and all of it passes, because the *server* is correct; the defect is in a 21 KB JavaScript string that Python only ever measures the length of.
