[wins/2026-09-02-f16-block-scales.md](docs/experience/wins/2026-09-02-f16-block-scales.md), [errors/2026-09-02-clamped-relerr-scales-with-n.md](docs/experience/errors/2026-09-02-clamped-relerr-scales-with-n.md)
- **reject** — top-k path search and the RadixArk DSpark head: the drafted token ranks top-1 96.8% / top-16 97.1% inside the trunk's own ordering over 592 verified positions, so 0.3 points is the whole ceiling for any path search. It also dies on this trunk three ways — 48 of 64 layers are gated-delta and a recurrent scan has no attention matrix to mask (72 MiB of state per tree node against DDTree's 256-node budgets), our drafter is autoregressive so the free path space does not exist, and rung 8 is a hard ceiling (M>8 falls to 127 us/row vs 22, so width 9 costs 14.5x width 8). DSpark (3.7 GB, 5 layers, confidence head) is a different architecture, not a checkpoint swap: dual-source KV injection, 5 mid-network taps into a graph-captured static buffer, non-causal per-row visibility windows our hard-causal sm70 kernel cannot express — and its selling point is draft accuracy, which 96.8% says is not the bottleneck. Fixed on the way: `_pad2d` CROPPED on a negative pad, so a [5120, 12800] weight silently became [5120, 5120]; it now raises. [errors/2026-09-02-topk-path-search-needs-a-non-recurrent-trunk.md](docs/experience/errors/2026-09-02-topk-path-search-needs-a-non-recurrent-trunk.md)
- **phase exit** — the KV pool dtype is the kernel ABI: dense sm70 decode 30.0 → 35.3 tok/s at 4096 ctx (~18% at every context), elementwise 7.69 → 2.75 ms/token, spec d3 peaking at 50.8 tok/s at 1024 ctx and 40.3 at 4096 — 2.41x the 16.7 it read that morning. A per-kernel profile found 4.71 ms/token (14%) in 32 elementwise calls, 2 per full-attn layer: the pool was bf16 and the sm70 attention kernel is f32, so every layer of every token cast the WHOLE plane (all 1024 blocks, not the live ones — hence flat in context); allocating at `backend.io` removes it for +1 GB device memory, the fix `LinearStatePool` already had. Roofline corrected twice: 20.35 GB is the checkpoint, but a decode token streams 16.04 GB (trunk 15.24 + lm_head 0.80 — `embed_tokens` 2.54 and the visual tower 0.92 are resident, not streamed), so the bound is 56.1 tok/s and dense 35.3 is 63% of it (`errors/2026-09-02-roofline-is-the-streamed-subset.md`). Also `_draft_step` was reading hidden it did not have — a chunked prefill overwrites `r.hidden` per chunk while `draft_pos` stays behind, so a 1024 prompt at 512/chunk asked for 1535 positions, held 511, and `F.pad` took the truncation silently; every speculative number before this fix used misaligned hidden, and tok/fwd is unchanged by it. [wins/2026-09-02-kv-pool-dtype-is-the-kernel-abi.md](docs/experience/wins/2026-09-02-kv-pool-dtype-is-the-kernel-abi.md)

## 2026-09-01

- **phase exit** — sm70 long-context decode fixed, 4096 ctx 17.4 → 30.0 tok/s (1.72x), slope 6.20 → 0.59 ms/1K (10.5x), decay from 32 to 4096 ctx 44% → 7%: decode fit `ms/tok = 31.9 + 6.20*(ctx/1K)` against a 0.15 ms/1K roofline, 42x off (this line first read 0.07 ms/1K and 83x, from a head_dim of 128; the checkpoint's `text_config.head_dim` is 256, so sm70 KV is 128 KiB/token, not 64 — `backend.py:353` allocates the pool f32 for sm70), and the slope was thread redundancy — `paged_attention_split` computed its dot as `for d in T.serial(D)`, never distributed, so all 64 threads in a block ran the same dependent chain. Diagnostic signature is a thread sweep where cost RISES with thread count (4K ctx: 32t 780us → 256t 4066us). Fix stages `block_N=16` positions into fragments and reduces with `T.reduce_sum`, KVSPLIT 16 → 32: kernel 6.3x faster at 4096 and flat from 512 to 4096 (157 → 163 us), parity relerr ≤ 5.3e-05 at S=1 and S=4. Speculation gains more than dense: d3 at 4096 ctx 16.7 → 37.0 tok/s (2.22x), 512 37.7 → 44.8, 1024 34.5 → 46.5, 2048 24.4 → 40.8, peak 46.5 at 1024 — it had been a net LOSS at long context because a verify forward multiplies the serial attention cost by the chain width, and tok/fwd held at 2.9-3.3 throughout, so acceptance never degraded. The split-KV rewrite that introduced this shipped on end-to-end tok/s with no per-kernel timing and the defect survived three later measurements. [wins/2026-09-01-sm70-attention-thread-redundancy.md](docs/experience/wins/2026-09-01-sm70-attention-thread-redundancy.md)
- **default flip** — sm70 spec depth 2 → 3; speculation wins on code, breaks even on chat: against a dense baseline (`scripts/bench_workloads.py`) coding 32.6 → 43.4 tok/s (1.33x), dialogue 31.9 → 32.0, thinking 32.0 → 30.2, counting 32.7 → 52.7 (kept only as a control). Depth is a staircase, not a line — verify width is 1+depth and the sm70 GEMV ladder serves 1/2/4/8 rows, rounding up, so depth 4 buys 8 rows to use 5 and measured 31.5 tok/s on coding, worse than not speculating; depth 3 is the deepest that fills rung 4, and `spec.LADDER_WIDTHS` plus an sm70 warning keeps `--depth`/`spec_depth` on the ladder. The 100% acceptance reported that morning was an artifact: `verify_lens` truncates each chain on the draft's own confidence before the counter runs and zero-keep ticks vanish from both counters, so accepted/drafted scored the truncation policy — the bench now reports tokens per trunk forward and no ratio. Long-context rows in that table are NOT decode measurements (a 4K prompt runs 8 chunked-prefill ticks with speculation off on every mixed tick). [errors/2026-09-01-spec-depth-is-a-staircase-not-a-line.md](docs/experience/errors/2026-09-01-spec-depth-is-a-staircase-not-a-line.md)
- **verdict** — speculation ACCEPTED on sm70: depth 3 vs dense B=1 32.7 → 52.7 tok/s at 31 ctx (1.61x), 94% of the era's 56.1 tok/s *dense* roofline and 32-42% of the ceiling a speculative rate is bound by (this line first read "82% of the 64 tok/s weight roofline" — two defects, a retired denominator and a dimensional one; a weight roofline bounds *forwards*, and 2.95 tok/forward puts the applicable ceiling at 126-166 tok/s, so the old figure overstated by 2.95x. [errors/2026-09-06-a-spec-rate-over-a-dense-roofline.md](docs/experience/errors/2026-09-06-a-spec-rate-over-a-dense-roofline.md)). Superseded in part by the entry above — the counting task's near-zero entropy makes it the best case, not a representative one, and the "100% acceptance / 1.29x at 1K" claimed here were both artifacts. Reverses that morning's rejection: the premise it failed on (a verify row costing more than a decoded token) was fixed by the packed-f16 GEMV. The "1.3 tok/s" that briefly said otherwise was a warmup artifact — `bench_b1_decode.py` warmed to `--lo`, which at depth 3 captures only the width-1 graph, so the other widths captured inside the timed lo point (2589/906/731 ms on ticks 1-3) and inverted the slope; warmup now runs to `--hi`, and per-tick timing was flat at ~78 ms out to 317 tokens throughout. [errors/2026-09-01-spec-warmup-one-width.md](docs/experience/errors/2026-09-01-spec-warmup-one-width.md)
- **phase exit** — packed-f16 X breaks the sm70 GEMV's per-row floor, dense B=1 25.8 → 32.7 tok/s at 31 ctx and 23.1 → 27.4 at 1K: the "127 us/row flat, issue-bound" ceiling was X, not the hardware — X is re-read by every block (0.71 GB = 78% of the M=8 time) and converted f32→f16 inside the tile loop (32 of ~49 per-row instructions), so packing it once at the dispatch site removes both. Per-row cost stops being flat: M=8 127.5 → 34.0 us/row at 17408x5120, M=1 128.1 → 88.2, bit-exact (relerr 0, same rounding done once), parity through the dispatch at M=1..8 worst 4.93e-04. A verify row now costs 10.7 ms against a 30.9 ms dense token (was 63 vs 40.9) — verification is cheaper than decoding for the first time on this card — and the f32 rungs have no caller left and are deleted. Speculation still OFF but the blocker moved: `--depth 3` serves 1.3 tok/s and `prof_spec_tick` puts 79% of the wall in `_draft_step` (371 ms/step vs 4.98 in isolation). [wins/2026-09-01-sm70-gemv-packed-x-f16.md](docs/experience/wins/2026-09-01-sm70-gemv-packed-x-f16.md)
- **phase exit** — sm70 verify replay W=2 271 → 78.4 ms (3.5x), W=4 271.7 → 146.8 (1.85x): `linear_fp4_gemv_sm70_m` had no weight reuse (127 us/ROW flat from M=1 to M=16) because it was issue-bound all along — 255 regs/thread, 12.2% occupancy, DRAM 6.8% at 42% SM, while the tile IS reused at 88% l1 hit. Dispatch now rounds M up a ladder (M=2/4/8) instead of padding everything to 8, which helps B=2..4 serving too; parity worst 1.40e-03 at the real projections. Speculation stays OFF: one verify row costs ~63 ms against a 40.9 ms dense tick, depth 1 = 19.4 tok/s vs dense 24.4. [wins/2026-09-01-sm70-gemv-m-ladder.md](docs/experience/wins/2026-09-01-sm70-gemv-m-ladder.md)
- **verdict** — the sm70 GEMV is occupancy-bound at 255 regs, not shape-broken; supersedes all three of 08-31's verdicts. Per-kernel profiling put 252 of the 271 ms verify tick in `linear_fp4_gemv_sm70_m` (507.5 µs/call vs the M=1 kernel's 64.5), GDN at 1.9-2.9 ms and attention 1.7 ms. ncu then withdrew the "reuse fails at 27B shapes" reading — duration tracks instructions tracks grid (3.3-3.6x) and per-block efficiency is identical at both shapes, so the 1.65x vs 7.5x ratio was a slow M=1 baseline at 4864, not a broken M=8. The real limiter is 255 registers/thread and 12.2% occupancy at every shape with DRAM at 6.8%: starved of parallelism, not bytes. [errors/2026-08-31-m8-gemv-occupancy-not-reuse.md](docs/experience/errors/2026-08-31-m8-gemv-occupancy-not-reuse.md)

## 2026-08-31

- **reject** — speculation on sm70, blocked on the GDN state path; corrects the earlier "draft outside the graph" verdict. Pure graph replay is 39 ms at W=1 but 266/267 ms at W=3/W=4 — flat in W, so W>1 is a path switch, not a scaling term: `backend.py:917` routes `q.shape[1] > 1` to `gdn_chunk_fused` and `gdn_decode` is sm90-only, so all 48 GDN layers take `state_gather` → kernel → `state_scatter`, 3 MiB out + 3 MiB back per layer of torch advanced indexing, 4.73 ms/layer. W=1 pays it too, which is why 39 ms is already 2.5x the 15.6 ms roofline. Shipped on the way: split-KV attention covers S>1 (9.5x at S=4/n=1024), and the draft head is fp4 on sm70 (4.98 ms/step vs 120.91 dense, 24x). - **reject** — MTP speculation for now, the draft step is outside the graph: the checkpoint's MTP head loads and is good (62% top-1 agreement with the trunk, 97-99% accept, 5.33 tokens committed per forward) but depth 6 measures 3.1 tok/s vs 25.8 dense. `prof_draft_step.py` prices a 1-layer 456M head at 120.91 ms/step eager, as much as the whole 64-layer trunk (103.58 eager / 39 captured) — both launch-bound at M=1, but the trunk hides it behind graph capture and `_draft_step` does not; capturing the draft step projects depth 6 at 62.7 tok/s. `serve --draft/--depth` and `Backend.has_kernel` land; speculation stays OFF. [errors/2026-08-31-draft-step-outside-graph.md](docs/experience/errors/2026-08-31-draft-step-outside-graph.md)
- **phase exit** — sm70 split-KV decode attention, 25.8 / 23.1 / 20.5 tok/s at 31 / 1K / 2K (was 20.3 / 8.7 / 5.3), falloff 3.8x → 20%: generic `paged_attention` grids over `(B, H)` — 24 blocks at B=1 on an 80-SM card — and its `T.serial(D)` dot means one active thread per block, measured 0.76 ns/scalar-FMA = 1.16 clocks, the single-thread rate. The position loop is split across the grid instead, so parallelism comes from the grid rather than a fragment reduction and the Metal constraint holds with `T.serial(D)` intact. sm70 cell only, dispatch arch-gated. [wins/2026-08-31-sm70-split-kv-decode-attention.md](docs/experience/wins/2026-08-31-sm70-split-kv-decode-attention.md)
- **phase exit** — B=1 long-context decode unblocked, 20.3 tok/s @ 31 ctx · 8.7 @ 1K · 5.3 @ 2K (256-token slope): `PrefixStore.capacity` counts ENTRIES, but each resident entry owns a 149.6 MiB GDN state snapshot in HBM, so the 4096 default is 576 GiB, eviction never fired, and B=1 at 1K ctx OOM'd after 18 publishes. The cap is now derived from `mem_get_info` at build time on CUDA (resolves to 3 on the V100). [errors/2026-08-31-prefix-snapshot-oom.md](docs/experience/errors/2026-08-31-prefix-snapshot-oom.md)
- **phase exit** — server B=8 2.8 → 12.9 tok/s: the daemon ran `step()` between HTTP arrivals, splitting a concurrent burst into eager mixed ticks with the decode graph off; a 10 ms batching window when idle + waiting turns 17 prefills + 6 mixed ticks into 1 prefill for 8 requests. Also `chat_template_kwargs.enable_thinking: false` now works (was silently dropped) and `num_blocks=256 → 512` for 8K context. [wins/2026-08-31-server-prefill-batching.md](docs/experience/wins/2026-08-31-server-prefill-batching.md)
- **phase exit** — sm70 prefill 64.9s → 15.1s: `gdn_chunk_fused` was only in `_SM90_KERNELS`, so sm70 fell back to `reference.gdn_forward` (a Python serial scan, ~250k eager ops for 8×64 tokens, 62s of the 64s tick 1) — one-line registry fix, the kernel is target-neutral TileLang and runs on V100 unchanged. Also shipped: `linear_fp4_gemv_sm70_m32` (M=32 prefill chunking, 32x fewer launches than the per-row M=1 loop; parity PASS M=1/8/16/32, worst 6.17e-4) and `build_engine(kv_tier_path=...)` wiring the SSD KvTier, which was `tier=None`. Decode unchanged at 260 ms/tick, 99.1% GPU / 0.9% Python — the decode tick is not Python-bound, the prefill was. [wins/2026-08-31-sm70-gdn-chunk-fused.md](docs/experience/wins/2026-08-31-sm70-gdn-chunk-fused.md)
- **phase exit** — B=8 decode on sm70, 1.3 → 31.8 tok/s aggregate at 263.8 ms/tick: the M-row fp4 GEMV (`linear_fp4_gemv_sm70_m`) loads+decodes W once per tile and reuses it across M=8 rows, replacing the per-row M=1 loop that OOMs at B=8, so weight bytes stop scaling with batch. The first B=8 e2e read 5.67 s/tick (1.3 t/s, 9% GPU) because the pod's `engine.py` was an old copy admitting one request/tick via `if` rather than `while`, producing mixed prefill+decode ticks that skip the decode-graph path. B=8 per-token cost 33.0 ms vs B=1's 37.9 ms — weight sharing makes B=8 cheaper per token than B=1. Parity M=1 and M=8 on 12 real projections, worst 6.17e-4 against a 1e-2 gate. It was a 6.5x loss first (dense bf16 head at ~30 GB/s; a duplicated `materialize` in `build_engine` masked three real fixes), and the head emitted anti-correlated logits until `load_draft` applied the zero-centered RMSNorm +1 fold (agreement 0.0% → 84.4%). [wins/2026-08-29-spec-decode-net-win.md](docs/experience/wins/2026-08-29-spec-decode-net-win.md), [errors/2026-08-29-draft-head-missing-zero-centered-fold.md](docs/experience/errors/2026-08-29-draft-head-missing-zero-centered-fold.md)
- **phase exit** — prefill 1836 → 2109.7 tok/s (1.15x): KV descriptors crossed to the device 971 times per prefill synchronously (+9%), lm_head ran over all 512 chunk positions to use one (+3.1%); MMLU held at 81.0%. Rejected on measurement: speculation (0.43–0.76x of graph decode), chunked GDN prefill (7.1x slower, third attempt), prefill graph capture (~1%). Four claims retired as measurement errors (spec "1.14x" vs eager; "wins at B=8" across prompts; sglang prefill gap 2.03x not 2.5x; "WY never measured"); the harness now measures its own no-draft arm, takes three prefill readings, requires spread under the raise margin, and `--suite accuracy` gates MMLU. GDN prefill is SM-limited (2x blocks = 1.67x time; V split worth 1.20x kernel, not implemented). `tilerl train --opd` runs on CPU (`add_lora` attached to nothing on a dense base). [wins/2026-08-29-pinned-kv-descriptors.md](docs/experience/wins/2026-08-29-pinned-kv-descriptors.md), [wins/2026-08-29-prefill-lm-head-last-token.md](docs/experience/wins/2026-08-29-prefill-lm-head-last-token.md), [errors/2026-08-29-gdn-48-blocks-78-sms.md](docs/experience/errors/2026-08-29-gdn-48-blocks-78-sms.md)
- **default flip** — the harness gates accuracy, not only speed: `--suite accuracy` seeds at 81.0% (162/200, 0-shot MMLU, fixed greedy slice), scoring shared with `mmlu.py`; baseline raises require spread under the margin. 27B LoRA rows pass after the backward-tile revert (26.1 / 32.5 / 37.6 tok/s). The engine's multi-token verify path is correct (matches T=1 greedy at chunk widths 2/5/9); chunkwise-WY was wrongly ruled out on 2026-08-24 by argument (`wy_fast` carries the intra-chunk term) — a 1.36x prefill win closed for four days.
